# Bill text — S8 Earned-Service Repricing (military service purchase), §§ 51.1-142.2 and 51.1-162 amended and new § 51.1-142.4

**MOO-42 · Drafting Specialist · store revision 72 · 13 October 2026 · INTERNAL WORKING DRAFT — pre-decisional**

**Byline correction.** This line read *"store revision 48"* through store revision 49, which did not update it. The label is corrected here and the §0 narrative for store revision 49 is immediately below this revision's.

> **Store revision 37** answers [MOO-104](/MOO/issues/MOO-104)'s counsel review of the G 4
> death-calculation clause drafted but never applied. **That clause is not in this revision.** The
> review rated it **fatal as a cure**: it confined the deeming *"for the purposes of this section"* while
> sending the payment duty out to *"this chapter"*, and the chapter routes the compelled-notice death case
> to § 51.1-163 and a refund of the member's own contributions. **Two minimal cures are enacted instead**,
> both inside subdivision G 4 and together **162 characters**: § 51.1-153's requirements a member cannot
> satisfy **by reason of his death** are disapplied for the existing *"as though"* hypothetical, and
> *"Notwithstanding the preceding sentence"* is re-anchored to the crediting it was meant to displace.
> **No figure moves**, no definition is added, no section joins the act, and the title does not change.
> **The death case itself is §7 item 60 and it is yours** — with a mechanism the review did not identify:
> the *"certified subsidy"* **floor**, which settles the subsidy at standard cost less price in a death
> case whatever the credit produced.

> counsel review of store revision 33's 24 edits, **19 findings on the enacted text, 0 fatal, plus 3
> serious defects in candidate texts that are not in the bill** — in **eleven edits in ten provisions**,
> and it moves no figure: `12`, `$3,000,000`, `$170,000` and `[20]` percent are untouched and the bill
> title does not change. **One definition paragraph is deleted** — B 4 g, which I created last revision
> and which 26 C.F.R. § 1.415(j)-1 shows cannot do what I said it did. **The retirement window is bounded
> for the third time and §6.0-24's assurance is withdrawn for the second**; it is not restated, and the
> envelope is stated once at **§6.0-36 item 1**. **The involuntarily separated member's 90 days is now 90
> days.** **Four questions are the Director's at §7 items 55 to 58**, and §7 **item 39 is repaired**.
> **Start at §0.x** for the record, **§8** for the status, and
> [`pass11-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-pass11-disposition) for the full
> finding-by-finding disposition.

> **Store revision 33** drafts out [MOO-95](/MOO/issues/MOO-95)'s **pass 9** — the adversarial and counsel
> review of revision 24's 37 bill-text edits, **29 findings, 0 fatal** — in **24 edits**, and it moves no
> figure: `12`, `$3,000,000`, `$170,000` and `[20]` percent are untouched, no section is added and the bill
> title does not change. **Start at §0.x** for the finding-by-finding record and at **§8** for the status.
> **Four findings are the Director's and are drafted in §7 as items 39 to 42**, and **my own recommendation
> on item 33 is withdrawn** — the case-decision sentence would have imported § 2.2-4021 B's deemed-decision
> default against the Board. One assurance I gave the Director in §6.0-24 was false and is corrected there.

> **Store revision 23 changes no bill text.** The bill is exactly store revision 22's — no word of
> either section, no enactment clause, no title change, and no figure. It folds
> [MOO-81](/MOO/issues/MOO-81)'s pricing of the completion-date dial into **§7 item 22**, and that
> pricing **reverses the framing revision 18 gave the item and withdraws my own recommended sentence**:
> the dial is a *count* dial, not a price dial; the two measures do **not** move in opposite
> directions, and the 12-month statutory maximum is the worse end on **both** (12.14 accepted and 11.81
> veterans reached, against 18.91 and 16.66 if the quantity were measured at the application date); and
> the payment-method standard I recommended binds nothing at all for a payroll-deduction payer, so it
> would make the size of a veteran's benefit turn on whether she can write a cheque. **New option (d),
> recommended and not inserted:** sever the measurement date from the payment deadline and fix it by
> statute, which also closes pass 5's § 415-deferral finding structurally rather than by patch. Store
> revision 23 also corrects the participant count in §8 to **18.9 to 19.6**, and it moves §0's
> narrative for revisions 2–12 out to
> [`bill-text-s8-revision-history`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-revision-history), which
> store revision 22 said was now the alternative to a companion document per revision: this file had
> **87 characters** of headroom against the 524,288-character ceiling and now has about 78,000.
> The §0 record for that revision.

> **Revision 21 drafts the Policy Director's four answers of 12 October 2026.** Eleven changed places
> in the bill text. **The retirement window is 12 months and no longer bracketed**; pass 4's **F4** —
> the last finding anyone had called fatal — is **drafted out**, both limbs, plus one reconciling
> sentence in subsection F that §6.0-21 item 2 records as mine rather than the Director's; the **APA
> Article 2 exemption is deleted entirely** and the deletion is verified operative against § 2.2-4002
> as published; and the **§ 51.1-155 A 2 conforming amendment is dropped**, so the act amends **one**

> `[20]` percent contingency are untouched, and `[20]` percent is now the only bracket in the text.

> authorised set was priced at the 0-month rung** and the **$170,000 ceiling was walked off that
> distribution** ([MOO-85](/MOO/issues/MOO-85)); and the Article 2 answer collides with M 1's
> 1 July 2029 deadline in a way my question card did not put in front of you (**§7 item 27**, new,
> three cures drafted and none enacted). The §0 record for that revision.

> **Revision 20 drafts the four residuals [MOO-79](/MOO/issues/MOO-79) found by checking revision 18
> ([MOO-83](/MOO/issues/MOO-83)).** Four edits to the bill text, all inside § 51.1-142.4, **none with
> policy content and no figure moved.** **R3 is the one that matters**: revision 18's notice package was
> keyed to the single trigger *"an applicant is not an eligible member"*, and a member refused under the
> new zero bar **is** an eligible member — she satisfied all seven clauses and was refused because clause
> (ii) of subsection C, measured at the completion date, came to zero. She was refused silently, with no
> reasons, no statement of her surviving right to buy at standard cost, and no review. Subsection C now
> carries the notice-reasons-rights-review sentence. **R1** letters the limbs of *"maximum purchasable
> amount"* (a)/(b)/(c), which removes three different *"clause (ii)"*s from four lines of the provision
> that decides who is admitted; **R2** replaces *"permitted by"* with *"specified in"*, so the definition
> no longer routes through a chapeau conditioned on the eligibility the definition exists to determine;
> and **R4** re-keys the three standard-cost notices in D 3, D 4 and D 5 off *"the service credit the
> applicant had applied to purchase"* — a quantity no provision now requires the application to state —
> and onto the applicant's own maximum purchasable amount, which the Board determines from its own
> records. **R2 and R4 turned out to be the same fix twice**, and that is the one thing in this revision
> nobody predicted: R4 makes the defined term operate on applicants the Board has just determined are
> **not** eligible members, which is exactly the chapeau problem R2 removes. Done in the wrong order, R4
> alone would have been a defect. The §0 record for that revision.

> **Store revision 19 changed no bill text.** It was two commentary pointers
> ([MOO-80](/MOO/issues/MOO-80) and [MOO-81](/MOO/issues/MOO-81)) and is not given a §0.x section.
> Revision 20 is rebased onto it and reverts none of it.

> **Revision 18 drafts out two adversarial passes: 24 findings from pass 5 ([MOO-76](/MOO/issues/MOO-76))
> and 14 from the S6 review of the new clause (vi) ([MOO-77](/MOO/issues/MOO-77)).** 29 edits to the bill
> text and 8 corrections to this document's own commentary. **33 of the 38 findings are drafted out; one is
> moot; three are notes with no text change; one is routed to the Director as §7 item 22, and it was then the
> largest unpriced dial left in the bill (**priced in store revision 23** on
> [MOO-81](/MOO/issues/MOO-81)).** The three changes that matter most: clause (vi) of "eligible
> member" is re-keyed to a new defined term, **"maximum purchasable amount"**, so admission turns on member
> facts rather than on the number the applicant writes on the form — which is also the quantity the priced
> run tests; **the Board must now determine eligibility, state the date it determines it as of, give
> reasons, state the refused applicant's standard-cost rights, and allow review**, none of which the section
> required; and the **zero-month election is barred**, which closes a path on which a member bought nothing,
> paid nothing, retired on a date she was already entitled to, and the Commonwealth paid a subsidy on her
> account. The §0 record for that revision.

> **Revision 17 bounds the new clause (vi) by what the member can actually buy.** One sentence added
> to "eligible member"; nothing else in the bill moves and no figure changes. It answers revision
> 16's **own** reviewer question 2 in the affirmative: clause (vi) and clause (vii) **could** select
> different members, because clause (vi) ran on an unbounded applied-for amount while clause (vii)
> runs on an amount capped by the member's real entitlement and by clauses (i) and (iii) of
> subsection C. A member 36 months short with 12 months of qualifying service could be admitted, pay,
> and fail at crediting. The same sentence secures revision 16's subsection C (ii) cure, which the
> implied-limit argument would otherwise undo. The §0 record for that revision.

**On the revision number.** From this revision the number is the **document-store revision number**
and nothing else. The narrative sequence that ran to "revision 12" is retired; it had drifted three
behind the store, which is why the Director's *"revision 13"* and a header reading *"revision 11"*
were the same text. The §0 record for that revision maps the two sequences. The §0.x section headings keep their
narrative labels as retained record.

> **Revision 16 drafts the application-date leg of "eligible member" and answers which
> certified-subsidy measure D 2 refuses on.** [MOO-75](/MOO/issues/MOO-75). One sentence of bill text
> changes: clause (vi) is new, the old clause (vi) becomes (vii), and nothing else in the bill moves.
> The Director's direction of 11 October 22:02 **had been dropped — not deferred, not objected to**;
> The §0 record for that revision says so plainly and names the process fix. The "certified subsidy" definition in
> subsection A yields the **corrected** measure (`CS_corrected`), mapped clause by clause, so the
> $170,000 ceiling is as intrusive as the figure was chosen to be and **neither the definition nor
> the figure changes.** The §0 record for that revision is the one real interaction this found: on the Director's
> literal wording the leg would have collided with subsection C (ii) and emptied the pilot, and the
> cure is inside the leg. Revision 16 is **rebased onto store revision 15** and reverts none of it.
>
> **Revision 12 drafts the five live pass-4 findings that were mine, including one of the two fatal
> ones.** **F3** — the § 51.1-153 notice that could not be filed — is **closed, and closed without
> choosing the window numeral**: the notice is now due on the later of 30 days before the chosen date
> and 30 days after the Board states the window, and § 51.1-153 E's 90-day retroactivity allowance
> does the rest. **S1** (the money flow did not close on death, disability or Board error), **S12**
> (the processing extension had no member right and no standard), **M6**'s D 3 limb (the Department of
> Planning and Budget's 30 days with no default) and **M7** (a lawful § 415 bar stranded the member)
> are drafted out. Drafting F3 turned up **one defect nobody had found** — § 51.1-153 E also requires
> the effective date of retirement to fall *after* the member's last day of service, which at a short
> window asks the member to stop working before they know the crediting date — and subsection E now
> lets the member fix the crediting date. The §0 record for that revision is the change list. **Four live findings
> remain: S7 is mine and needs a decision; F4, S5 and S11 are the Director's.**
>
> **Revision 11 folds the first eight of pass 4's findings.** [MOO-51](/MOO/issues/MOO-51)'s
> adversarial review landed — **4 fatal, 12 serious, 7 moderate, 3 minor** against revision 4.
> **Eight were already fixed by revisions 5 to 10, one is moot, eight are drafted out here, and
> nine are live** — four of those being the Director's. The §0 record for that revision is the finding-by-finding
> triage, and it is the first thing a reviewer of this draft should read.
>
> **Revisions 9 and 10 enact all four board answers of 11 October 2026.** Revision 10 deletes
> subsection J, renumbers K through O up one letter, chases every cross-reference and trims the bill
> title; and it drafts G 4's middle treatment. The §0 record for that revision is its change list.
>
> **Revision 9 enacts two board decisions and makes three staleness corrections.** D 2's per-member
> ceiling is **$170,000**, unbracketed, on the Policy Director's direction of 11 October 2026; the
> obligor bracket is resolved to **Alternative A** (the Commonwealth pays), which the Director answered
> on the same day. The §0 record for that revision is the change list. The header read "revision 7" through revision 8,
> which changed no bill text; that was a stale header and it is corrected here.

## 0. Record of revisions — the retained §0, §5 and §6 record is moved out of this document

### 0.0-72 Store revision 72 — record only. [MOO-211](/MOO/issues/MOO-211)'s counsel pass on store revision 70, disposed into the record; six cures priced and none enacted; five figures of counsel's own corrected, and five of this record's

**No word of any section in the act changes in this revision.** §1 is byte-for-byte what store revision 71 left. What changes is the record: [MOO-211](/MOO/issues/MOO-211)'s counsel pass — **5 serious, 5 moderate, 3 minor, 18 null results** — is folded in, the three findings [MOO-208](/MOO/issues/MOO-208) asked it to test are resolved, and **five demonstrated errors of fact in this record are corrected in place**: a citation that does not hold, two character counts, one conforming-amendment column, and one claim about what a character delta proves. Record patches R1 to R8. Following the convention this file sets for store revisions 64 and 68, a record-only revision writes a §0 narrative and **no §5 sweep and no §6 note** — there is no cross-reference to chase when no citation moves.

**The three findings MOO-208 sent to be tested all stand, and the first one stands with a second reason this record did not have.** Counsel agreed that **F3's exception is in the wrong provision** and recommended form M-a. The reason this record gave — that D 1's sentence is conferring, so an exception to it confers less, and M 2 does not look at D 1 — is sound, and counsel added the point that settles it: **D 1 expressly disapplies M 2's second sentence, and a provision that does not apply of its own force cannot be disapplied.** The drafter's own words therefore foreclose the contrary reading. Counsel also found a **second route this record missed, and it inverts the subdivision**: because D3c carved the advice failure out of the whole sentence, it carved it out of the disapplication too, so for that one failure M 2's second sentence applies in full and the certification is itself a reviewable determination. **The member whose certification omits the least legally substantive of the seven items now has the widest channel of any member in the subdivision** — the opposite of F3's purpose, and a second reason the clause has to move to M 2. §7 item 135 carries both routes.

**All six cures were re-resolved on store revision 71, not on the revision counsel read.** Counsel verified on store revision 70; store revision 71 landed first and moved §1 by **+1,455**, to 134,012. Every one of the six FIND strings still resolves **exactly 1× inside the §1 slice**, and **all nineteen counts counsel asserts about §1 re-count exactly** at store revision 71 — the tag counts, the conformed phrase at 3×, the *"for the purposes of subsection M"* family at 6× and 5×, M 2's three clauses, M 1's *"content of a complete application"*, and the wider *"Nothing in this section"* formula at 3×. Nothing in the counsel pass was spent by the intervening revision. Kit and script at `moo211-cures/`.

**The markup, which counsel got wrong on five of the six and which is the one thing in the pass that would have damaged the text.** Counsel wrote `<u></u>` tags into five cures. **Three of those insertion points — S1, S3, S5 — sit inside an open underscored span**, because subsection M 2 and subdivision D 1 are wholly new language, so counsel's tags would have **nested**, taking §1 from 62/62 to 63/63. **The other two — S2 and M3 — sit in enactment clause 3, which carries zero `<u>` tags in 6,176 characters**, the enactment clauses not being amendatory text and not being redlined, exactly as the title line is not. **So no cure carries a tag, and all six leave `<u>`/`</u>` at 62 / 62** — which makes the standing post-condition on the tag counts true of every one of the six, whichever the Director takes. The corrected figures: **S1 +115, S2 +118, S3 +127, S5 +310, M3 +95, M4 +42.** Counsel's S2 figure is right for the wrong reason, being the figure net of tags counsel also wrote; **M3's is off by one on counsel's own words**, and the other three were offered in both forms.

**Five corrections to this record, each a demonstrated error of fact and none of them a change of position.**

1. **R2 and R3 — the title authority does not hold.** §5.47 direction 1 row 7 and §6.0-70's T2 item both cite *Dean v. Paolicelli*, 194 Va. 219 (1952) for the sufficiency of a title line. **That case has no title holding**; it decides eligibility for state office under art. II, § 32 and special legislation under the 1902 Constitution's art. IV, § 64. The authority that does hold is ***City of Richmond v. Pace*, 127 Va. 274, 103 S.E. 647 (1920)** — a title is sufficient if it gives notice of the general subject and of the interests likely to be affected, and unnamed subjects are fine if germane or cognate. **The proposition this record asserted is correct Virginia law; only the case was wrong.** The consequence runs the other way from the one the record implied: under *Pace*, **T2 was never constitutionally required** — the death-benefit provisions were already germane to the stated object — so T2 is a better title and was not a save. *Dean* stays in the file against the eligibility classifications in subsections B and C, where it is good authority.
2. **R1 and R6 — row 3's conforming-amendment column does not hold.** The column says of *"may make or change that designation … at any time before the member's death"* that no conforming amendment is needed because *"the words are tied to A, so the statement follows A if A is amended."* **The citation carries the source of the power; it does not carry the timing.** *"At any time before the member's death"* is neither A's language nor A's content — it is this drafter's inference, as §6.0-70 item 2 says in terms — so if A were amended to fix a moment of irrevocability, D 1 would still compel the old proposition. The *"Verified"* column is sound; the conforming column was not. **S5's saving clause cures it, § 51.1-162 being in S5's list.**
3. **R4 — the re-anchored [MOO-176](/MOO/issues/MOO-176) Edit 1 string is 151 characters, not 150.** Everything else in check 9 is exact, and the string still resolves 1× in §1 and 2× in the document at store revision 71.
4. **R5 — a matching character delta is a checksum, and this record called it a proof.** Check 10 said §1 moved by exactly the 634 characters the six substitutions account for, *"which is the arithmetic proof that nothing else in §1 changed."* **It is not a proof.** Two offsetting changes of equal magnitude pass it, and so does any change of exactly zero characters — and **§6.0-70 item 4 records that store revision 70 contains one**, F4's relocation of the series' *"and"*. Counsel ran the proof this record should have run: §1 diffed against the fetched base, **exactly two lines differing**, line 4 the title line at +44 and line 115 subdivision D 1 at +590. The conclusion was right and the method was not. **The standing rule from this revision is a line diff against the fetched base, not a delta assertion**, and the next kit to write §1 carries it.
5. **R7 and R8 — the F3 residue does not exist, because this record counted the propositions wrong.** The Director's instruction to §7 item 133 was that the misstatement limb reach *"the six propositions of law and not the advisory."* After F4 and F1/F2/F6/F9, **item 7 carries one advisory and two propositions of law** — the power to re-designate and the no-fault clause — so **the certification series carries eight propositions of law and one advisory, not six and one.** The drafted scope, *"every matter other than the advice"*, therefore reaches all eight and excepts the one, which is exactly the Director's intent. **The asymmetry §6.0-70 item 6 recorded was an artefact of counting the propositions as six; there is no residue to cure, no three-way split to consider, and the (i)–(vii) renumbering this commission forbade is not needed for anything.** Item 133's words are left alone and its description is corrected.

**Counsel's own most serious finding is not about this revision at all, and it is not drafted.** **S2: enactment clause 3 clause (v) continues subsection M past the 1 July 2033 expiry for a determination, for a failure to determine and for a failure to give notice — and not for a failure to state a matter.** That is the limb F3 and S1 are about, and it is the limb whose harm surfaces last, because it surfaces on the member's death. Clause (i) continues members' rights *"as though those provisions had not expired"* and clause (iii) carries the subsection J reports out to a possible December 2053, so the long tail was plainly contemplated and the wrong limb of M was carried across it. **In its sharper form the omission is in the subdivision G 4 notice itself**, in which case after 1 July 2033 the content of the one document a survivor ever receives is unreviewable. **A decision for the Director, drafted at +118 and enacted nowhere.**

**And one measurement, re-taken, that moves the wrong way.** Counsel's S4 — that nothing requires the certification to be readable, and that the cheapest compliant path is to staple D 1 to the back of it — rests on the length of the *"shall also state"* sentence, which counsel measured at store revision 70 as **3,447 characters and 576 words, one sentence, seven undesignated items**. At store revision 71 it is **3,680 characters and 612 words**: **+233 and +36 in one revision.** S4 is a cost of holding F5, which the Director has decided and which this record does not reopen; the measurement is recorded because it is getting worse and because it is the reason the four findings the composite took — an actor, an object, a terminus and a no-fault clause — may buy nothing a member ever reads.

**Nothing in this revision is enacted and nothing is agreed. All six cures are the Director's decision**, priced and anchored at §7 item 135 and in the deliverable at [MOO-208](/MOO/issues/MOO-208). **Internal working draft. Not a Division of Legislative Services bill, not cleared by counsel or by DLS, not legally sufficient for introduction. Pre-decisional and internal.**

| 71-P4/P5/P6 | MOO-200 **F16** | the remaining twelve float-rendered §0 pointers | — |

**Measured, not predicted.** §1 **132,557 → 134,012 (+1,455)**; **eight** lines of §1 differ; §1 `<u>`/`</u>` **62 / 62 before and after**. Counted mechanically over §1: bare `§ 51.1-162` **19 → 20**, `§ 51.1-165` **17 → 18**, `§ 51.1-124.3` **2 → 3**, `accumulated contributions` **9 → 10**, `the abolished system` **1 → 2**, `died in service prior to retirement` **0 → 1**, `post-retirement employment` **1 → 0**, `is obtained` **3 → 2**, `except as subdivision G 4 of § 51.1-142.4 provides` **1 → 0**, `qualifying retirement` **74 → 74**, `§ 51.1-153` **8 → 8**, `§ 51.1-163` **4 → 4**, `§ 51.1-165.01` **7 → 7**.

**The one fatal finding is not in this revision, and that is the whole of the reason this revision is not larger.** MOO-210's **R1** holds that *"the effective date of which the Board determines"* has no referent in Chapter 1 — §§ 51.1-153 A/B/E and 51.1-156 A/B/D put the effective date in the member's own written notification, and §1 gives the *stating* of it to the member three times and calls it *"the member's actual effective date of retirement"* — so on the whole-act reading the exception the Director approved as **f1-b** never fires, and [MOO-189](/MOO/issues/MOO-189)'s fatal F1 is uncured. **Every cure is a fourth form of the condition on that sentence, and the Director's standing direction of 12 October 2026 forecloses two of the three families.** Per standing direction **A1** and per the terms on which MOO-210 was commissioned, a defect in that sentence needing a new form of the condition is a card for the Policy Director and not a drafting fix. It is **§7 item 136**.

**71-A is enacted even though R1 may delete it, and the reason is on the record.** R2 is the windfall half of the same trigger: on the administrative reading that R1 must assume for f1-b to work at all, nothing confined *"determines"* to the first determination, so a post-death correction of a long-retired disability retiree's effective date fired the death-in-service branch and paid her survivor a § 51.1-162 B allowance with **no offset for the $165,240 she drew in life** — because § 51.1-162 B's reduction attaches to the refund and never to the allowance. That is **about $250,000 an occurrence, 18% of the programme's central aggregate**. Bounding it takes nothing from any class the agreed policy means to pay. **If the Director's answer to R1 replaces the trigger, 71-A's 89 characters come out with it**; that is said here so it is not discovered later.

**R2's fix direction offered *"first determines … otherwise than by way of correction or redetermination"* and the enacted words drop *"first"* as inert.** A determination made before the death is already outside *"after the date of the member's death"*; a later correction is already outside the new limb. *"First"* adds nothing and a word that adds nothing to an operative trigger is a word a court must find work for.

**71-B names § 51.1-124.3, which this act does not amend.** The deeming operates *for the purposes of* that definition and displaces nothing in it, but it is the act's third operative reach into a section outside its own footprint, and **it must travel in the drafting request to Division of Legislative Services with the two *"notwithstanding"* displacements and f7-toll's override of § 51.1-162 C — a drafter's note is not enacted.** Standing direction **A6** is extended to say so.

**71-E widens nothing and closes a permanent defect.** The amendment to § 51.1-162 C is in the first enactment and is permanent; the section it cites expires on 1 July 2033. From that date the Code would have carried, in a death-benefit section of general application, an exception keyed to a subdivision of a section that no longer exists. Clause (vi) is drafted in the form the act already uses at clause **(iv)** for the undesignated paragraph of § 51.1-142.2 subsection B, including its *"except as clause (i) of this enactment provides"* carry-forward, so the act reverts § 51.1-162 C by the same device it already reverts something else — **no new mechanism, and no republication of § 51.1-162 at length is required for a reversion the act itself effects, on the act's own precedent.**

### 0.0-70 Store revision 70 — the Policy Director's composite of [MOO-191](/MOO/issues/MOO-191) enacted, commissioned at [MOO-208](/MOO/issues/MOO-208): the designation-review advisory given an actor, an object, a power and a no-fault clause, moved to the end of the certification series, excepted from subdivision D 1's subsection M deeming and kept in the subsection J report; and the bill's title line cured

**Six substitutions, one revision.** Takes **F1** (the object), **F2** in form D2b (the terminus, referent named), **F3** in form D3c conformed (the channel), **F4** (placement), **F6** (the no-fault clause), **F9** (the actor), and **F8**'s title cure **T2**, the Director having expressly extended the commission to the title line. **F5 is held and the acknowledgment form is not drafted. F7 is not cut.** Every word enacted here is the Director's approved word; nothing is added, and the three defects found on the way are recorded at §5.47 and §6.0-70 rather than drafted around.

| # | Finding | What it does | FIND | Δ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | F4 | Lift the advisory out of item 5 | 208 chars, 1× | **−137** |
| 2 | F9 + F1 + F2 + F6 | Set it down as item 7 of 7 | 47 chars, 1× | **+502** |
| 3 | F4 | Move the series' *"and"* to the new last item | 38 chars, 1× | **−4** |
| 4 | F3 | Except the advice from D 1's subsection M deeming | 131 chars, 1× | **+116** |
| 5 | F3 | Keep it in the subsection J report | 84 chars, 1× | **+113** |
| 6 | T2 | *"death benefits; designation of beneficiary"* into the title's *"relating to"* clause | 51 chars, 1× | **+44** |

**Why one revision and not six cuts.** D1, D2b, D6 and D9 all substitute on one FIND string, so they travel together — and **D3c travels with them, which the disposition did not carry.** D3c writes D9's 61-character FIND into the subdivision twice more, so D9's anchor goes from 1× to 3×. In the other order D3c's exception would have described *"a failure to state that the member is advised to review…"* while the subdivision required *"that the Board advises the member to review…"*, and **F3 would have been enacted and inert in both limbs with nothing failing.** The apply kit demonstrates the hazard before it applies anything.

**Re-resolved on the base as it stands, which is not the base the disposition was counted on.** The disposition was built on store revision 68 and required §1 at 131,656 and the document at 449,830. **Store revision 69 — [MOO-189](/MOO/issues/MOO-189)'s four answers — landed first**, so the base here is document revision `3b9ac270`, number 88, store revision **69**: document **482,941**, §1 **131,923**, §1 `<u>`/`</u>` **62 / 62**, the series at **7 items** with the advisory at item **5 of 7**. The Director's absolute figures are therefore spent and the **deltas** carry over. **All six FIND strings resolved exactly once inside the §1 slice at the moment each was applied**, each counted after its predecessor and not against the base.

**Measured: §1 131,923 → 132,557 (+634); the document 482,941 → 483,575 (+634) before the record layer.** The Director's post-condition gave §1 at +590 on the footing that substitution 6 landed *"outside §1"*. **It does not: the bill's title line sits under `### Title`, which is inside §1 as §1 is bounded in this document.** The apply kit asserts the corrected delta. §6.0-70 item 9.

**Every other post-condition met and re-checked on the returned body.** §1 `<u>`/`</u>` **62 / 62, unchanged** — the whole of subdivision D 1 already sits inside one `<u>` span, so new language joins it and no markup is added. Series **7 items, advisory at item 7 of 7**; **six semicolons, unchanged**; the series' period count unchanged, so no sentence boundary moved; **zero occurrences of *"the member is advised to"* left in §1**; the conformed phrase *"that the Board advises the member to review the member's designation of beneficiary"* appears **exactly 3× in §1** — the item, the M exception, the J limb. No figure moves, no designator is renumbered, no section joins or leaves the act, no defined term is added.

**Three defects found and recorded rather than cured.**

1. **Serious — F3 does not close the channel it was taken to close, because subdivision M 2 does not look at subdivision D 1.** M 2's first sentence reaches *"a failure of the Board to state in a certification or notice a matter this section requires to be stated in it"* on its own terms, and its clock for a failure to state expressly contemplates another provision giving *"a longer or later period"* — so D 1's sentence **extends** subsection M rather than constituting it, and the exception withdraws only the extension. **The member keeps reconsideration under M 2 on a 30-day clock from the certification; a claimant who is not the member loses the subdivision G 4-notice clock, the only one a survivor could ever have used.** Substitution 5, the J limb, is unaffected and works in full. **No cure inside D 1 can work; the clause has to go in M 2, which this commission did not open, so it is drafted at §7 item 135 and not enacted.** §5.47 direction 2 check 1; §6.0-70 item 5.
2. **Moderate — the F3 residue the Director named is now wider than the Director's statement of it.** D3c's exception names only the first of item 7's three propositions. **What follows, and the Director's words do not reach, is the asymmetry with F7**: §7 item 133's misstatement limb is re-scoped to the six propositions of law as directed, so if it were ever enacted, **misstating** the re-designation or no-fault clauses would be unreached while **omitting** them stays reviewable. Strict scoping is not draftable without designating the series (i) to (vii), a renumbering this commission forbids. §6.0-70 item 6.
3. **Moderate, and pre-existing — [MOO-176](/MOO/issues/MOO-176) revision 6's Edit 1 anchor is dead, and this revision did not cause it.** The live provision reads *"…from the giving of that statement**, notice, or certification** is extended by three days.</u>"*, widened after that revision was written. **0× on the base and 0× after the composite; Edits 2 and 3 are 1× before and 1× after.** The re-anchored string is at §5.47 direction 2 check 9. **Nothing of MOO-176 revision 6 is touched here**, as directed.

**Eight in-place record corrections, each marked and none rewritten away.** Six store revision 65 drafter's-note items are marked where this composite supersedes or reverses them: **item 1** (superseded as to the words by F9), **item 2** (placement reversed by F4, both of its reasons preserved and better served), **item 3** (the citation reason restated for the new position), **item 5** (the terminus kept, the implicature cured), **item 6** (F3 now enacted, so its heading is no longer the law of the draft, with the M 2 result named), **item 7** (F5 held, the acknowledgment form not to be drafted). The §5 and §6 self-describing paragraphs are brought current, and **store revision 69's cross-reference to its own sweep, written as *"§5.@46@"*, is corrected — the same corruption store revision 67 made at *"§5.@45@"*, and twice is a pattern rather than a slip.**

**The retention rotation §0.1 sets had to be performed, because this revision could not otherwise write.** With its record layer the document landed at **527,893** characters against the store's **524,288** ceiling. The §5 paragraph has named §5.38 and §5.39 as the next to move since store revision 58, and **store revision 70 is the revision that was short of room**: both sweeps are moved **verbatim** to [`bill-text-s8-record-s0-s5-s6-rev2-51`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-record-s0-s5-s6-rev2-51), each leaving a stub in the form §5.37 established, and a reference of the form **§5.38** or **§5.39** still resolves. **Nothing is edited and no enacted word is touched by the move.** Only the §5 half of the rotation was performed; **§6.0-59 and §6.0-60 remain and are the next to go.** The document closes at about **9,000** characters of headroom. Flagged for the Director.

**Scope held, and verified held.** **Subsection E1 is not re-cut** — accepted at [MOO-188](/MOO/issues/MOO-188) §2 and under the counsel gate at [MOO-192](/MOO/issues/MOO-192). **The [MOO-141](/MOO/issues/MOO-141) patch is not applied.** **[MOO-97](/MOO/issues/MOO-97) §7 item 129's other answers stay open**, and no other §7 item is answered or closed. **F7 is not cut**; §7 item 133 stays open and its drafted words are re-scoped, as directed. **D5 is not applied and no acknowledgment form exists anywhere in this draft.**

**Internal working draft. Not a Division of Legislative Services bill, not cleared by counsel or by DLS, not legally sufficient for introduction. Pre-decisional and internal.**

### 0.0-69 Store revision 69 — the Policy Director's four answers of 12 October 2026 enacted out of [MOO-189](/MOO/issues/MOO-189)'s counsel pass: the FATAL F1 cured, the greater-of confined to coincident payees, the eligibility bar moved out of the local-mandate disclaimer, and the authority question commissioned

**Five substitutions in §1, all inside new § 51.1-142.4: subdivision G 4 (three) and subsections B and L (one each). §1 goes from 131,066 to 131,923 characters and three lines of §1 differ.** No figure moves, no designator is renumbered, no section joins or leaves the act, no defined term is added, no enactment clause changes, and **the title line does not change**. Amendatory markup is unmoved: `<u>`/`</u>` in §1 stay **62 / 62**, because every word touched is already new text. Written on the live base `db67d2bb` — store revision 68, 449,196 characters — and verified by comparing the returned body with the body sent.

**The card.** The Policy Director accepted four of the six answers at §7 item 132 at **18:35:16 UTC on 12 October 2026** (card `829fa6d2`): **f1-b**, **f4-a**, **f14-move** and **f11-authority**. Three are drafting and are in the text. **f11-authority is a request to counsel and not a drafting answer**, so it is commissioned as an issue and nothing is cut for it. **Not selected, and closed by the selection of their alternatives:** f1-a and f1-c (F1's other two forms), f4-b and f4-c (F4's other two forms). **Not selected and still drafted and open:** f12-ninety (the § 51.1-162 C waiver floor stays at the thirtieth day after the notice) and f3-operative (the act still does not override a straight-life election under subdivision A 1 of § 51.1-165).

#### 1. f1-b — MOO-189's fatal finding, cured in subdivision G 4's carve-out sentence

The defect was in the **pre-existing** carve-out and not in any word store revision 62 wrote: a disability retirement is not a *"qualifying retirement"*, so for a member who died with a disability application pending and whose effective date the Board set, after her death, to a date before it, the carve-out switched clause (i) off and the death branch never reached her. On the reviewer's facts the widower took § 51.1-163's refund of about **$92,600** instead of a § 51.1-162 B allowance worth about **$260,000**, decided by an administrative date chosen after his wife was dead.

**The Director's words are enacted word for word, and two conforming limbs are added. Both are on the record here rather than silent, and either can be reversed in one substitution.**

1. **"that is not a qualifying retirement"** — the approved words were confined only by *"for the purposes of this subdivision."* Read on the live text, they would have reached **a member who retired in a qualifying retirement** and whose effective date the Board determined after her death, and so would have pulled a retiree out of § 51.1-163 and into the death branch. **That is the class [MOO-161](/MOO/issues/MOO-161)'s F1 rated fatal and the class the Director's standing direction of 12 October exists to protect.** The exception is now expressly about the kind of retirement the sentence it qualifies is about.
2. **The second limb** — *"and for those purposes the member shall not be treated as having retired before the date of death in such a retirement."* The approved words define the **noun**, *"a retirement before the date of death."* The death branch's own condition is the **verb**: *"where clause (i) of this subdivision applies and **the member has not retired** before the date of death."* Without the limb the cure removes the carve-out and leaves the branch's second condition unsatisfied, so **the approved answer would not have reached the class it was approved to reach.** The limb uses the chapter's own formulation: subsection E and subdivision G 5 already say *"shall not be treated as having reached his unreduced retirement eligibility date."*

**One residual of (b), named and not drafted.** A disability retiree **already in payment** whose effective date the Board *re-determines* after her death is, on the words, a retirement the effective date of which the Board determines after the death. The natural reading is that an effective date is determined once, at approval, and a later correction is not that determination — but it is arguable. **f1-a's limb (*"and a retirement allowance has become payable to the member under that retirement"*) would close it, and f1-a was not selected**, so taking that limb now would be choosing between two forms the Director chose between. It is the first thing the next counsel pass is told to attack.

#### 2. f4-a — the greater-of is confined to coincident payees

The sentence directed the Board to pay *"whichever is greater"* of two benefits that § 51.1-165 C 2 and § 51.1-162 may make payable **to different people**, while opening with a savings clause that said the C 2 benefit was not reduced. **The card's option (a) is described and not drafted, so these words are mine and the policy is the Director's.** The greater-of now operates only where *"the person or persons to whom that subdivision requires a benefit to be paid are the same as the person or persons to whom the benefit determined under the preceding sentence would be payable"*, and where they are not the same the Board **pays the C 2 benefit and does not pay the benefit determined under the preceding sentence**.

- **The non-coincident branch is joined to the same sentence by a semicolon and is deliberately not a new sentence.** That sentence contains **four** references to *"the preceding sentence"*, all of which mean the § 51.1-162 determination sentence. A new sentence would have made *"the preceding sentence"* inside it mean the greater-of sentence. **Any further amendment of this branch must stay inside that sentence for the same reason**, and that is recorded as a standing direction.
- **Two referents are made express rather than pronominal:** *"the benefit **it** provides"* becomes *"the benefit **that subdivision** provides"*, and the payee tail becomes *"shall pay it to **that person or those persons**"*, which is the set the new condition has just identified.
- **What it costs, stated plainly because it decides who is paid.** In the non-coincident case the person to whom a § 51.1-162 benefit would have been payable **gets nothing from the act**; the § 51.1-165 C 2 payee keeps what Chapter 1 gives him. The card put that consequence in terms — *"the brother keeps his $94,000 and the husband gets nothing from the act"* — and it is the Director's choice among three forms, not a drafting choice. **No person is worse off than under present law**, because without G 4's sentence § 51.1-165 C 2 governs that death of its own force; what is removed is a double recovery the live words arguably created.

#### 3. f14-move — the eligibility bar leaves subsection L for subsection B

The sentence is moved **word for word**, −345 characters out of subsection L and +345 into subsection B: **net zero, and one space is the only character that is neither struck nor re-enacted.** It now stands immediately after the sentence that makes an accepted election the only route to a purchase and before the avoidance-of-doubt sentence, so a reader of the operative grant meets the bar in the same place. Subsection L is left as the local-mandate disclaimer alone. **Nothing strands:** *"subsection L"* is cited **nowhere** in §1, counted mechanically, and no citation count in the act changes — the Chapter 2, 2.1 and 3 citations are still **two each**, one in the definition and one in the bar.

#### 4. f11-authority — commissioned, not drafted

The Director asked for the *"complete in itself"* authority to be hardened before the drafting request goes to Division of Legislative Services. The exposure is **not** § 51.1-162 C, which store revision 67 cured by amending the section on the face of the act; it is the **two *"notwithstanding"* displacements in subdivision G 4**, which reach subdivision C 1 of § 51.1-165 and subsection F of § 51.1-165.01 and are accompanied by no amendment to either. **That is a research question for counsel and nothing is cut for it.** It is commissioned as [MOO-207](/MOO/issues/MOO-207), self-contained, with the five things wanted and the instruction that an adverse answer is a completed answer. **If the authority turns out adverse, the cure widens the act's footprint again** — a conforming amendment to § 51.1-165 or § 51.1-165.01 would add it to the title line, to the first enactment clause and to the sections reproduced at length under Article IV, Section 12.

#### 5. [MOO-92](/MOO/issues/MOO-92)'s third residual — M 2's uncapped tolling — is closed by the Director, and the item says so

The Policy Director **declined route (b)** on §7 item 130 on **13 October 2026**, out of [MOO-164](/MOO/issues/MOO-164) — *"as a decision and not by implication"*, and declined **(b) + (c)** as well. **The ground is incidence:** subdivision G 4's *"a failure of the Board to act … shall not extend that aggregate"* caps the **Board's** clocks, while in subdivision M 2 the identical words cap the **member's** tolling, so the form is conserved and the effect reverses. **Route (c) is adopted instead** in MOO-164, as candidate 164-7 with the trigger limb (B). Limb (i) is already bounded by **m8-a**, enacted at store revision 67, at 120 days plus at most 90; limbs (ii), (iii) and (iv) are MOO-164's. **The item-130 annotation is replaced, not supplemented**, because an annotation that says *"held"* against a decision that says *"declined"* is a defect of the record. Nothing is drafted for it here and nothing is reversed.

#### 6. Items 132, 133 and 134 were stranded outside the live list, and are moved back

All three had been written **after** the *"### 7.1 Retained record"* subsection, which is captioned a retained record — so a reader of the live item list never reached them, and this is the second time this has happened in this document (store revision 37 did it with items 59, 60 and 61). **The three items are moved verbatim to the end of the live list, immediately before §7.1. Nothing is renumbered, no word of any item changes in the move, and §7 is asserted contiguous 1 to 134 before and after**, so a reference of the form *"§7 item 132"* resolves both before and after this revision.

#### 7. What this revision does not do

**No answer the Director did not select is enacted.** f12-ninety and f3-operative stay drafted and open at §7 item 132, and f1-a, f1-c, f4-b and f4-c are closed by the selection of their alternatives and are marked as such. **§7 item 60's (a), (b) and (c) are untouched and still unanswered** — the elected-option class whose contingent annuitant is a sibling, an adult child or an unmarried partner, whom § 51.1-162 B does not pay. **Subsection E1 is not re-cut** and stays under the counsel gate at [MOO-192](/MOO/issues/MOO-192). **No record section is moved out of this document**; the ceiling did not force one.

#### 8. [MOO-200](/MOO/issues/MOO-200) landed while this revision was being cut, and three of its findings bear on the answers enacted here

The bounded pass on store revision 67's nineteen substitutions reported at **00:15 UTC on 13 October 2026**: **0 fatal, 5 serious, 7 moderate, 6 minor, and 12 null results**, read on this same base. **Its findings are not disposed in this revision** — that is the next revision, and it is mine — but three of them touch what is enacted here and the record says so now rather than later.

- **F14 (minor) is aggravated by f14-move, and the Director should know it.** The pass says subsection L exists to tell localities, school boards and the Commission on Local Government that the act does not reach them, and that store revision 67's cross-reference to the definition already made the comfort derivative. **f14-move removes the sentence from that subsection altogether**, so the audience the subsection is written for now gets nothing on the subject in the subsection addressed to them. **The eligibility rule is unchanged** — the bar is in the act, in subsection B, and the definition is where it always was — so this is an audience question and not a defect in the bar. F14's second point, that the bar has **no time anchor** while subdivision D 4 determines eligibility as of the filing date of the complete application, is **independent of where the bar sits** and survives the move unchanged. Both are on the Director's card.
- **F6 and F7 (moderate) attack the sentence f4-a amends, and f4-a neither cures nor worsens them.** F7's case is that the greater-of comparison *"changes no amount for anyone"* — in the branch where subdivision C 2 of § 51.1-165 routes to § 51.1-163 the allowance wins on any basis, and in the branch where C 2 routes to § 51.1-162 B both sides are the same number — and F6's is that *"the actuarial assumptions and factors the Board uses to determine actuarial equivalence under § 51.1-165"* is not an identifiable set, because § 51.1-124.3 defines *"actuarial equivalent"* by reference to *"actuarial tables **adopted** by the Board"* and no § 51.1-165 option converts an allowance to a lump sum at all. **f4-a changes when the comparison runs and not what it is measured on.** Whether the comparison should exist at all is F7's question and it is the Director's.

**On f4-a's own consequence, with the pass's reading folded in and stated plainly.** Where subdivision C 2 of § 51.1-165 applies and its own switch to § 51.1-162 B fails — because the contingent annuitant is not the § 51.1-162 B payee, or because the B benefit is not greater — C 2 requires payment in accordance with § 51.1-163, and under f4-a the Board pays that and does not pay the benefit determined under the preceding sentence. **That is a refund where the live text of store revisions 67 and 68 would have paid an allowance.** It is also exactly what Chapter 1 does for that family today: **no person is worse off than under present law, and the act buys that class nothing.** The card said so in terms, and the choice is the Director's.

#### 9. These five substitutions have had no adversarial pass

[MOO-200](/MOO/issues/MOO-200) is bounded to store revision **67** and **excludes them**; it was read and reported before they existed. On this subdivision every unreviewed cure so far has drawn a fatal finding from the next pass, and two of these five are the cure to a fatal finding. **A bounded pass on these five and nothing else is commissioned.**

**Internal working draft. Not a Division of Legislative Services bill, not cleared by counsel or by DLS, not legally sufficient for introduction. Pre-decisional and internal.**

### 0.0-68 Store revision 68 — record only. [MOO-195](/MOO/issues/MOO-195)'s pass 21 on the designation-review advisory dispositioned: four corrections to store revision 65's own record, two new §7 items, and seven findings put to the Policy Director undrafted

**Not a drafting revision.** No word of § 51.1-142.2, § 51.1-142.4 or § 51.1-162 moves. No enactment clause, no title line, no figure, no designator, no defined term. **§1 is character-for-character what store revision 67 left**, and `apply68.py` asserts that before it writes. The whole of this revision is four in-place corrections to records **I** wrote at store revision 65, two new items on [`bill-text-s8-item-list`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-item-list), and this narrative.

**Why there is a revision at all.** Pass 21 at [MOO-195](/MOO/issues/MOO-195) reviewed the 137 characters cut as store revision 65 and returned **11 findings — 0 fatal, 3 serious, 5 moderate, 3 minor — and 11 null results**. Two of the findings are not about the enacted words at all; they are about **statements in my own record that are not true**, and a record that misstates the law is worse than no record. Those are corrected here, in place and marked, not rewritten away.

**Re-verified on the live base before anything was written.** The pass was run on store revision 65. The store has moved twice since — **66** ([MOO-182](/MOO/issues/MOO-182)'s A4 and A2) and **67** (the Director's four answers and eleven [MOO-189](/MOO/issues/MOO-189) findings) — and store revision 67 amended **subdivision D 1 itself**. So every anchor the pass relies on was re-counted on document revision `ea3b25b0`, §1 at **131,066** characters, `<u>`/`</u>` **62 / 62**:

- the enacted advisory — *"that the member is advised to review the member's designation of beneficiary under subsection A of § 51.1-162 before completing payment;"* — resolves **1× in §1** and is unaltered;
- **all five FIND strings** the pass hands to drafting resolve **exactly once in §1** at store revision 67;
- the certification's series is still **seven items**, the advisory is still item **5 of 7**, and the forward reference it splits (item 4's *"subsection C of that section"* → item 6) is still split.

**What store revision 67 changed about the findings, stated because it changes two of them.**

1. **F1 is weakened and not cured.** Store revision 67 rewrote item 4 of the series, which now states **both** branches of § 51.1-162 B: the designated qualifying person *"or who is entitled in the order of precedence that subsection B of that section states where the member has made no designation."* So the certification now does tell the member the no-designation rule, one item before the advice. **It still does not tell her that making a designation of a non-qualifying person destroys that rule**, which is the whole of F1's harm, and the advisory still gives the review no object.
2. **F8 is ripe and larger, not smaller.** The pass said the title cure was free and would come when the title was next opened. **The title was opened at store revision 67** — it now reads *"to amend and reenact §§ 51.1-142.2 **and 51.1-162** of the Code of Virginia"* — **and the *"relating to"* clause still names no death benefit, beneficiary or designation**, while subdivision D 1 now compels seven statements about them and the act now amends a death-benefit section on its face. That is a Va. Const. art. IV, § 12 point and it is for the Director and the Editor, not for a record revision.

**The four corrections, each in place and each marked.**

| Where | What was wrong | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| **§6.0-65 item 5** | The item gave two reasons for *"before completing payment."* The second — that it is *"the whole window during which re-designating changes the outcome"* — **is false.** § 51.1-162 A fixes no designation at the completion of payment; a designation is revocable until the member dies. The real window runs ~27 months on the act's own dials, and without end for a member who does not retire. **Withdrawn.** The first reason stands | F2 |
| **§6.0-65 item 6** | The item routed the channel question to *"the counsel gate at [MOO-192](/MOO/issues/MOO-192)."* MOO-192 is the **subsection E1** gate and has the channel out of scope. The question went to pass 21, which answered it. **Repointed, and the answer recorded** | F10 |
| **§5.43 direction 1** | The *"designation of beneficiary"* row is accurate as written — it claims the **component** vocabulary is § 51.1-162 A's — but the compound itself is **not** in A, and the MOO-191 issue description restated the row as though it were. **Precision added; no text changes** | F11 |
| **§5.43 check 4** | Two things. §7 item 129 answer (7) **has since been answered and enacted** at store revision 67, so the check is spent; and it **conflated** answer (7)'s § 51.1-162 C operative override with the general point that a reader of § 51.1-162 learns nothing. The advisory overrides nothing and does not enlarge (7) — that is pass 21's **N8**, a null | N8, F5, F8 |

**Two new items on the item list, and one residual closed.**

- **§7 item 133 is new** — D 1's failure sentence and subdivision M 2 are both framed on **absence**: *"a failure to state."* Neither reaches a matter that is stated and **wrong**. The advisory is the one item of the seven a plain-language template will paraphrase rather than drop, so it is the item for which the gap bites hardest. **The words are drafted and nothing is enacted**: the defect is a property of a sentence the Director approved at store revision 62, it interacts with the channel question, and it is not in the scope of a cut commissioned as one edit. Pass 21 F7.
- **§7 item 134 is new, and it closes item 129's conservator residual as asked.** Item 129 asked the next counsel pass whether a conservator acting for a living member, who receives the D 1 certification and is in no class in subdivision M 2, is a defect or a non-case. **Pass 21's answer: a non-case**, because M 2's class is a list of parties in interest, not of representatives, and a conservator exercises the member's own standing. The **narrow** residual — a Board that reads M 2's *"written request filed"* as personal to the member could refuse a conservator at intake — is drafted as one clause and **not enacted**. Item 129's residual is marked answered and points here.

**What this revision does not do.** **No enacted word is changed, added or struck.** The advisory is **not** re-cut: not given an object (F1), not re-pointed off *"before completing payment"* (F2), not excepted from subsection M (F3), not moved to the end of the series (F4), not given a route or an acknowledgment (F5), not given a no-fault clause (F6), and not reworded to name the Board as the advisor (F9). **Each of those seven reverses or widens a word the Policy Director approved verbatim at [MOO-188](/MOO/issues/MOO-188) §3.3 and §3.4, and the choice is theirs and not mine.** They are drafted word for word, re-verified against store revision 67, at [`moo195-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-191#document-moo195-disposition) on [MOO-191](/MOO/issues/MOO-191). **Subsection E1 is not re-cut** and stays under the counsel gate at [MOO-192](/MOO/issues/MOO-192). **The [MOO-141](/MOO/issues/MOO-141) patch is not applied.** **MOO-97 §7 item 129 answers (3) and (5) stay open**, and no other §7 item is answered or closed.

**Internal working draft. Not a Division of Legislative Services bill, not cleared by counsel or by DLS, not legally sufficient for introduction. Pre-decisional and internal.**

### 0.0-67 Store revision 67 — the Policy Director's four answers of 12 October enacted, eleven of [MOO-189](/MOO/issues/MOO-189)'s findings enacted, and **§ 51.1-162 joins the act's amended sections**. MOO-189's fatal F1 is **not** cured here and is on a card

**Nineteen substitutions in §1.** Sixteen are inside new § 51.1-142.4 — subsection A's giving rule, subdivision D 1, subdivision G 4, subsection L, subdivision M 2 and subsection N. Three are the act's **footprint**: the title line, the first enactment clause, and **§ 51.1-162 reproduced in full as amended**, which is the first section this act has added to its amended sections since revision 21 removed § 51.1-155. **No figure moves, no designator inside § 51.1-142.4 is renumbered, no defined term is added, and no § 51.1-142.4 provision joins or leaves the act.** Sweep **§5.45**; drafter's note **§6.0-67**.

**What was decided and by whom.** Four answers on §7 item 129, approved by the Policy Director on the pending card at **18:04:59 UTC on 12 October 2026** — **f7-a**, **s163-valuation**, **m8-a** and **s162c-conforming**; **f7-b, f3-c-instead, f5-discovery, m7-matching and m8-b were not selected**. Answer (4) was answered separately at [MOO-188](/MOO/issues/MOO-188) and enacted as store revision 65 on [MOO-191](/MOO/issues/MOO-191); it is not re-cut. Eleven findings out of [MOO-189](/MOO/issues/MOO-189)'s counsel pass on store revision 62 — **F2, F3, F5, F6, F7, F8, F9, F10, F13, F14 and F15** — are enacted on the Drafting Specialist's own authority as conformance of the act to its own cited sections, which is drafting and not policy. The full disposition, finding by finding, is at [`moo189-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-97#document-moo189-disposition).

**The act's footprint changes, and that is the largest thing in this revision.** The title line now reads *"to amend and reenact §§ 51.1-142.2 **and 51.1-162** of the Code of Virginia"*, the first enactment clause follows it, and **§ 51.1-162 is reproduced in full as amended in §1 in Code order after § 51.1-142.4**, carrying exactly one underlined insertion in the third paragraph of subsection C:

> Any beneficiary entitled to the entire amount of a retirement allowance under the provisions of this subsection as a result of the death of a member shall be entitled to waive his rights to the allowance by written notification to the Board within ninety days after the death of the member<u>, except as subdivision G 4 of § 51.1-142.4 provides,</u> in order to make available a retirement allowance under the provisions of subsection B of this section.

**Every other word of § 51.1-162 is the official text, reproduced plain and verbatim, read at source on 12 October 2026** (`law.lis.virginia.gov/vacode/title51.1/chapter1/section51.1-162/`, text current to 10/12/2026). **It was not retyped from any record in this file or in any other issue's document.** The reproduction is what makes the act readable as amended: a reader can now see that the Program's extension of the § 51.1-162 C waiver period is in § 51.1-162 C, which was [MOO-189](/MOO/issues/MOO-189)'s **F11** — the hidden override — and that nothing else in that section moves.

**What the other three answers do.**

- **f7-a**, in subsection N. The Commonwealth may not pay an amount settled on a G 4 death until the Board knows whether a § 51.1-162 allowance is payable and, where it is, whether the person entitled to it takes contributions or a lump sum instead. **This is the answer to the package's load-bearing fiscal assumption.** On subsection N's own words the Commonwealth paid *"upon the settlement"*, subdivision G 4 settles on the date of death, and the release enacted in store revision 61 operates *"only to the extent the amount settled has not been paid"* — so **nothing was ever released**, and every such death left a permanent charge against the $3,000,000 limitation for a benefit nobody received. The answer makes the release operable by deferring the cash, not by taking money back out of the trust fund; **f7-b, which would have done the latter, was not selected.**
- **s163-valuation**, in subdivision G 4's greater-of sentence. The approved measure compared two benefits *"on the basis of actuarial equivalence as of the date of the member's death"*, and one side was incomputable, because § 51.1-163 measures contributions *"as of the effective date of his retirement"* and in a pre-effective-date death that date never arrived. The § 51.1-163 side is now determined as though that date were the date of death. **It fixes an amount, which is why it was a card and not a drafting fix.**
- **m8-a**, in subdivision M 2 clause (ii). The 120-day aggregate tolling cap expressly excluded *"any period after the expiration of the 90 days allowed by this subsection and before the Board acts upon the request"* — so **every day of Board delay past 90 days tolled without limit.** The excluded periods are now capped at 90 days in the aggregate. **It bounds the Board's delay without ever costing the member her qualifying retirement, which is what m8-b would have risked and the Director did not select.**

**One word of an approved sentence is changed and it is on the record rather than silent.** f7-a's drafted tail read *"or a lump sum in lieu of **it**."* The enacted tail reads *"or a lump sum in lieu of **any other benefit under that section**"* — which is § 51.1-162 B's own phrase and the phrase subdivision G 4 already uses twice for the same election. Two formulations of one concept inside one section is a defect; *"in lieu of it"* also left *"it"* with two candidate referents. **The meaning is unchanged. §6.0-67 item 3.**

**One placement decision on an approved clause.** s162c-conforming's approved words were *"except as subdivision G 4 of § 51.1-142.4 provides"*, with no place stated. They are placed **immediately after the ninety days and before *"in order to make available"***, so that they qualify the **period** and not the right to waive. **§6.0-67 item 4.**

**The fatal finding is not answered here and that is deliberate.** [MOO-189](/MOO/issues/MOO-189)'s **F1** holds that subdivision G 4's death branch does not reach a member who dies with a disability-retirement application pending and whose effective date the Board then sets, after her death, to a date before it — § 51.1-156 D expressly allows a ninety-day look-back and § 51.1-156 F expressly allows her appointing authority to file for her. **Clause (i)'s own carve-out then switches the branch off and § 51.1-163 governs; the reviewer puts the loss at about $167,000 in present value, decided by an administrative date chosen after she was dead.** Two things follow. **The defect is in the carve-out, which is pre-existing, not in store revision 62's words.** And **every cure is a third form of the condition on that sentence** — the Policy Director's standing direction of 12 October, recorded at [`standing-drafting-directions`](/MOO/issues/MOO-97#document-standing-drafting-directions) Part A1, forecloses two forms, and the reviewer was told in terms that a defect needing a third form is a card and not a drafting fix. **It is drafted two ways on the card and enacted neither way. This is the fourth cure of this one sentence to be put to the Director rather than taken.**

**Two other findings are carded for the same reason.** **F4** — subdivision G 4's savings clause (*"Nothing in the preceding sentence reduces a benefit payable under subdivision C 2 of § 51.1-165"*) and its greater-of cannot both stand where the two provisions name different payees: on the reviewer's facts the brother loses the whole $94,000 rather than the difference. **The sentence is internally contradictory and resolving it by drafting choice would decide who is paid.** **F12** — whether the § 51.1-162 C waiver floor should be thirty days after the notice or ninety, which the reviewer expressly left as a policy setting.

**Record housekeeping, no operative effect.** The §1 editorial note's *"The act now amends one section and adds one"* is **corrected** — it is now two and one. §2's section-by-section table gains a row for § 51.1-162 C. §5's and §6's preambles and §8.1's count are brought current. **No record is moved out of this document**: headroom on arrival was 178,174 characters and this revision uses about 24,000 of it.

### 0.0-66 Store revision 66 — [MOO-182](/MOO/issues/MOO-182)'s counsel findings A4 and A2 cut in. One inserted sentence in subdivision G 4, ten substituted anchors in § 51.1-142.4, an actor for subdivision B 4 e, one new defined term, and four conforming amendments

**Sixteen substitutions and two insertions across six provisions of §1 — subdivision G 4, subdivisions G 2, G 3 and M 2 and the "Certified subsidy" definition of § 51.1-142.4, subsection J 1, the fifth enactment clause, and subdivisions B 4 a and B 4 e of § 51.1-142.2. One defined term is added. No figure moves, no designator is renumbered, no section joins or leaves the act, not one word of existing (non-underlined) text is struck or reworded, no sentence boundary of any existing sentence moves, and the title line does not change.** Sweep **§5.44**; drafter's note **§6.0-66**.

**What was decided and by whom.** The **board decision of 12 October 2026 on [MOO-182](/MOO/issues/MOO-182)** — fix counsel findings **A4** and **A2** before the package goes to Division of Legislative Services — commissioned on [MOO-196](/MOO/issues/MOO-196), which carried both findings drafting-ready and self-contained. The source of the findings is [`rev65-handoff`](/MOO/issues/MOO-182#document-rev65-handoff). **Both findings are accepted in full; neither is refused and neither is narrowed.**

**Verified before the cut.** Document revision `bc0576f5`, **store revision 65**, the base MOO-196 names. **Every one of MOO-196's five asserted before-counts was re-counted over enacted §1 and every one matched to the integer** — `in accordance with subdivision D 7` **7**, `employer that paid` **10**, `accept no further election until the excess is eliminated` **1**, `otherwise than in accordance with subdivision D 7` **3**, curly apostrophes in §1 **4**. The store had not moved, so no anchor needed re-counting against a different base.

---

#### Edit 1 — A4: subdivision G 4's reinstatement sentence no longer captures the releases the act itself commands

**The defect, confirmed at source and worse by one case than the finding states.** Subdivision D 7 is a closed list, and **three of the act's own commanded releases are on none of its grounds**:

1. **Subdivision G 1, third sentence** — *"The Board shall release the amount by which the amount reserved for the election, together with the contingency amount reserved under subdivision D 3, exceeds the amount settled."*
2. **Subdivision G 4's own redetermination release** — the same words in the lower case and joined by *"and"*, which is why a FIND on the capitalised form resolves once in §1 and not twice.
3. **Subdivision D 3, third sentence** — *"the Board shall release it when the certified subsidy is settled under subdivision G 1 or G 4 or the reservation is released under subdivision 7."*

**A fourth, which MOO-196 does not list and which the cut also catches:** subdivision G 4's **death-case release** — *"the Board shall release the amount settled under this subdivision and shall restore it to the amount available under the limitation stated in the first sentence of subdivision D 2."* It is commanded by G 4 and is on none of D 7's grounds, so before this cut it too fell into the 60-day *"learns of the release"* limb. **The enacted words reach it without naming it, because the general words do the work and the "including" list is illustrative.**

**The enacted words — one inserted sentence, counsel's words verbatim.** Inserted in subdivision G 4 immediately **before** the sentence beginning `Where a reservation has been released otherwise than in accordance with subdivision D 7,` and immediately **after** the sentence ending `whereupon the Board shall restore the whole of the service credit reduced under the determination.` The whole of § 51.1-142.4 is new language and subdivision G 4 is a single underlined paragraph of 30,897 characters, so the insertion **carries no markup of its own**:

> … whereupon the Board shall restore the whole of the service credit reduced under the determination. <u>**For the purposes of the next sentence, a release that this section required or permitted the Board to make at the time it was made, including a release required by subdivision G 1, by this subdivision, or by subdivision D 3 upon the settlement of a certified subsidy, is a release in accordance with subdivision D 7.**</u> Where a reservation has been released otherwise than in accordance with subdivision D 7, or has been released in accordance with that subdivision where a determination is thereafter rescinded under this subdivision …

**Form chosen: the deeming sentence, which is counsel's recommendation, and not the alternative affirmative trigger.** MOO-196 left the choice of form to me. Four reasons, in the order they decided it:

1. **It preserves the second limb, which is the whole point of deeming in rather than excluding out.** Deeming the commanded release *into* subdivision D 7 moves it out of the 60-day *"learns of the release"* limb and into the *"released in accordance with that subdivision where a determination is thereafter rescinded … or is reversed or modified"* limb. An ordinary commanded release is left alone; **a commanded release that followed a determination later rescinded or reversed still comes back.** The A1 exclusion form first drafted on MOO-182 would have stranded that second case, which is why MOO-196 directed the A4 words and not the A1 words, and why I used them.
2. **One insertion against three substitutions and a change to compelled notice content.** The alternative would have rewritten both occurrences of `otherwise than in accordance with subdivision D 7` inside the reinstatement sentence and conformed the notice sentence to *"of whether the release was one this section required or permitted"* — **changing what the Board must tell the two money-committee Chairmen.** The deeming sentence changes no compelled notice content at all. Minimal footprint.
3. **It needs no new vocabulary.** The alternative introduces *"as this section required or permitted at the time of the release"* as an operative trigger in three places. The deeming sentence uses the same idea **once**, in a sentence whose only function is to define a term of the next sentence.
4. **Scope is on the face of it.** `For the purposes of the next sentence` is visible, checkable and self-limiting, where a global deeming provision would have moved four independent operative uses — above all the settlement bar, which decides **when a certified subsidy may be settled at all**.

**The scoping held, and it is the criterion this edit had to meet.** `in accordance with subdivision D 7` appears **7** times in §1 before the cut and **8** after. **All four independent operative uses read after the cut exactly as they read before it**, each still resolving once: the settlement bar (*"released in full in accordance with subdivision D 7 and has not been reinstated"*), the rescission sentence, the reversal/modification sentence, and the notice sentence's *"of whether the release was in accordance with subdivision D 7."* **The involuntary-separation sentence's borrowing is unchanged** — *"in the manner this subdivision requires in the case of a reservation released otherwise than in accordance with subdivision D 7"* — which borrows the *manner* and not the trigger. `otherwise than in accordance with subdivision D 7` is **3 before and 3 after**. `accept no further election until the excess is eliminated` is **1 before and 1 after**.

**What the cut stops.** On the plain words before it, the Board had to undo its own commanded release within 60 days of learning of it, charge it back over the self-executing **$3,000,000** bar, notify both money-committee Chairmen of *"the nature of any error"* where there was none, notify every subdivision D 5 waiting-list applicant, request a subdivision D 3 confirmation, certify an excess to the Governor, and **accept no further election until the excess is eliminated** — on every ordinary, correct settlement the Program produces. **That is a self-executing shutdown of the pilot triggered by the pilot working.**

---

#### Edit 2 — A2: `"employer"` is undefined, resolved to the Commonwealth, and subdivision B 4 e's policy duty had no actor

**The defect, confirmed at source.** § 51.1-142.4 A defines **13** terms — **MOO-196 says 18, and counted on the body it is 13; nothing in the finding turns on it** (§5.44 check 4) — and *"employer"* is **not** one of them, while *"state agency"* **is** — *"any agency, authority, board, bureau, commission, department, or institution of the Commonwealth, including a public institution of higher education and an agency of the legislative or judicial branch."* § 51.1-124.3 therefore governed: *"'Employer' means the Commonwealth in the case of a state employee."* **Subsection L** still excludes teachers and employees of political subdivisions from the Program, so **in every case the Program can produce, *"employer"* meant the Commonwealth** — one entity, which made *"in proportion to the amounts each paid"* meaningless and made the Commonwealth a party entitled to seek reconsideration and judicial review against its own Board, with the Attorney General entitled to appear on both sides under § 2.2-507 A.

**2a — the ten anchors, all ten substituted, including the curly one.** `employer that paid` → **0** occurrences in §1; `state agency from whose appropriation an amount was paid` → **10**. Counsel's suggested words, in the form the surrounding text required:

> any ~~employer that paid~~ <u>**state agency from whose appropriation an amount was paid**</u> on the member's behalf

**The anchor was chosen to stop before the apostrophe, which dissolves the string-anchor hazard rather than working around it.** MOO-196 warned that **9 of the 10** anchors use a straight apostrophe in `member's` and **one uses the curly `member’s`** — subdivision G 4's *"the member, or any employer that paid on the member’s behalf, may pay to the Board the amount refunded…"* — and that a substitution anchored on the straight form would silently miss it. **Anchoring on `employer that paid` alone leaves `on the member’s behalf` untouched in every case, so the curly occurrence is caught by the same substitution as the other nine and no apostrophe is normalised, moved or created.** Curly apostrophes in §1: **4 before, 4 after.** The curly occurrence now reads *"any state agency from whose appropriation an amount was paid on the member’s behalf"* and is verified present in the written body.

Where they are — and **MOO-196's own inventory is off by one on this, which the record should say.** MOO-196 describes the ten as *"8 refund, payment and notice limbs in subsection G"* plus the two M 2 class lists. The total of **ten** is exactly right and every one was found and substituted, but the distribution is **D 6 ×1, G 2 ×1, G 4 ×6, M 2 ×2**: the first anchor is in **subdivision D 6**, not subsection G — *"The Board shall pay a refund required by this subdivision to the member and to any employer that paid on the member's behalf, in proportion to the amounts each paid, within 60 days after the recertification"* — which is the **recertification** refund and is governed by subdivision D 6, whose words *"required by this subdivision"* tie it there. One is in subdivision **G 2**, six are in subdivision **G 4**'s refund, payment and notice limbs, and two are the subdivision **M 2** reconsideration and judicial-review class lists. **Nothing turns on the miscount** — the substitution is uniform and the D 6 limb needed it for the same reason as the other nine — but a later reader counting *"eight in subsection G"* would not find them. The M 2 pair keeps the indefinite *"a member's behalf"* the class list was drafted with; the eight in subsection G keep the definite *"the member's behalf"*. **No article, number or possessive was changed.**
