**What the substitution repairs beyond the definition.** *"In proportion to the amounts each paid"* now does work: a member may be employed successively by more than one state agency, so there can genuinely be two or more agency payers to apportion between. And the Commonwealth is no longer in M 2's reconsideration and judicial-review classes in its own right.

**2b — the actor gap in subdivision B 4 e of § 51.1-142.2.** The act named **no one** with power to adopt, file or publish the policy. Read literally, an agency could pay only if **the Commonwealth** had adopted one policy applying to all state employees who are eligible members and filed it with the Board — and no officer was empowered to do that. **Either every agency payment was unlawful, or each agency filed its own and the Board had to decide, with no standard, whether an agency policy was the employer's.**

**Enacted: the state agency that employs the member is the duty-holder**, which is the fix direction MOO-196 recommended. Five substitutions in subdivision B 4 e, and **the first sentence is not touched**:

> <u>Notwithstanding subsection G, the member shall pay no less than five percent of the amount determined under subdivision b from funds other than funds paid by an employer to or on behalf of the member for the purpose of making such payment.</u> **A <u>state agency</u> may pay the remainder of that amount only if the <u>state agency</u> has first adopted a written policy governing such payments that applies to all of its employees who are eligible members as defined in § 51.1-142.4, and the <u>state agency</u> shall file that policy with the Board before the Board accepts any election by its employees. The policy may state a maximum aggregate amount that the <u>state agency</u> will pay in a fiscal year and the order in which the <u>state agency</u> will apply that amount, provided that the order shall be stated in the policy, shall be fixed in advance of any application of the policy, and shall be either the order in which its employees file complete applications with the Board or an order determined by lot. The <u>state agency</u> shall publish the policy.

**The five-percent member-funded floor is enacted exactly as it stood and deliberately keeps *"an employer."*** §6.0-66 item 4. The floor disqualifies *funds*, and the class of disqualified funds must stay as wide as § 51.1-124.3 makes it; narrowing it to *"a state agency"* would have been a **substantive loosening nobody agreed**. The floor sentence resolves once in §1 before and after, character for character. **The order-fixing proviso is likewise untouched** — *"provided that the order shall be stated in the policy, shall be fixed in advance of any application of the policy, and shall be either the order in which its employees file complete applications with the Board or an order determined by lot"* — one occurrence before, one after, and the fiscal-year maximum stays **permissive** while only the order is mandatory and only where a maximum is stated, exactly as MOO-196 directed.

**A new defined term, because the term is now used before it is defined.** *"State agency"* is defined in § 51.1-142.4 A, but subdivision B 4 e is in **§ 51.1-142.2**, whose own definitions for subdivision 4 sit in subdivision B 4 a. Inserted there, alphabetically between *"Standard cost"* and *"Statutory interest"*, in the definition-by-reference form this act already uses three times (*"Election"*, *"Involuntary separation"* and *"Standard cost"*, each *"means … as defined in …"*) and matching its citation practice of naming the subsection that carries the definition:

> <u>**"State agency" means a state agency as defined in subsection A of § 51.1-142.4.**</u>

**This is the one thing in the cut that MOO-196 did not ask for, and it is not a policy choice.** It is the direct consequence of using the term: a term used in § 51.1-142.2 and defined only in § 51.1-142.4 would have been an undefined operative term in the section where the duty sits. §6.0-66 item 3.

**2b is not a Dillon's Rule issue, and the record should say why.** Subdivision B 4 e imposes no duty on a locality in any case the Program can produce, because **subsection L** excludes political-subdivision employees, and the policy condition is keyed to *"employees who are eligible members as defined in § 51.1-142.4"* — which only a state agency can ever have. **No express grant to a locality is needed and none is drafted.** If the Lab ever intends B 4 e to reach purchases outside the Program, that is a change of footprint and belongs on a card; §6.0-66 item 7.

---

**Four conforming amendments, which MOO-196 did not list and which the sweep required.** Making the state agency the B 4 e payer stranded four references to the payer elsewhere in the act. §5.44 direction 2:

| Where | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| "Certified subsidy" definition, clause (iii) | the amounts payable by the member and by any **employer** under subdivision B 4 of § 51.1-142.2 | … by any **state agency** … |
| "Certified subsidy" definition, floor sentence | the amounts payable by the member and by any **employer** under subdivision B 4 of § 51.1-142.2 | … by any **state agency** … |
| Subsection J 1, report content | the amounts paid by members and by **employers** | … by members and by **state agencies** |
| Fifth enactment clause, clause (ii) | the payment methods and the **employer payment** authorized by subdivision B 4 of § 51.1-142.2 | … the **state agency payment** authorized … |

**Six uses of *"employer"* survive in § 51.1-142.4 and every one is deliberate.** §5.44 direction 3 lists them individually. In short: **one anti-coercion shield** (*"no employer shall require, request, or induce any employee to make an election"*), **four non-liability and no-obligation shields**, and **one term of art** (*"employer contributions under § 51.1-145"*). For the five shields the § 51.1-124.3 term is **wider than *"state agency"* and the width is protective** — narrowing them would have let a payer outside the class coerce a member or pursue one for money. **The sixth is the name of a thing § 51.1-145 creates and cannot be renamed here.**

**Movement, measured on the written body and reconciled edit by edit.** §1 **121,575 → 122,400**, document **346,114 → 346,939**, **+825 on both** before this revision's own §0, §5, §6 and §8 record. The +825 reconciles exactly and with no residue:

| Edit | Characters |
|---|---|
| Edit 1 — the deeming sentence and its trailing space | +317 |
| Edit 2a — ten anchors at +38 each | +380 |
| Edit 2b — the subdivision B 4 a definition and its two newlines | +88 |
| Edit 2b — the five subdivision B 4 e actor substitutions | +23 |
| Four conforming amendments | +17 |
| **Total** | **+825** |

**One measurement convention, stated because it differs by one character from the figure in §0.0-65.** My §1 span runs from the `## 1. The bill` heading to the character before the `## 2.` heading. §0.0-65 recorded §1 at **121,576** on this same base and I measure **121,575**. **Nothing changed between the two readings** — store revision 65 is the base I verified at document revision `bc0576f5` — and the one character is a boundary-newline convention, not a movement. The delta is the figure that matters and it is **+825** on either convention.

**`<u>` / `</u>` balance in §1: 60 / 60 before, 61 / 61 after, and the one added pair is the new subdivision B 4 a definition.** Edit 1's sentence and all fifteen substitutions add no markup, because each falls inside a paragraph that is already underlined end to end. The balance is equal before and after, which is the check that matters.

**What this revision does not do.** **No open §7 item is answered or closed** — this cut is MOO-182's A4 and A2 and nothing else. **Subsection E1 is not re-cut** and stays under the counsel gate at [MOO-192](/MOO/issues/MOO-192). **The [MOO-141](/MOO/issues/MOO-141) patch is not applied**, and [MOO-176](/MOO/issues/MOO-176) revision 6's three FIND strings and all six [MOO-164](/MOO/issues/MOO-164) anchors were re-counted over §1 **after** every substitution and each still resolves exactly once. **No publication deadline is added to subdivision B 4 e** — it is a real gap, it is a new provision, and it is raised at §6.0-66 item 5 for the Policy Director instead of being drafted in.

### 0.0-65 Store revision 65 — F10's designation-review advisory enacted. One substitution in subdivision D 1, and [MOO-97](/MOO/issues/MOO-97) §7 item 129 answer (4) is answered and closed

**One substitution in one provision of §1 — subdivision D 1 of § 51.1-142.4. No figure moves, no designator is renumbered, no section joins or leaves the act, no defined term is added, not one word of existing text is struck or reworded, and the title line does not change.** Sweep **§5.43**; drafter's note **§6.0-65**.

**What was decided and by whom.** The Policy Director at [MOO-188](/MOO/issues/MOO-188) **§3.3** — *"Approved, and here is the whole of my reasoning"* — and commissioned at §8 of the same decision as the single edit of this cut, on [MOO-191](/MOO/issues/MOO-191). It is the **second limb of [MOO-169](/MOO/issues/MOO-169)'s F10**, drafted at [MOO-97](/MOO/issues/MOO-97) §7 item 129 answer (4) and enacted nowhere until now: counsel asked for two things on F10 and store revision 62 enacted one of them.

**The defect, in one sentence.** § 51.1-162 B pays a **designated** spouse, minor child or parent, so a member who designated her sister in 2009, buys 36 months and is survived by her mother gets **nothing** from the purchase — and **store revision 62 stopped the certification telling her something untrue, which does not tell her to look.** The designation is the one fact that decides whether the Commonwealth's subsidy buys her family anything and the one fact she can change for free and in a minute.

**The enacted words.** Inserted into the Board's certification under subdivision D 1 as **its own semicolon item, immediately after the § 51.1-162 B proposition** — the FIND resolved **once in the whole document and once in §1** on the body that was written, asserted before the substitution. The whole of § 51.1-142.4 is new language and subdivision D 1 is a single underlined paragraph, so the insertion carries no markup of its own:

> … or who is entitled in the order of precedence that subsection B of that section states where the member has made no designation or the person designated has predeceased the member and no other designation has been made; <u>**that the member is advised to review the member's designation of beneficiary under subsection A of § 51.1-162 before completing payment;**</u> that subsection C of § 51.1-162 provides a retirement allowance …

**Two departures from the words drafted at item 129 answer (4), both the Director's and both deliberate.**

1. **The verb.** The drafted clause read *"and that the member **should review** the member's designation of beneficiary."* Every other item in this series is a proposition of law introduced by *"that"*, so *"that the member should review"* reads as a statement about what the law requires of the member, which it is not. **The enacted words make it advice on its face** — *"that the member **is advised to** review"* — and the actor who gives the advice is named by the carrier sentence, which is the Board's own certification. **Nothing in the enacted words imposes a duty on the member, and nothing turns on whether she takes the advice.**
2. **The placement.** Its own semicolon item rather than an appendage to the § 51.1-162 B proposition, because **the designation is B's fact but the advice is not B's law**. Keeping it separate leaves the propositions of law together and makes the advice visibly advice.

**The consequence, accepted by the Director at MOO-188 §3.4 before the cut and not discovered after it.** Subdivision D 1's failure sentence reaches *"every matter this subdivision requires to be stated"*, so **omitting this advice is a failure reviewable under subsection M** — the member's 30 days running from the certification she received — **and a failure the Board must state in the report required by subsection J**. The advisory is **not** excepted from the failure sentence, and the Director's reasoning is that while the member lives the review restores the only thing that matters, which is time to re-designate, and after she dies it adds nothing and costs nothing for the same reason.

**The label moved twice under this cut, and it is the sixth and seventh proofs of the standing rule.** [MOO-191](/MOO/issues/MOO-191) commissioned this edit as **store revision 63**. That label was taken at 17:44 UTC on 12 October 2026 by the §7 item 124 cut (§0.0-63), and **store revision 64** was taken at 17:53 UTC by the record-only revision that opened §7 item 131 (§0.0-64) — so **this is store revision 65**, and nothing of store revision 63 or 64 is reverted, renumbered or re-cut. **Store revision 64 changed no word of either section, so it wrote no sweep and no drafter's note of its own**; §5.43 and §6.0-65 are this revision's, and the gap at §6.0-64 is that revision's silence and not a lost note.

**The Director's expected movement was taken on store revision 62** (document revision `a83b7396`): document 306,404 → 306,541, §1 120,888 → 121,025. The base has moved three times since, and **§1 has not moved since store revision 63**. **Re-resolved at the moment of application on store revision 64 (document revision 81, `d552f1ed`):** FIND **1** in the whole document and **1** in §1, and the movement is the Director's figure to the character — **the body the substitution was applied to goes 324,617 → 324,754 and §1 goes 121,439 → 121,576, +137 on both**. The document as written is longer than that by this revision's own §0, §5, §6 and §8 record and by nothing else. `<u>` / `</u>` balance **60 / 60 in §1, unchanged**, which is what the Director's figure asserts. **No markup of any kind is added to §1.** The document's balance stays equal and its raw count goes **61 / 61 to 63 / 63**: this narrative's block quote of the enacted words is one added pair, and the mention of the two tags in this very sentence is the other, which is how §0.0-63's quote accounts for the pair before it.

**Two record defects of store revision 64 are corrected here, and both are labels.** Store revision 64 bumped the document title and left the byline reading *"store revision 63"*, and its title carried the words *"record only"*, which this revision inherited and which are untrue of it — **document revision 82 carried this body and document revision 83 corrected the title, with no change of a character of the body.** The byline is corrected to this revision in the ordinary way; the same defect was corrected once before, for store revisions 48 and 49, and the note at the head of this document records it.

**What this revision does not do.** **Subsection E1 is not re-cut:** store revision 62's E1 is accepted at MOO-188 §2 and is under the counsel gate at [MOO-192](/MOO/issues/MOO-192), and its condition remains a condition on the member's own retirement status and not on any act of filing, notification, acceptance or Board receipt. **The [MOO-141](/MOO/issues/MOO-141) patch is not applied and [MOO-176](/MOO/issues/MOO-176) revision 6's three FIND strings are not touched** — all three, and all six [MOO-164](/MOO/issues/MOO-164) anchors, were re-counted over §1 **after** the substitution and each resolves exactly once. **The other six answers at §7 item 129 — (1), (2), (3), (5), (6) and (7) — are not answered here**, four of them decide who bears a loss, and they stay open on MOO-97's card.

### 0.0-64 Store revision 64 — record only. No bill text changed; one defect in store revision 63's two sentences recorded as §7 item 131

**Not a drafting revision.** No word of either section moves, no enactment clause, no title, no figure, no designator. The whole of it is three record edits, made because re-reading store revision 63's insertion in its live context turned up a defect in it that no review has raised:

- **§7 item 131 is new** in [`bill-text-s8-item-list`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-item-list) — subdivision M 2's Article 5 grant is keyed to *"such case decision"*, whose nearest antecedent is the completion sentence, and the enacted characterisation is bounded *"for the purposes of this subsection"* while § 2.2-4026 sits outside the subsection. **Each reading is survivable alone and the pair closes the route into court the cure exists to open.** Two one-phrase repairs drafted; **neither enacted**, because the limiting phrase is in the words the board approved verbatim.
- **§0.0-63 repair 1 is qualified in place** — the express characterisation was the right repair and is not withdrawn, but it must not be read as a warranty that the route into court is open.
- **§6.0-63 gains item 7**, recording the defect as mine and naming it to [MOO-190](/MOO/issues/MOO-190) as the first thing to break.

**Verification of store revision 63 itself, performed in this heartbeat against the live body** (document revision `37a5426c`, 320,665 characters, §1 at offsets 73,074–194,513): each of the three operative strings of the insertion occurs **exactly once in §1**, immediately after *"The Board shall act upon a request for reconsideration within 90 days."* The three endpoints keyed to the Board's act — the tolling of the member's final qualifying retirement date, clause (ii)'s exclusion from the 120-day aggregate, and the subdivision D 4 / D 5 filing-date retention — are each unchanged and each still keyed to *"the Board acts upon the request"*, so **§7 item 130's residual is live and neither cured nor worsened**, exactly as store revision 63 reported.

### 0.0-63 Store revision 63 — §7 item 124 answered by the board as option (d) and enacted. Two sentences inserted in subdivision M 2; the no-decision residual is closed and [MOO-141](/MOO/issues/MOO-141)'s gate 2 is cured

**One insertion in one provision of §1 — subdivision M 2 of § 51.1-142.4. No figure moves, no designator is renumbered, no section joins or leaves the act, no defined term is added, not one word of existing text is struck or reworded, and the title line does not change.** Sweep **§5.42**; drafter's note **§6.0-63**.

**What was decided and by whom.** Interaction `ff469794` on this issue, answered by the board at 17:38 UTC on 12 October 2026: **option (d)**, the member-triggered denial — against the carded (b), which was this item's own recommendation until the addendum of the same day. Occasioned by the Policy Director's comment of 17:28 making §7 item 124 a first-class blocker edge on [MOO-141](/MOO/issues/MOO-141), its **gate 2**.

**The residual it closes.** Store revision 59 enacted §7 item 88's option (d) together with the answer to item 110, and the two in combination closed both escapes: M 2's last sentence disapplies Articles 3 (§ 2.2-4018 et seq.) and 4 (§ 2.2-4024) of the Administrative Process Act, and M 3 disapplies the deemed-in-favour sentences of § 2.2-4021 B and C. So a reconsideration the Board simply never completed produced **no case decision at all**, and § 2.2-4026 A gives the court action only to a party aggrieved by one. **The member had no route into court, by two express exclusions rather than by argument.** Each of those three provisions was re-counted in §1 of the live body before any substitution: one occurrence each.

**The enacted words**, inserted immediately after M 2's sentence *"The Board shall act upon a request for reconsideration within 90 days."* — one occurrence in §1, asserted before the substitution. The whole of § 51.1-142.4 is new language and subdivision M 2 is a single underlined paragraph, so the insertion carries no markup of its own:

> <u>Where the Board does not act upon a request for reconsideration within the 90 days allowed by this subsection, the person who filed the request may file with the Board written notice that the period has expired. Where the Board does not act upon the request within 30 days after that notice is filed, the request is denied on the first day after the expiration of those 30 days, and that denial constitutes the final determination of the Board upon the reconsideration and a case decision as defined in § 2.2-4001 for the purposes of this subsection.</u>

**Three repairs are built into the enacted words, and two of them are against this document's own card.**

1. **The denial is characterised as a case decision expressly, because the carded sentence did not reach the sentence it relied on.** Item 124 said the cure *"uses the section's own vocabulary … so it needs no further words to become a case decision."* That was wrong. M 2's existing sentence is keyed twice to events a deemed denial does not produce — *"**Upon completion** of a reconsideration under this subsection, the final determination **issued by** the Board shall constitute a case decision"* — and nothing is completed and nothing is issued. Unrepaired, the cure would have given the member a dated denial and still no route into court, which is the whole of what it exists to supply. **The express characterisation is safe here in a way it was not at item 88**, where it was refused because calling Board inaction a case decision opens § 2.2-4021's statutory default against limitations subdivision D 2 calls self-executing: **M 2's last sentence and M 3 now close that default by express text.** Nothing in the enacted words is deemed in the member's favour. **Qualified in this heartbeat by §7 item 131, and the qualification is against this paragraph.** The express characterisation is **necessary** and it may not be **sufficient**: M 2's Article 5 grant runs to a person aggrieved by *"such case decision"*, whose nearest antecedent is the **completion** sentence, and the enacted characterisation is itself limited *"for the purposes of this subsection"* while § 2.2-4026 sits outside the subsection. Each reading is survivable alone; the pair closes the route. **Two one-phrase repairs are drafted at §7 item 131 and neither is enacted**, because the limiting phrase is in the words the board approved verbatim. Nothing in this paragraph is withdrawn — the characterisation was the right repair to make — but it must not be read as a warranty that the route into court is open.
2. **The denial falls on the first day after the 90 days expire, which is the Policy Director's *"91st day"* and not the carded *"last day of that period."*** On the carded words the denial fell on day 90, a day on which the Board may still lawfully act, so the section would have carried a denial and a live power to act on one date and two inconsistent determinations could have existed on the same day. Cost of the correction: one day.
3. **The silence is not cured and is disclosed rather than netted away.** A denial by operation of this sentence carries no writing, no reasons and no notice, where a **completed** reconsideration is *"written reconsideration by the Board stating the reasons for its decision"* and M 2 keys the member's own 30-day window to *"after the notice of the determination is given."* Option (d) answers it as far as drafting can — **the member creates the date, so she knows it** — and that is the whole reason the board's answer is the better one. What remains unverified is whether an Article 5 period could run from a silent decision at all: **§ 2.2-4026 does not state the period** (subsection A commits it to the Rules of the Supreme Court of Virginia; subsection C fixes an appeal date only for regulations, verified at source on 12 October 2026), **and Rule 2A:2 has not been retrieved — one source returned 403 and one host is blocked.** [MOO-186](/MOO/issues/MOO-186) asks Statutory Researcher. It does not bear on the enacted words, because on option (d) the date is the member's own and she has notice of it by construction.

**What I did not decide, and it is new §7 item 130.** The enacted sentence does **not** deem the denial to be the Board *"acting upon the request"*, and I will not make it do so by drafting, because that verb is the subject of [MOO-164](/MOO/issues/MOO-164). The consequence is stated in §5.42 and is real: on the text as it now stands a Board that never acts leaves **the member's tolling** and **the subdivision D 4 / D 5 filing-date retention** running without end, because both endpoints are the Board's act and M 2 clause (ii) expressly excludes from its 120-day aggregate *"any period after the expiration of the 90 days allowed by this subsection and before the Board acts upon the request."* **Store revision 62 enacted the cure for the mirror-image exposure in subdivision G 4** — *"a failure of the Board to act upon such a request within the period allowed to it for acting upon the request shall not extend that aggregate"* — so the precedent for what M 2 clause (ii) could say is already inside this bill. It is [MOO-97](/MOO/issues/MOO-97)'s uncapped-tolling residual and MOO-164's verb, and this revision neither cures nor worsens it.

**The base moved under this revision while it was being cut, and it is the fifth proof of the standing rule.** The read that produced the §7 item 124 addendum was at **271,611** characters (store revision 61); the write was made on **306,404** characters (store revision 62, document revision `a83b7396`), which amended **M 2's own first sentence**. **The anchor resolved exactly once in both bodies.** An anchor that still resolves is not evidence that the text around it is current — so the whole of M 2, M 3 and the three load-bearing provisions were re-read in the new body before the substitution. **Rebased; nothing of store revision 62 is reverted or renumbered.**

### 0.0-62 Store revision 62 — [MOO-169](/MOO/issues/MOO-169)'s counsel pass disposed and the Policy Director's seven answers of 12 October 2026 enacted. Twenty-one substitutions in five provisions of § 51.1-142.4

**Cut and applied on [MOO-97](/MOO/issues/MOO-97). Record: [`moo169-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-97#document-moo169-disposition).** Sweep §5.41; drafter's note §6.0-62; the carded answers §7 item 129. **No figure moves, no designator is renumbered, no section joins or leaves the act, no defined term is added, and the title line does not change.** §1 goes from 117,980 to 120,888 characters and **five lines of §1 differ** — subdivision D 1, subdivision G 4, subsection L, subdivision M 2 and subsection N.

**The fatal finding, cured by taking the window out again.** [MOO-169](/MOO/issues/MOO-169) F1 held that store revision 61's confinement of the death-benefit sentence to the notification window in subdivision D 1 *moved* MOO-161 F1's injury rather than curing it: a member who is out of service after an involuntary separation, whom subdivision D 1 expressly tells she need not file her § 51.1-153 notice early, and who dies before filing, fell outside the window — so the in-service waiver never reached her, § 51.1-162 B was unavailable, and § 51.1-162 A paid her survivor a refund of contributions while the Commonwealth settled and paid the certified subsidy on the date of her death. **The window is deleted and the sentence is conditioned instead on the member not having retired before the date of death**, which is the carve-out clause (i) already uses in its own second sentence. That restores the scope of answer **(f)** as the Director approved it on 12 October 2026 and as it stood at store revisions 54, 58 and 60; **the confinement was this drafter's, not the Director's**, and §6.0-62 item 1 says so. **F2 is cured by the same deletion** — there is no longer a cross-reference to a notification *"required by subdivision D 1"* for the Board to read narrowly.

**Three statements of law the act compels the Board to make before it may take a member's money were wrong, and are now right.** § 51.1-165 **C 2** does not provide a greater-of at large: it pays in accordance with § 51.1-163 and switches to § 51.1-162 B **only where the contingent annuitant and the § 51.1-162 B payee are the same person or persons**, and the certification's invented words *"each payable to the person entitled to it under the provision under which it is determined"* described a mechanism the Code does not have (F9). § 51.1-162 **B** does not pay on survival alone: it pays a **designated** spouse, minor child or parent, or, absent a designation, in the order of precedence it states — so a member who designated her sister in 2009 and is survived by her mother gets nothing from the purchase, and the certification told her otherwise (F10). § 51.1-162 **C** requires death **in service** (M6). § 51.1-162 **A**'s payee rule is now stated too (m3), and the displacement of § 51.1-165 C 1 and § 51.1-165.01 F is described as what it is — a displacement of their routing to § 51.1-163 only, leaving their first sentences, which void the member's option election, in force (M5).

**The remedy for an incomplete certification now works for the person most affected by it.** It reached only the six propositions, not the certification's earlier content sentence; it ran the member's clock from a death notice she will never receive; it named a category — *"a failure of the Board"* — that subdivision M 2 does not contain; and it collided with M 2's second sentence, which deems a certification a determination and had already closed her 30 days. **All four are cured in one substitution** (F5, m2): every matter the subdivision requires, the M 2 category named exactly, the member's period run from the certification she actually received, the death-case person's period left where it was, and M 2's second sentence expressly disapplied to such a failure. M 2's own third window — *"within the period provided by the provision that requires the matter to be stated"* — was **empty everywhere but subdivision D 1**, and now carries the chapter's own 30 days with a longer-period rider (F6).

**The reconsideration class is widened, with the boundary store revision 61 was reaching for.** Standing turned on the Board having already decided against the claimant, so the Board could defeat review by never deciding — and because M 2 makes the case decision the product of reconsideration, exclusion from reconsideration foreclosed the circuit court as well (F3). The same narrowing had stripped the **court-appointed conservator of a minor beneficiary** whom the same revision had made the addressee of the death notice (F4). Both of M 2's class lists now name *"person who **has** claimed any benefit payable on the death of a member"* — with no adverse-determination gate — and *"person to whom this section requires a notice to be given **in respect of a benefit payable on the death of a member**."* **This is not a reversal of store revision 61's reasoning**: what was wrong with the open formula was that it had no subject-matter boundary and so reached the public through subsection K; the new words carry one. §6.0-62 item 3.

**The $3,000,000 bar is made arithmetically honest in two of the three places the pass attacked.** A reinstated settled amount is now **charged against the limitations in subdivision D 2** notwithstanding that the amount available is thereby exceeded, so commitments can no longer stand at $3,148,000 under a bar subdivision D 2 calls self-executing (F8); the release carries a *"to the extent it has not already been released and not reinstated"* rider, so it cannot be required twice of the same amount (M3); and the stranded words *"together with any contingency amount released with it"*, which pointed at a release that never carries a contingency amount, are deleted (M2). **The third place is not cured and is the Director's**: subsection N fixes no time for payment while subdivision G 4 settles the subsidy on the date of death, so whether a subsidy that buys no benefit comes back to the cap is an unregulated administrative choice (F7, §7 item 129 answer 1).

**Two corrections to the pass itself, both stated at §6.0-62.** F8's fix direction asked for the general reinstatement sentence's *"charging rule and excess consequences"* to be adopted by reference. **Only the charging rule was missing.** Subdivision G 4's notice sentence already speaks of *"every reinstatement under this subdivision"*, so the notice to the Chairmen, the notice to every waiting-list applicant, the subdivision D 3 confirmation, the certification of the excess to the Governor, the subsection J line and *"shall accept no further election until the excess is eliminated"* already applied to a reinstatement under this release. Adopting them again by reference would have been a second formulation of one concept. And F1's fix direction offered *"the member dies before the effective date of any retirement of the member"*; that is **vacuous for the member who never retired at all**, who is the largest part of the class the finding is about, so the enacted words are the negative the subdivision already uses.

**The Director's seven, as enacted.** The greater-of's **measure** (actuarial equivalence as of the date of death, on the Board's assumptions then in use) and its **payee** (the person the provision producing the greater benefit requires to be paid) — f3-a and f3-b, cumulative, in one sentence. The **§ 51.1-162 C waiver period**, which the act's own clocks could destroy before the Board's notice of it arrived, now cannot expire before the thirtieth day after a late notice — f7-toll, **and it is the act's first operative override of a period stated in a section this act does not amend**. A **further notice with a fresh review period** where the Board later determines an amount differently — f14-supp. **Lateness reviewable for 30 days after the Board cures it** — m2-lateness. The express statement that **a § 51.1-162 death benefit is not charged against the subdivision D 2 limitations** — f11-disclose. And the **confinement of the Program to Chapter 1 members** — f10-b, enacted in subsection L **in the chapter form the definition of *"eligible member"* already uses**, not in the three-named-system form the card carried; see §6.0-62 item 12, because the scope decision the Director made was already the law of the act and the sentence is a restatement, parallel to subsection L's existing sentence on teachers.

**Store revision 62 is cut and applied on the live base at document revision `3efccfe2` (store revision 61).** Every one of the twenty-one FIND strings was asserted to occur exactly its expected number of times in §1 before any substitution — twenty of them once, the M 2 class list twice, once in each of its two lists. **Every citation figure in §5.41 is a count taken over §1 of the body that was written, not a prediction**; the rule that a predicted count is not a count was set at §5.37 and holds here.

### 0.0-61 Store revision 61 — [MOO-161](/MOO/issues/MOO-161)'s counsel pass disposed and the Policy Director's five answers enacted. Thirteen substitutions in subdivisions G 4, D 1 and M 2; the fatal finding cured by deleting four words and confining one sentence

**Thirteen substitutions in three provisions of §1 — subdivisions G 4, D 1 and M 2 of § 51.1-142.4. No figure moves, no designator is renumbered, no section is added or deleted, no defined term is added, and the title line does not change.** Five of the thirteen carry the Policy Director's answers of 12 October to §7 item 122; eight answer [MOO-161](/MOO/issues/MOO-161). The record proper is a companion document, [`moo161-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-97#document-moo161-disposition), on the convention store revisions 33, 36, 40, 41, 42, 44, 49, 55 and 58 set: every FIND/READ pair verbatim, subdivisions G 4, D 1 and M 2 reproduced in full as amended, both directions of the sweep, the ambiguities, and every answer drafted and not enacted.

**The fatal finding is cured by taking words out, not by adding them.** **F1**: store revision 58 replaced a self-limiting formulation (*"the Board's receipt of a written notification ... shall not cause § 51.1-163 to apply"*) with a freestanding *"§ 51.1-163 shall not apply to the member's death"*, governed only by *"where clause (i) of this subdivision applies."* Clause (i) is *"dies"*, and its only carve-out is a member who retired in a retirement that is **not** a qualifying retirement — so by negative implication the sentence reached **a member who retired in a qualifying retirement on a basic allowance and died years later as a retiree**, and switched off the one provision that returns her unrecovered contributions to her family. On the reviewer's facts a brother lost $122,000 and the trust fund kept it. **The act, whose case for existing is that forfeiting purchased military service is wrong, forfeited more than the defect it was curing.**

**Two edits, and both are subtractions.** The citation check establishes why: **§ 51.1-163 is captioned *"Death after retirement"* and opens *"If a member dies after the effective date of retirement"* — it cannot reach a pre-effective-date death of its own force at all.** It reaches one only by deeming, and there are exactly three deeming provisions (§ 51.1-165 C 1, § 51.1-165 C 2 and § 51.1-165.01 F), two of which this sentence's notwithstanding already displaces and the third of which it deliberately preserves. **So the disapplication was never load-bearing.** It is deleted, and the sentence is confined to the window in the words D 1's own certification already uses — *"after the Board has received the notification of the member's effective date of retirement required by subdivision D 1 and before that date."* That cures **F1** and, because § 51.1-163 is available again as a measure, **F2** — the greater-of that could not be performed because its input had been switched off.

**This restores the agreed scope; it does not choose a policy.** Every answer the Director has given on this branch — (d), (f), and the certification — speaks to the death of a member who has filed her § 51.1-153 notice and not yet reached her effective date. Reaching a death in retirement was a drafting artefact, not an agreed policy, and §7 item 128 offers the wider alternative if the Director wants it.

**Three propositions of law the certification compelled the Board to state were wrong, and the reviewer re-read the Code to prove it.** § 51.1-162 **C** pays a surviving spouse, **minor children and parents** — not a spouse alone (**F4**), so an unmarried member with a child was being told in an official certification that the work-related death allowance could not reach her child. § 51.1-162 **A**'s condition is *"if no benefits are payable under subsection B"* — subsection C is not mentioned (**F5**), so the act compelled the Board to certify the opposite of the statute and supplied a future plaintiff with his own best evidence. And subdivision C 2 of § 51.1-165 was described as entitling the contingent annuitant, when that is true of one of its two limbs only, and its in-service condition was suppressed (**F6**). **All three are now stated as the Code states them, by cross-reference rather than by paraphrase.**

**The incomplete certification had a remedy that could not work, and now has one that can.** Deeming the omission a *determination* (store revision 58's F7 cure) gave the review right to the only person who had received the certification — the member, who is not adversely affected while she is alive — and ran the clock from a document her survivors never saw. It is now a **failure** of the Board, M 2's failure limb is widened to reach a notice or certification that was given on time and left out a matter the section requires, and the period runs for 30 days from the G 4 death notice, which is the first writing the person adversely affected ever receives.

**The reconsideration class is narrowed because it had no boundary.** *"Other person to whom this section requires a notice to be given"* reached, on the face of the text, **the public**, because subsection K requires the Board to *"give public notice"* of a year in which it accepts no election; it also reached the two money-committee Chairmen, who receive information-only notices. The formula is replaced in both class lists by the two recipients the amendment was for — **the claimant the Board rejected** and **the employer that paid on the member's behalf** — both of which were the stated object of store revision 58's own cure, and both of which are now in the act expressly rather than by accident.

**The Policy Director's five answers.** A **30-day duty on the employing state agency** to tell the Board of the death, with a subsection J line — the crediting deadline runs from when the Board *"learns of"* the death and until now nothing required anyone to tell it. The **90-day redetermination** now runs, in the death case, from the date the Board learns of the death, so it can no longer expire before the Board can begin. The release of a settled amount now fires **where no allowance is payable at all**, not only where someone elects, and restores to the $3,000,000 limitation **only so much as has not already been paid under subsection N**. And a released amount is **reinstated** where the determination that released it is reversed on reconsideration or on judicial review.

**What is not here.** **F3** — what the greater-of is measured on and who is paid it — is on the Director's card, because every form of it moves money between a spouse, a sister, a designated beneficiary and the trust fund. **F7**, the § 51.1-162 C waiver period that can expire before the Board's compelled notice of it arrives, is the reviewer's highest-value repair after F1 and needs an override of another section. **F10**, whether a State Police, VaLORS or Judicial Retirement System member is in this Program at all, is a scope question for the Director and a research question for the Statutory Researcher. All of them, drafted, at §7 item 128 — which since store revision 59 lives in [`bill-text-s8-item-list`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-item-list), where a reference of the form *“§7 item n”* resolves.

### 0.0-60 Store revision 60 — the Director's eight answers of 12 October 2026 enacted in thirteen substitutions in five provisions

**[MOO-64](/MOO/issues/MOO-64)'s question card of 12 October 2026 is answered in full and every answer is taken as given:** item 114 **enact the single amendment to the settlement bar**; item 115 **enact**; item 116 **both limbs**; item 117 **both clauses**; item 118 **both**; item 119 **enact**; items 103 and 104 **both**; and of the cheap bundle, **120(a), 120(b) and 121's M 2 limb** — the interest limb was **not** selected and is not enacted. **Thirteen substitutions. Five provisions move: the definition of *"unreduced retirement eligibility date"* in subsection A, subsection E, subdivision G 4, subdivision J 1, and subdivision M 2.** No designator is renumbered, no section is added or deleted, no defined term is added, and the title line does not change. Record, with every FIND and ENACTED string verbatim: [`moo64-rev60-record`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-moo64-rev60-record). Sweep **§5.39**; drafter's note **§6.0-60**; the closures and two new items at **§7**.

**a. Item 114 — the settlement bar is amended once (G 4).** *"[R]eleased **in full** in accordance with subdivision D 7 **and has not been reinstated under this subdivision**"*, and *"has taken effect **and has not been rescinded, reversed, or modified**."* This cures the two routes that still reached the release limb — the member whose purchase is reduced on a **D 6 recertification** and who then dies before retiring, and the **involuntary-separation rescission**, which has no *notwithstanding* of its own — and it makes the two existing *notwithstanding* clauses surplusage rather than load-bearing. **It settles subsidies the bar on its face refused, so it spends money**, which is why it was carded and not fixed silently.

**b. Item 115 — the reversal sentence's settlement duty is conditioned (G 4).** The duty now runs *"where the determination as reversed or modified is such that the certified subsidy falls to be settled under subdivision 1 or under this subdivision."* The member who seeks reconsideration and **loses** no longer triggers a command to recertify and settle a subsidy subdivision 2 refuses.

**c. Item 116 — the arrears formula is net, not gross (G 4).** *"[E]very amount **by which the amounts paid to the member fall short of the amounts that** would have been payable"*, with the second limb *"or, where the determination is reversed in its entirety, had no determination been made."* On the review's worked facts the sentence commanded **$64,080** where **$8,280** was owed, and **G 4's own no-collection sentence** barred recovery of the difference.

**d. Item 117 — the carve-out reaches the timely filer (G 4).** Two clauses: an application *"the filing of which did not require a waiver of a ninety-day notification period set forth in subsection A or B of § 51.1-156"*, and the trigger anchored to *"a certification described in clause (ii) of subsection H of § 51.1-156"* in place of the counterfactual *"the Board was required … to waive."* **Class 3 — the discretionary waiver — remains excluded**, which is the line the Director drew on item 91 and re-made knowing class 4 existed.

**e. Item 118 — pendency is capped and the report has a long-stop (G 4, J 1).** The 120-day aggregate now counts *"applications for a disability retirement allowance filed after the first such application"*; and J 1 closes with *"In no event shall the Board be required to report under this subdivision after the first December 1 following the twentieth anniversary of the expiration of this section."*

**f. Item 119 — the residual temporal rule (definition of *"unreduced retirement eligibility date"*).** *"[O]r, where this sentence is applied otherwise than in the determination of the member's unreduced retirement eligibility date, on or before the member's effective date of retirement."* The member certified at 48 and involuntarily separated at 50 can now take the § 51.1-155.1 A retirement **G 4's own hardship branch requires of her**. This is also pass 17's M-4 cure: the same sentence previously yielded **no date at all** in a G 1 settlement or a qualifying-retirement determination.

**g. Items 103 and 104 — a route and two report lines (G 4, J 1).** The member may now **file written evidence** that she was unable to retire by reason of an error of the Board, and the Board owes a determination with reasons **within 30 days**, reviewable under subsection M. J 1 gains the **reversals and modifications** line and **every amount paid or refunded** on account of a rescission, reversal or modification.

**h. The bundle — 120(a), 120(b) and 121 (subsection E, G 4, M 2).** Subsection E's crediting-bar exception now names *"the member's approval for a disability retirement allowance, or … an error of the Board"* alongside death. The **death branch's** refund is now paid *"to the member's estate and to any employer … and shall apply no amount of it to the purchase of service credit"* — it no longer buys service credit for a dead member. Subsection M 2's failure clause reaches a failure *"to make a payment"*, so five bare 60-day payment duties acquire an *"or else"*; **the interest limb was not selected and is not drafted into the text.**

**No figure moves, and nothing needs re-running.** Every figure in the package prices the admitted cohort — the unreduced subdivision A 1 cohort reached through § 51.1-153 A, B 2 or B 3 — **as settling**. **No admission test moves:** nothing in these thirteen substitutions touches *"eligible member"*, the subsection C clause (ii) cap, or the clauses (v) to (viii) determinations, and **item 119 can only ever admit a retirement route the section had disabled for a member already inside the program.** Items 114, 115, 116, 117 and 118 move **settlement and timing** — 114 and 117 toward the priced basis, 115 away from a payment the basis never assumed, 116 toward the correct quantum. Nothing in §3, `model-s8-s9` or the [MOO-85](/MOO/issues/MOO-85) reprice needs re-running. **Two of the answers spend money one-sidedly and the Director took them on that footing** (114 settles subsidies the bar refused; 116's net formula reduces an irrecoverable overpayment and so moves money **in the Commonwealth's favour**); §7 item 99's quantification is with [@Fiscal & Impact Modeler](agent://428b53bf-f49e-4505-a607-3560a26b3d50) and no figure is asserted here.

**What is NOT done, and it is the next round.** **[MOO-156](/MOO/issues/MOO-156)'s pass 18 — 6 serious, 11 moderate, 3 minor on store revision 57's six repairs — is NOT dispositioned in this revision.** One of its findings is cured here as a by-product (M18-3, the death refund's displaced deadline, by item 120(b)'s conjunctive form) and one defeats the stated premise of an answered item (S18-6, below). **The remaining eighteen findings are read, not answered**, and they are the work of the next cut. Nothing in this revision claims otherwise.

**One answer rests on a premise pass 18 falsifies, and the repair is enacted anyway.** Item 120(a) was carded on the ground that *"G 4 credits the whole purchase on clauses (ii) and (iii) too."* **It does not.** G 4's crediting duties are confined to **death** and to the **rescission** branch; for a member to whom clause (ii) or (iii) applies with **no** prior subdivision 2 or 3 determination, G 4 imposes **no crediting duty at all**, and clause (ii) switches off G 3's partial-crediting duty with subdivisions 2 and 3. The E amendment is enacted because it is right on its own terms — E's death-only exception conflicted on its face with G 4's unqualified *"Subsection E does not prevent the crediting of service credit required by this subdivision"* — **but it lifts a bar in front of a duty that does not exist, and it does not do what the card said it would.** The missing crediting duty is **§7 item 126**, drafted in full and **enacted nowhere**; it decides what a partial payer is credited with, which is a money question.

**Numbering, and the file changed shape under this cut.** This is **store revision 60, not 59**, and the two new items are **126 and 127, not 123 and 124**. While these thirteen substitutions were being drafted a concurrent run published **store revision 59**, which answered §7 items 88 and 108 to 113, opened items 123, 124 and 125, and — on the Director's answer to item 112 — **moved §7's whole item list out of this document** to [`bill-text-s8-item-list`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-item-list). Two consequences. **The closures for items 114 to 121, 103 and 104, and the two new items, are in that document and not in this one**; §7 here is a pointer. And **item 113's enactment changed one of this revision's own anchors**: J 1's further-report sentence now has an independent trigger — *"or where any matter this section requires the Board to state in the report required by this subdivision arises after the Board has made that report"* — so item 118(b)'s long-stop was re-anchored to the amended sentence before it was applied, and it is **more** necessary than it was, not less, because the further report can now be triggered by any matter whatever. **Store revision 59 touched none of the other twelve anchors, and nothing in it is reversed, re-opened or renumbered by this revision.**

**Housekeeping — the rotation, and it restores two retention rules the file had let slip.** The §7 split leaves this file with about **275,000 characters of headroom**, so **none of this rotation was done for room.** It was done because §0.1 and §8.1 state retention rules the file had drifted from, and because these blocks had already been copied out to their retained-record documents when the split landed, so leaving them here would have left two copies of each. **Moved verbatim**, each behind a stub restating its durable result:

- to [`bill-text-s8-record-s0-s5-s6-rev2-51`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-record-s0-s5-s6-rev2-51) — **store revision 57's §0.0-57, §5.36 and §6.0-57; store revision 56's §0.0-56; and store revision 58's §5.37 and §6.0-58.** §0.1 provides that §5 and §6 carry **the live revision alone**, and three live sets apiece was the drift; **§5 and §6 now carry this revision and store revision 59** — one set more than §0.1's rule, for the same reason, and the file has the room;
- to [`bill-text-s8-revision-history-2`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-revision-history-2) — the **§8 status notes for store revisions 54, 53 and 52**, on the retention §8.1 states: *"§8 retains the current note and the four before it."* §8 now carries store revisions 60, 59, 58, 57, 56 and 55 — one more than that retention, because store revision 59's own note landed under this cut and moving a note a concurrent run has just written is not a tidy-up.

**Every one of those designators still resolves**, with every heading, blockquote, figure and caveat preserved. **Nothing is summarised away and no bill text moved. §1 is untouched by any of it.** **§7 item 102 is relieved, and not by me:** store revision 59's split took the item list out of this file, and §7 item 112's minimum ask — that the bill text and the item list be separate documents — is met.

### 0.0-59 Store revision 59 — the Policy Director's seven answers of 12 October 2026 enacted. Five provisions amended, one new subdivision, one answer declined, the §7 item list split out, and two consequences of the answers carded and not drafted in

**Seven answers, five enactments, one refusal, one document split, and three new §7 items. No figure moves. No designator is renumbered. No section joins or leaves the act and the title line does not change.** The answers were given at 13:26 UTC on 12 October 2026 on [MOO-145](/MOO/issues/MOO-145), on the seven-question card store revision 56 raised. Dispositions are in the item list, [`bill-text-s8-item-list`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-item-list), at items 88, 108, 109, 110, 111, 112 and 113. Sweep **§5.38**; ambiguities **§6.0-59**; banner **§8**.

**a. The two clocks, item 108 option (b) — the reason this revision matters most.** Subdivision D 3 takes a new second sentence requiring the Board to **file** the Director of Planning and Budget's confirmation request within 15 days after the application is completed; subdivision D 1 takes a new third sentence requiring the Board to **give** the certification within 15 days after that confirmation is obtained. Both verbatim from [`moo153-counsel-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-145#document-moo153-counsel-disposition) §4. **Before them the Board could satisfy every period § 51.1-142.4 contained and still strand an eligible member for ever**, because subdivision D 1 bars acceptance until the certification issues, nothing required the certification to issue, and subdivision D 3's own 15-day and 90-day clocks both ran from a request the Board had first to choose to file. **The *"or else"* is borrowed, not written:** each new duty is now a failure *"to make a determination or to give a notice within a period required by this section"*, which subdivision M 2's second limb already reaches, and a reportable failure under subsection C's reporting sentence and subdivision J 1. On the worst case the existing 90-day applicant notice now fires at about **day 105** instead of never, which is what makes the stranding at item 24 visible.

**b. The report duty reaches a failure to state, item 109 option (b).** Subsection C's reporting sentence — its **eighth**, and the item said seventh, which is corrected in the item list — now reads *"every failure to make a determination, **to state a matter this subsection requires it to state,** or to give a notice."* Not added to subdivision M 2: M 2's second sentence already deems a *certification* a determination, so a certification that issues and omits a required statement was already reviewable. **Subsection C still has nine sentences**, counted on the amended text.

**c. Item 88 is answered (d) WITH the optional second limb, over my withdrawn recommendation, and I record the disagreement rather than bury it.** Subdivision M 2's final sentence becomes three: the completed reconsideration *"shall constitute a case decision as defined in § 2.2-4001"*; the aggrieved person *"shall have a right to seek judicial review thereof in accordance with Article 5 (§ 2.2-4025 et seq.)"*; and *"Articles 3 (§ 2.2-4018 et seq.) and 4 (§ 2.2-4024) of the Administrative Process Act shall not apply to a reconsideration under this subsection."* **This answers pass 15's S-3 at the root** — § 2.2-4026 A requires a case decision and the act now supplies one by statute instead of assuming it in a savings clause. **What the limb costs is stated and not netted away: it removes § 2.2-4019 informal fact finding, § 2.2-4020 formal hearings and Article 4's hearing officer from the reconsideration.** Counsel objected that this strips the member of hearing rights the bill exists to confer; I accepted that objection in store revision 56 and withdrew my recommendation; **the Director's direction overrides it, which is the Director's to do, and M 2's own floor — a 30-day window, written reasons, a 90-day duty to act, and tolling — is what remains.**

**d. One conforming change to option (d)'s drafted words, and it was not optional.** (d) was drafted in store revision 53 and its class read *"Any member, retiree, beneficiary, or applicant."* **Store revision 58 then widened M 2's class list to six in both of its occurrences, and one of the two was the judicial-review sentence** — §5.37 records it. Enacting (d) verbatim would have taken the Article 5 right away from the rejected claimant and the subdivision B 4 e employer one revision after store revision 58 gave it to them, which is a policy change nobody directed and a silent reversal of another revision's cure. **The class enacted is M 2's own six-class list**, and *"Any member"* becomes *"A member"* to match M 2's first sentence. §6.0-59 ambiguity 1.

**e. The § 2.2-4021 B carve-out, item 110 option (a), as a new subdivision M 3** — verbatim, and the only addition of a designator in this revision. § 2.2-4021 was read at source on 12 October 2026: it sits in **Article 3 (Case Decisions)**, subsection B carries the 90-day/30-day deemed-decision default, subsection C the corresponding one after a hearing officer's recommendation, and the General Assembly wrote its own environmental exceptions into both — which is the authority for the form. Agency silence therefore cannot award a member a certified subsidy against the $170,000 or the $3,000,000 that the Board never confirmed and the Director of Planning and Budget never approved.

**f. Item 111 is declined and subsection C keeps both boundary sentences.** The zero bar stays on the record side. **Counsel's M-2 therefore stands on the record as a known, accepted cost** and I am not reopening it: the bar still speaks to the *entry* rather than to the *determination*, and a reader has to carry the seventh sentence to get the right answer. The declined text stays drafted at [`moo153-counsel-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-145#document-moo153-counsel-disposition) §4.

**g. Subdivision J 1's post-terminal further report gets an independent trigger, item 113 option (b)** — so a matter arising after the last annual report has somewhere to go even where no rescission under subdivision G 4 occurs, the rescission having been the only trigger. **Two deliberate departures from the §4 draft, neither a change of substance:** the draft's *"following **it**"* is replaced by the named antecedent, because a pronoun with two candidates inside a deadline is the kind of thing this file has had rot before; and *"in that report"* is given its full referent, *"in the report required by this subdivision."* **This is the first time I have touched subdivision J 1**, and it is on direction. [MOO-152](/MOO/issues/MOO-152) owns store revision 52 and is closed.

**h. Item 112 is answered yes and the §7 item list is now a separate document.** Items 1 to 125, the preamble and the §7.1 stub are moved **verbatim** to [`bill-text-s8-item-list`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-item-list); §7 of this document is a pointer stub and **§7 item n** still resolves. **This document falls from 510,963 characters to 249,041** — about 112,000 of which is §1 — **and the headroom problem that forced seven record moves since store revision 33 is over**: the file now has roughly 275,000 characters of headroom against the store's 524,288 ceiling, so nothing was moved out of §0, §5 or §6 in this revision and nothing needed to be. **The split is a document boundary and not a containment control**, and I will not describe it as one; both documents are internal issue documents on [MOO-42](/MOO/issues/MOO-42). What it buys is that a release of the bill text no longer carries 306,000 characters of unresolved internal question by accident. **One housekeeping repair came with it:** items 114 to 121 had been written *after* the §7.1 retained-record heading in store revision 57, so eight live items sat inside a stub that says its content was moved out; the heading now sits after item 125 and the numbered items run 1 to 125 in order. **No item's text changed in the reordering and the reassembled file is the same length to the character**, which is how it was verified.

**i. Two consequences of the Director's own answers are carded and NOT drafted in, because neither was agreed.** **Item 124 is the one that matters.** Article 3 contains § 2.2-4021, so item 88's limb disapplies it to a reconsideration, and subdivision M 3 disapplies its deemed-decision sentences again in terms — **so a reconsideration the Board simply never completes now produces no case decision at all, by two express exclusions, and § 2.2-4026 A gives the court action only to a party aggrieved by one.** Before this revision that was *uncertain*; it is now settled against the member. Item 124 drafts the cure — a deemed **denial** on the 91st day, which creates a reviewable decision and not a favourable one, so it does none of the work M 3 exists to prevent — and recommends it. **Item 123** is the hygiene point: M 3's reconsideration limb is now redundant with the limb, I kept it, and the three reasons are there. **Item 125** asks what happens to the item list on release, which is the Director's question and not mine.

**j. Record corrections — five, all ordinals, and all of the same class.** Three statements said *"D 1's third sentence"* of the sentence beginning *"The Board's certification shall state the matters required by subdivision B 4 c"* — in §5.37, in §6.0-58 and in an item — and edit **b** makes that sentence D 1's fourth. **The ordinal is removed and replaced with the sentence's opening words rather than renumbered**, which is the only form that survives the next insertion. Two more called subsection C's reporting sentence its *"seventh"* when it is its eighth. **One of the three was wrong before this revision too** — the item said *"third"* of a sentence that was then D 1's fourth and is now its fifth.

### 0.0-58 Store revision 58 — [MOO-154](/MOO/issues/MOO-154)'s counsel pass disposed. Eight substitutions in subdivisions G 4, D 1 and M 2; the fatal finding cured; nine answers drafted and none enacted

**Eight substitutions in three provisions of §1 — subdivisions G 4, D 1 and M 2 of § 51.1-142.4 — answering eleven of [MOO-154](/MOO/issues/MOO-154)'s sixteen findings. No figure moves, no designator is renumbered, no section is added or deleted, no defined term is added, and the title line does not change.** The record proper is a companion document — [`moo154-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-97#document-moo154-disposition) — on the convention store revisions 33, 36, 40, 41, 42, 44, 49 and 55 set. It carries every FIND/READ pair verbatim, subdivisions G 4, D 1 and M 2 reproduced in full as amended, both directions of the cross-reference sweep, the ambiguities, and all nine drafted answers word for word.

**The fatal finding is cured by naming one more provision.** **F1**: (f)'s *"Notwithstanding subdivision C 1 of § 51.1-165"* did not reach **subsection F of § 51.1-165.01**, which independently routes a post-notification, pre-effective-date death to § 51.1-163 — so for the partial-lump-sum electorate, the most common thing a departing state employee does, the act did nothing except compel the Board to tell her the opposite. The notwithstanding now names it, **and names only so much of it as requires payment under § 51.1-163**, so § 51.1-165.01 F's own disapplication of the in-service requirement survives and works for the member rather than against her.

**The conflict with § 51.1-165 C 2 is resolved by preserving C 2, not by displacing it.** C 2 is a *saving* provision: it gives a spouse, minor child or parent named under a joint and last-survivor option the **greater** of § 51.1-163 and § 51.1-162 B. Displacing it would have taken an existing right from a named class to make the act tidier. The enacted sentence instead pays the greater of the two, so no member is worse off than under present law. **The displacement alternative is drafted at §7 item 122 and is not enacted**, because it decides who bears a loss.

**Three more cures are about words that were not true.** The certification compelled the Board to state four propositions of law and **three were wrong**: all of subsection C of § 51.1-165 displaced when one subdivision is (F6(a)); § 51.1-162 B's relation test stated as its only condition when it is also conditioned on § 51.1-162 C (F6(b)); and *"in every other case subsection A"* when a death compensable under the Virginia Workers' Compensation Act is governed by § 51.1-162 **C** (F6(c)). The Board may not be put under a statutory duty to misstate the law to a member about to file.

**And one premise of my own was wrong.** The issue reasoned that D 1's day-for-day extension carried the consequence of an incomplete certification. It does not — that extension attaches to the statement of qualifying retirement dates required by the definition in subsection A, not to the Board's certification (F7). The duty had **no** consequence at all. It now has the two the section uses everywhere else: a determination for the purposes of subsection M, and a line in the subsection J report.

**What this revision does not do.** **F5 — the unreduced-retirement-eligibility bar in the second limb of subsection E — is not closed**, and it is the ERF residual this issue was opened on. The Commonwealth prices the subsidy on an **unreduced** retirement and subsection E's second limb may leave the survivor the **reduced** allowance. Closing it costs money and the figure is Fiscal's, so it is drafted two ways at §7 item 122 and enacted neither way. **F9, F10, F12's alternative, F13(c) and (e), and F15's payment side are likewise drafted and not enacted**, each for the same reason: each moves a loss between the survivor, the trust fund, the General Fund and the employing agency.

**Internal working draft. Not a Division of Legislative Services bill. Not reviewed by DLS. Pre-decisional.**

**Moved verbatim** to [`bill-text-s8-record-s0-s5-s6-rev2-51`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-record-s0-s5-s6-rev2-51), under the heading *"Moved in store revision 60"*, on the rotation §0.1 sets. **Nothing is lost, nothing is summarised away and no bill text moved.** Its durable result: [MOO-152](/MOO/issues/MOO-152)'s pass 17 dispositioned in six substitutions in subdivisions G 3 and G 4 — a payee for the subdivision-3 rescission refund where the trigger is death, a reinstatement deadline that is not already past, the two arrears fallbacks subdivision 2 already had, item 96 made operative, and the authorised offset made reciprocal and stated as a power — with its eight serious findings carded as §7 items 114 to 121, all eight of which are answered and enacted in store revision 60. Record: [`moo152-pass17-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-moo152-pass17-disposition).

**Moved verbatim** to [`bill-text-s8-record-s0-s5-s6-rev2-51`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-record-s0-s5-s6-rev2-51), under the heading *"Moved in store revision 57"*, on the rotation §0.1 sets. **Nothing is lost, nothing is summarised away and no bill text moved.** Its durable result: [MOO-131](/MOO/issues/MOO-131)'s conforming amendments drafted out in eight substitutions in six provisions — subdivision D 1 now **dates the determination it reports** (30 days from a completed application), M 2's filing-date retention is bounded by the Board's act, a one-sentence deeming makes thirteen classes of act and event reviewable *"for the purposes of this subsection,"* J 1 must report every failure to determine or to notify, enactment 3 clause (v) saves the failure limb as well as the determination, and D 4 states the mode of withdrawal. ### 0.0-54 Store revision 54 — performed; the §0 record is moved verbatim to the retained-record document in store revision 57

**Moved verbatim** to [`bill-text-s8-record-s0-s5-s6-rev2-51`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-record-s0-s5-s6-rev2-51), under the heading *"Moved in store revision 57"*, on the rotation §0.1 sets. **Nothing is lost, nothing is summarised away and no bill text moved.** Its durable result: the Policy Director's six answers of 12 October 2026 enacted in seven edits in subdivisions G 4, D 1 and M 2, with [MOO-126](/MOO/issues/MOO-126)'s F9 and F4 folded into the rebuilt death notice — a death in service stays a death in service, computed on the service credit credited under G 4, and the pre-filing disclosure states the content the act actually produces rather than the one the card named, which is §7 item 106.

**Moved verbatim** to [`bill-text-s8-record-s0-s5-s6-rev2-51`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-record-s0-s5-s6-rev2-51), under the predecessor rule, [MOO-153](/MOO/issues/MOO-153) having closed that pass. Nothing is lost and nothing is reworded.

### 0.0 Store revision 52 — performed; the §0 record is moved verbatim to the retained-record document in store revision 55

**Moved verbatim** to [`bill-text-s8-record-s0-s5-s6-rev2-51`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-record-s0-s5-s6-rev2-51), under the heading *"Moved in store revision 55"*, on the rotation store revision 52 itself set up at §0.1 on the Policy Director's answer to §7 item 53. **Nothing is lost, nothing is summarised away and no bill text moved.** Its durable result: [MOO-64](/MOO/issues/MOO-64)'s seven answers enacted in thirteen edits in four provisions, +3,337 characters, closing §7 items 89 to 97 and 99 — among them the **fatal** item 89, where G 4's settlement bar had a D 7 release limb the item-54 rescission did not disapply.

### 0.1 Where the retained record is

On the Director's answer to §7 item 53, **the whole retained §0, §5 and §6 record is moved out of this document.** `bill-text-s8` kept §1 bill text, §2, §3, §4, §7 and §8, plus this §0.0 narrative, §5's live sweep for the current revision and §6's live drafter's note for the current revision. **101,593 characters moved; nothing is lost and no bill text moved.** The move was necessary and not cosmetic: before it the file had **4,008 characters of headroom** against the store's 524,288-character ceiling, both earlier retained-record documents were effectively full, and the ~235-character fatal fix could be written but its record could not.

| Retained record | Document |
|---|---|
| §0 narrative, store revisions 2–33 | [`bill-text-s8-revision-history`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-revision-history) |
| §0 narrative, store revisions 34–49 | [`bill-text-s8-revision-history-2`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-revision-history-2) |
| §0 stubs and index, §0 for store revisions 49, 50 and 51, **§5.1–§5.30 and §6.0-18 to §6.0-50, §6.1–§6.4**, store revisions 2–50 | [`bill-text-s8-record-s0-s5-s6-rev2-51`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-record-s0-s5-s6-rev2-51) |
| **§7 — the whole item list, items 1 to 125, the preamble and the §7.1 stub**, moved in store revision 59 on the Director's answer to item 112. **§7 item n** resolves there. | [`bill-text-s8-item-list`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-item-list) |

**Store revision 60 moved six more blocks into the same document** — §0.0-57, §0.0-56, §5.36, §5.37, §6.0-57 and §6.0-58 — and the §8 status notes for store revisions 55, 54, 53 and 52 into [`bill-text-s8-revision-history-2`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-revision-history-2), each behind a stub restating its durable result. **A reference of the form §0.x, §5.n or §6.0-n for any revision up to 51 still resolves**, in the document named above, with every heading, table row and lettered label preserved. §5 and §6 in this document carry the **current** revision only; each future revision moves its predecessor's §5 and §6 to the same document and keeps the file bounded. **Store revision 59 ends the pressure that rule existed to relieve.** With §7 moved out this document is 249,041 characters against the store's 524,288-character ceiling — roughly **275,000 of headroom**, where every revision from 33 onward worked within a few thousand. **Nothing was moved out of §0, §5 or §6 in store revision 59 and nothing needed to be**, so §0 now holds four narratives and §5 and §6 two each. The retention rule still stands as the Director set it; it is simply no longer forced, and a revision that wants to keep more of its predecessors' record in place may now do so.

## 1. The bill

### Title

**A BILL to amend and reenact §§ 51.1-142.2 and 51.1-162 of the Code of Virginia and to amend the Code of Virginia by adding a section numbered 51.1-142.4, relating to the Virginia Retirement System; purchase of prior active duty military service credit by state employees; cost; pilot program; death benefits; designation of beneficiary; reports.**

### Enacting clause

**Be it enacted by the General Assembly of Virginia:**

**1. That §§ 51.1-142.2 and 51.1-162 of the Code of Virginia are amended and reenacted and that the Code of Virginia is amended by adding a section numbered 51.1-142.4 as follows:**

---

### § 51.1-142.2. Prior service or membership credit for certain members; service credit for accumulated sick leave.

**A.** Certain members may purchase credit for service as provided in this section.

**B. 1.** Any member in service may purchase service credit from the following categories of service or leave: (i) leave of absence for educational purposes that was previously approved by the member's employer, including unpaid educational leave as set forth in subsection D of § 22.1-296; (ii) leave of absence for a serious health condition of the member or of an immediate family member, all as defined in the Family and Medical Leave Act of 1993, 29 U.S.C. § 2601 et seq., as amended, and previously certified by the member's employer; (iii) up to one year of service credit per occurrence of leave for any unpaid leave of absence due to the birth, adoption, or death of a qualifying child, as defined in § 51.1-500; (iv) service as a full-time employee of another state, a public school system of another state, or a political subdivision of the Commonwealth or another state, as certified by such state, public school system, or political subdivision; (v) full-time service of a political subdivision of this state not credited to the member under an agreement as provided for in § 51.1-143.1, as certified by such political subdivision; (vi) full-time civilian service of the United States; (vii) full-time service at a private institution of higher education if the private institution is merged with a public institution of higher education and graduates of the private institution are then issued new degrees from the public institution; or (viii) any period of time when the member was employed part time or in a wage position by a participating employer and not otherwise eligible to participate in the retirement system because the member was not an employee as defined in § 51.1-124.3. However, no member in service shall be allowed to purchase more than a total of four years of service credit pursuant to this subdivision.

**2.** In addition to the service credit that may be purchased under subdivision 1, any member in service may purchase up to four years of service credit for prior active duty military service in the armed forces of the United States, provided that the discharge from a period of active duty status with the armed forces was not dishonorable. <u>The cost of service credit purchased under this subdivision by a member whose qualifying military service election has been accepted by the Board under § 51.1-142.4 shall be determined under subdivision 4.</u>

**3.** The service credit to be credited to a member under this subsection shall be calculated at the ratio of one year, or portion thereof, of service credit to one year, or portion thereof, of service purchased, except for employment service purchased under clause (viii) of subdivision 1, which shall be calculated at the ratio of one month of service credit for each 173 hours of service as certified by the employer.

<u>**4.** a. As used in this subdivision 4:</u>

<u>"Initial average compensation" means the annual average of the member's creditable compensation for the first 36 months of membership service earned by the member as a state employee, as determined by the Board from the records of the retirement system. If the member has earned fewer than 36 months of such service, "initial average compensation" means the annual average of the member's creditable compensation for all such service. Service credit purchased under this section, service credited under § 51.1-143.1, and service credited under subsection I shall be excluded in identifying such 36 months. If the records of the retirement system are insufficient to determine the member's creditable compensation for any month within such 36 months, the Board shall determine initial average compensation from the earliest 12 consecutive months of creditable compensation shown in those records, annualized, and the member may submit payroll records certified by the employer for the Board's consideration before the Board makes its determination.</u>

<u>"Limitation year" means the limitation year of the defined benefit plan under which the member is covered, determined under § 415 of the Internal Revenue Code and the regulations thereunder.</u>

<u>"Qualifying military service election" means an election to purchase service credit under subdivision 2 that the Board has accepted under § 51.1-142.4.</u>

<u>"Standard cost" means, for each year or portion thereof of service credit to be purchased, the amount the member would be required to pay for that year or portion thereof under this subsection if subdivision 4 did not apply.</u>

<u>"State agency" means a state agency as defined in subsection A of § 51.1-142.4.</u>

<u>"Statutory interest" means interest at the rate of six percent per annum, compounded annually.</u>

<u>"Total normal cost rate" means the rate, expressed as a percentage of creditable compensation for each year or portion thereof of service credit purchased, that the Board publishes and uses to determine the approximate normal cost under subdivision 3 for the retirement plan under which the member is covered. On July 1, 2026, that rate was 12.50 percent for a member who is not described in clause (i) or (ii) of subdivision A 1 of § 51.1-155 and 10.74 percent for a member who is so described.
