Where clause (iii) of this subdivision applies and, by the member’s final qualifying retirement date or, where the member has none, by the completion date stated in the certification, the member has neither died nor been approved for a disability retirement allowance nor retired, the certified subsidy for the election is settled on that date, and this subdivision and subsection N apply to that settlement as they apply to a settlement on a date stated in clause (a), (b), or (c) of this subdivision, except that the period within which the redetermination required by this subdivision must be made runs from the date on which the Board determines that clause (iii) of this subdivision applies or, where the Board does not make that determination within the period allowed to it by this subdivision, from the expiration of that period. The Board shall determine whether clause (iii) of this subdivision applies to an election no later than 60 days after the member's final qualifying retirement date or, where the member has none, after the completion date stated in the certification; shall give the member written notice of the determination and of the reasons for it no later than 15 days after making it; and shall state in the report required by subsection J every failure to make that determination, or to give that notice, within the period this subdivision allows for it. A determination under the preceding sentence is a determination of the Board for the purposes of subsection M. Where a determination under subdivision 2 or 3 has taken effect, a member may file with the Board written evidence that the member was unable to retire in a qualifying retirement by reason of an error of the Board. The Board shall determine whether the member was unable to retire in a qualifying retirement by reason of an error of the Board and shall give the member written notice of the determination and of the reasons for it no later than 30 days after the member files that evidence, and a determination under this sentence is a determination of the Board for the purposes of subsection M. No certified subsidy is settled under this subdivision after the reservation for the election has been released in full in accordance with subdivision D 7 and has not been reinstated under this subdivision, or after a determination under subdivision 2 or 3 has taken effect and has not been rescinded, reversed, or modified, and no certified subsidy is settled more than once under this subdivision or under subdivision 1. The Board shall not make a determination under subdivision 2, and shall not give the notice required by subdivision 2, while an application of the member for a disability retirement allowance under this chapter is pending, and a determination made in contravention of this sentence is of no effect. No period stated in subdivision 2 or in this subdivision, whether it is a period within which the Board is required to act or a period within which a determination is required to take effect, runs while such an application is pending or while a request the filing of which tolls the running of the member’s final qualifying retirement date under subsection M is pending, except that the aggregate of all such non-running by reason of such requests and by reason of applications for a disability retirement allowance filed after the first such application shall not exceed 120 days, and a failure of the Board to act upon such a request within the period allowed to it for acting upon the request shall not extend that aggregate. For the purposes of this subdivision an application for a disability retirement allowance is pending from the date on which the member files it until the date on which the Board first acts upon it, and such a request is pending from the date on which the member files it until the date on which the Board first acts upon it. Where a determination under subdivision 2 or 3 has taken effect and the member is thereafter approved for a disability retirement allowance under this chapter upon an application filed no later than the date on which the determination took effect, or upon an application the filing of which did not require a waiver of a ninety-day notification period set forth in subsection A or B of § 51.1-156, or upon an application as to which the Medical Board has made a certification described in clause (ii) of subsection H of § 51.1-156, or thereafter dies in a case to which clause (i) of this subdivision applies, or the Board thereafter determines that the member was unable to retire in a qualifying retirement by reason of an error of the Board, the Board shall rescind the determination and shall restore or credit service credit as this subdivision provides, no later than 60 days after the date on which the member is so approved, or dies, or the Board makes that determination, and the certified subsidy for the election is settled under this subdivision notwithstanding that the rescinded determination had taken effect, and notwithstanding that the reservation for the election has been released in accordance with subdivision D 7. A determination under subdivision 2 or 3 that has taken effect is not affected by an approval of the member for a disability retirement allowance upon an application filed after the date on which the determination took effect, except as this subdivision provides for the rescission of the determination. Where the rescinded determination is a determination under subdivision 3, the Board shall credit the member with the largest number of full or partial months of service credit for which full payment has been made, determined in the manner provided in subsection F of § 51.1-142.2 and without regard to the method by which payment was made, valued at the cost determined under subdivision B 4 of § 51.1-142.2, and shall recertify the certified subsidy to reflect the service credit so credited, and subdivision D 6 applies to that recertification only so far as it provides for the reservation of an increase; and shall refund, to the member or, where the member has died, to the member's estate, and to any state agency from whose appropriation an amount was paid on the member's behalf in proportion to the amounts each paid, any amount paid that exceeds the cost of the service credit so credited. The Board shall pay a refund required by the preceding sentence within 60 days after it credits the service credit, except that where the member has died the Board shall pay the whole of the refund to the member's estate and to any state agency from whose appropriation an amount was paid on the member's behalf in proportion to the amounts each paid and shall apply no amount of it to the purchase of service credit, and except that in every other case, to the extent the refund may not be paid without adverse federal tax consequence, the Board shall apply that amount to the purchase of additional service credit for the member at the standard cost. Where a determination under subdivision 2 was made or took effect before the Board learned of an involuntary separation of the member occurring on or before the member's final qualifying retirement date as it stood immediately before the determination was made or, if later, immediately before the determination took effect, the Board shall rescind the determination and shall restore the service credit reduced under it no later than 60 days after the date on which the Board learns of the separation, this subdivision applies to the election as though the determination had not been made, and the Board shall reinstate the reservation and any contingency amount released on account of the determination, in the manner this subdivision requires in the case of a reservation released otherwise than in accordance with subdivision D 7, no later than the date of the rescission, and the member’s final qualifying retirement date is the first day of the second month following the month in which the Board rescinds the determination. Where the Board rescinds a determination under subdivision 2 or 3 as this subdivision requires, the period within which this subdivision requires the redetermination to be made runs from the date on which the member is so approved, or dies, or the Board makes the determination stated in this subdivision that the member was unable to retire in a qualifying retirement by reason of an error of the Board. Where a determination under subdivision 2 or 3 that has taken effect is reversed or modified on reconsideration under subsection M or on judicial review, the Board shall restore or credit service credit as this subdivision provides, and, where the determination as reversed or modified is such that the certified subsidy falls to be settled under subdivision 1 or under this subdivision, shall recertify and settle it accordingly, no later than 60 days after the reversal or modification, and the next payment of the member's retirement allowance shall include every amount by which the amounts paid to the member fall short of the amounts that would have been payable to the member had the determination, as reversed or modified, taken effect on the date on which the determination took effect, or, where the determination is reversed in its entirety, had no determination been made. A certified subsidy settled as required by the preceding sentence is settled notwithstanding that the reversed or modified determination had taken effect and notwithstanding that the reservation for the election has been released in accordance with subdivision D 7. Where no payment of a retirement allowance has become payable to the member when the determination is reversed or modified, the Board shall include the amount that this subdivision requires to be included in the next payment of the member's retirement allowance on a reversal or modification in the first payment of a retirement allowance to the member; and where the member has died, the Board shall pay that amount to the person entitled to any benefit payable on the member's death or, if there is none, to the member's estate, no later than 60 days after the reversal or modification. The Board shall give the member, or the person entitled to any benefit payable on the member's death, written notice of a rescission under this subdivision, of the service credit restored, of the amount settled, and of the reasons for the rescission, no later than 30 days after the rescission, and that person may seek review of the rescission under subsection M. For the avoidance of doubt, an amount refunded under clause (ii) of subdivision 2 is not recoverable from any person; where a determination under subdivision 2 is rescinded under this subdivision the Board shall restore service credit to the extent of the amounts paid under the election and not refunded; and the member, or any state agency from whose appropriation an amount was paid on the member’s behalf, may pay to the Board the amount refunded no later than 90 days after the Board gives notice of the rescission, whereupon the Board shall restore the whole of the service credit reduced under the determination. For the purposes of the next sentence, a release that this section required or permitted the Board to make at the time it was made, including a release required by subdivision G 1, by this subdivision, or by subdivision D 3 upon the settlement of a certified subsidy, is a release in accordance with subdivision D 7. Where a reservation has been released otherwise than in accordance with subdivision D 7, or has been released in accordance with that subdivision where a determination is thereafter rescinded under this subdivision or is reversed or modified on reconsideration under subsection M or on judicial review, the Board shall reinstate it, together with the contingency amount released with it, no later than the date on which the certified subsidy is settled under this subdivision or, where no certified subsidy is settled under this subdivision, no later than 60 days after the rescission, reversal, or modification or, where the reservation was released otherwise than in accordance with subdivision D 7 and no determination is rescinded, reversed, or modified, no later than 60 days after the Board learns of the release, and the reinstated reservation and contingency amount shall be charged against the limitations in subdivision D 2 notwithstanding that the amount available to be reserved is thereby exceeded. The Board shall give written notice of every reinstatement under this subdivision, of the date of the release, of whether the release was in accordance with subdivision D 7, of the nature of any error, of the amount of any resulting excess, and of the cause of the release, to the Chairmen of the House Committee on Appropriations and the Senate Committee on Finance and Appropriations no later than 15 days after the reinstatement, which notice shall not identify any member; shall give written notice of the fact, the amount, and the date of any resulting excess, of the applicant's rights under subsection B of § 51.1-142.2, and of the standard cost, determined as of the date of the notice, of the service credit, if any, the applicant is eligible to purchase under subdivision B 2 of § 51.1-142.2 as of that date, to each applicant then on the waiting list maintained under subdivision D 5 no later than 15 days after the reinstatement, which notice shall not state the cause of the release and shall not identify any member; shall request a confirmation under subdivision D 3 in respect of the amount of any resulting excess no later than 15 days after the reinstatement, and shall certify the amount of the excess to the Governor and to those Chairmen as an amount necessary to fund the purposes of this section; shall state every reinstatement and every such excess in the report required by subsection J; and shall accept no further election until the excess is eliminated. For the purposes of this subdivision, an excess is eliminated when the aggregate of the amounts charged against the limitation in the first sentence of subdivision D 2 no longer exceeds that limitation. A reinstatement under this subdivision is a determination of the Board for the purposes of subsection M. A redetermination under this subdivision shall not create any obligation of the member, of any survivor or beneficiary of the member, or of any employer, shall not reduce the service credit credited to the member, and shall not affect any allowance or benefit payable to the member or to any survivor or beneficiary of the member. A transfer of the member's employment from one state agency to another state agency shall not affect an election. If the member incurs an involuntary separation after the Board accepts the election, subdivisions 2 and 3 shall not apply and the service credit purchased under the election shall vest at the cost determined under subdivision B 4 of § 51.1-142.2 if the member retires in a qualifying retirement no later than 90 days after the date of the involuntary separation, and where the ninetieth day is not the first day of a month the member may retire in a qualifying retirement with an effective date of retirement that is the first day of the month next following that day. A member who retires in a retirement that is not a qualifying retirement has not retired in a qualifying retirement for the purposes of the preceding sentence. Where the involuntary separation occurs on or before the date that would otherwise be the member's final qualifying retirement date and the first day of the month next following the ninetieth day after the date of the involuntary separation is later than that date, the member's final qualifying retirement date is that first day, provided that the Board has received a notice of the separation required of a state agency by this subdivision, has otherwise determined that an involuntary separation occurred, or has otherwise learned of the involuntary separation. Where the first day stated in the preceding sentence has passed when the condition stated in the proviso to that sentence is first satisfied, the member's final qualifying retirement date is, notwithstanding that sentence, the first day of the second month following the month in which that condition is first satisfied, and the member's retirement in a qualifying retirement on or before the date so determined satisfies the requirement of this subdivision that the member retire no later than 90 days after the date of the involuntary separation. A member's final qualifying retirement date shall not be extended under this subdivision by reason of more than one involuntary separation. If the member does not so retire, subdivision 2 shall apply, and the Board shall make the determination required by subdivision 2 within 60 days after the earlier of (a) the later of the last day on which the member may retire in a qualifying retirement under this subdivision and the member's final qualifying retirement date, if any, and (b) the date on which the member retires in a retirement that is not a qualifying retirement. The Board shall give the member written notice, no later than 15 days after the earlier of the date on which the Board receives the notice required by this subdivision and the date on which the Board otherwise learns of the involuntary separation, of the date by which the member must retire in a qualifying retirement under this subdivision and of the consequences under subdivision 2 of not retiring by that date. Notwithstanding the preceding sentence, where the Board learns of the involuntary separation by written evidence filed by the member under this subdivision, the notice required by that sentence shall be given no later than 15 days after the Board makes the determination required by this subdivision. A state agency shall notify the Board of the involuntary separation of any member whose election the Board has accepted no later than 15 days after the separation. A notice required of a state agency by this subdivision shall state the date of the involuntary separation and the cause of it and shall be certified by the agency head or his designee. A member may file with the Board written evidence of an involuntary separation of the member. The Board shall determine whether an involuntary separation occurred and shall give the member written notice of the determination, of the date of the separation as determined, and of the reasons for the determination, no later than 30 days after the member files that evidence; and a determination under this sentence is a determination of the Board for the purposes of subsection M. The Board shall state every failure of a state agency to give that notice of which the Board becomes aware in the report required by subsection J. No amount shall be collected, offset, or withheld from any member, retiree, survivor, beneficiary, or estate of a member on account of an involuntary separation, of a rescission or restoration under this subdivision, or of any other event to which this subdivision applies, and nothing in this subdivision creates any liability of the member, of any survivor or beneficiary of the member, or of the member's estate to the retirement system, to an employer, or to the Commonwealth. Notwithstanding the prohibition stated in this subdivision on the collection, offset, or withholding of any amount, and notwithstanding subsection F, the Board may apply a retirement allowance paid to the member for a month against a disability retirement allowance payable to the member for the same month, and may apply a disability retirement allowance paid to the member for a month against a retirement allowance payable to the member for the same month.</u>

<u>5. A member may cancel an election at any time before payment is completed by giving written notice to the Board. Upon cancellation the Board shall credit the member with the service credit for which full payment has been made, valued at the standard cost determined as of the date the Board accepted the election, shall refund any remaining amount paid, and shall release the reservation. The Board shall pay a refund required by this subdivision within 60 days after it receives the notice of cancellation, except that to the extent a refund may not be paid to the member without adverse federal tax consequence to the member, the Board shall apply that amount to the purchase of additional service credit for the member at the standard cost and shall notify the member in writing of the amount so applied.</u>

<u>**H.** A member whose election has been settled under subdivision G 1 or G 4, or who has been credited with service credit under subdivision G 2, G 3, or G 5, shall not make another election under this section. Nothing in this section limits a member's right to purchase service credit under subsection B of § 51.1-142.2 at the standard cost, to receive credit under subsection I of § 51.1-142.2, or to exercise any right under Chapter 1223 of Title 10 of the United States Code or under 38 U.S.C. § 4318.</u>

<u>**I. 1.** A member who retires in a qualifying retirement and who, within 12 months after the effective date of retirement, performs services for a state agency as an independent contractor, or as an employee or agent of a third party under a contract with a state agency, that are substantially the same as the duties the member performed before retirement shall be treated, for the duration of the performance of those services and solely for the purposes of the first sentence of subdivision B 1 of § 51.1-155, as in service as an employee in a position covered for retirement purposes under this chapter. For purposes of this subsection, services are substantially the same as the duties the member performed before retirement if they include a majority of the core responsibilities stated in the Employee Work Profile, or equivalent position description, for the position from which the member retired.</u>

<u>2. A state agency shall report to the Board, within 30 days after it begins, every engagement under which a person who retired in a qualifying retirement performs services described in subdivision 1. Upon receiving such a report, or otherwise learning of such an engagement, the Board shall give the retiree written notice stating the facts on which the Board relies and the Board's proposed determination, and the retiree shall have 30 days from that notice to respond in writing. The Board shall make a written determination stating its reasons within 30 days after the response period closes. The Board shall cease payment of the retirement allowance only from the first day of the month following the date of the determination and only for the duration of the engagement, and the retiree may seek review of the determination under subsection M.</u>

<u>3. A period during which a retiree is treated as in service under subdivision 1 shall not increase, decrease, or affect in any way the retiree's retirement benefits before, during, or after that period, no creditable service shall accrue to the retiree for that period, and no employer contributions under § 51.1-145 shall be payable for that period.</u>

<u>**J. 1.** The Board shall report, no later than December 1 of each year in which this section is in effect and no later than December 1 of each year thereafter until the first December 1 following the later of (a) the date on which every election the Board accepted under this section has been settled under subdivision G 1 or G 4, cancelled under subdivision D 6 or G 5, or determined under subdivision G 2 or G 3, or has had its reservation released under subdivision D 7 without any service credit having been credited to the member, and (b) the date of the last rescission under subdivision G 4 that has occurred on or before the date stated in clause (a), to the Governor, to the Director of the Department of Planning and Budget, and to the Chairmen of the House Committee on Appropriations and the Senate Committee on Finance and Appropriations. The report shall state, for the reporting period and cumulatively: the number of complete applications filed; the number of elections accepted, denied, and wait-listed; the amounts certified, reserved, settled, released, and paid; the number of members who retired in a qualifying retirement and the number who did not; the service credit purchased and the amounts paid by members and by state agencies; the number of determinations rescinded and the service credit restored under subdivision G 4; the number of determinations reversed or modified on reconsideration or on judicial review and the service credit restored or credited on account of them; every amount paid or refunded under subdivision G 4 on account of a rescission, reversal, or modification; the number of elections settled under subdivision G 4 by reason of an error of the Board and the amounts so settled; every amount by which a reservation reinstated under subdivision G 4 caused a limitation in subdivision D 2 to be exceeded; the variance between the certified subsidies and the actuarial cost to the retirement system as determined in the most recent actuarial valuation; every failure of the Board to make a determination, or to give a notice, within a period required by this section, stated separately for each provision; and, to the extent not otherwise required by this subdivision, every other matter this section requires the Board to state in that report. Where a rescission under subdivision G 4 occurs after the Board has made the last report required by this subdivision, or where any matter this section requires the Board to state in the report required by this subdivision arises after the Board has made that report, the Board shall make one further report, limited to that rescission or matter and its consequences, no later than the first December 1 following that rescission or the date on which that matter arises, as the case may be. In no event shall the Board be required to report under this subdivision after the first December 1 following the twentieth anniversary of the expiration of this section.</u>

<u>2. The Department of Human Resource Management and the Department of Accounts shall report to the Department of Planning and Budget, no later than September 1 of each year in which this section is in effect and each year thereafter until the Board has made the last report required by subdivision 1, the data in their possession relating to positions vacated by employees who retired in a qualifying retirement, including, for each such position, the salary of the employee on the effective date of retirement, whether and when the position was filled, the salary and benefit cost of any person filling it, the cost of any contract under which the duties of the position were performed, and the general fund and nongeneral fund shares of each such cost. The Director of the Department of Planning and Budget shall specify the form of the report and shall publish only aggregated data in a manner that does not identify any individual.</u>

<u>3. The Director of the Department of Planning and Budget shall submit to the Governor and to the Chairmen of the House Committee on Appropriations and the Senate Committee on Finance and Appropriations, no later than December 1, 2032, an evaluation of the Program that states the matters reported under subdivisions 1 and 2, distinguishes recorded retirement dates from estimated changes in retirement timing, and states the assumptions and limitations of any estimate it contains. The Board shall provide to the Director the data in the Board's possession that the Director requires for the evaluation.</u>

<u>**K.** If the General Assembly does not appropriate, for any fiscal year, an amount the Board has certified to the Governor and to the Chairmen of the House Committee on Appropriations and the Senate Committee on Finance and Appropriations as necessary to fund the certified subsidies the Board expects to reserve in that fiscal year and to administer this section, the Board shall accept no election filed on or after the first day of that fiscal year, and shall give public notice of that fact. An election the Board has accepted, a reservation the Board has made, and service credit the Board has credited before that date shall not be affected, and the Board shall continue to administer them.</u>

<u>**L.** Nothing in this section imposes any duty or cost upon a county, city, or town, upon any other political subdivision of the Commonwealth, upon a local school board, or upon any local retirement system.</u>

<u>**M. 1.** The Board shall adopt and publish, no later than July 1, 2029, procedures for administering this section, which shall address the content of a complete application, the channels through which an application may be filed, the determination of initial average compensation where records are incomplete, the determination and reservation of certified subsidies and contingency amounts, the waiting list, recertification, the crediting of service, settlement, cancellation, refunds, and the review of the Board's determinations. The Board shall publish the procedures on its website and shall make them available to any person on request.</u>

<u>2. A member, retiree, beneficiary, applicant, personal representative of a deceased member's estate, person who has claimed any benefit payable on the death of a member, person to whom this section requires a notice to be given in respect of a benefit payable on the death of a member, or state agency from whose appropriation an amount was paid on a member's behalf, adversely affected by a determination of the Board under this section, or by a failure of the Board to make a determination, to give a notice, or to make a payment within a period required by this section, or by a failure of the Board to state in a certification or notice a matter this section requires to be stated in it, may obtain, upon written request filed within 30 days after the notice of the determination is given or, in the case of a failure to make a determination, to give a notice, or to make a payment, at any time before the Board makes the determination, gives the notice, or makes the payment or, where the Board has made the determination, given the notice, or made the payment late, within 30 days after the notice of the determination is given or, in the case of a failure to give a notice or to make a payment, within 30 days after the Board gives the notice or makes the payment, or, where the Board has made the determination late and has not given the notice of it that this section requires, at any time before it gives that notice, or, in the case of a failure to state a matter, within 30 days after the certification or notice is given or, where the provision that requires the matter to be stated provides a longer or later period, within that period, written reconsideration by the Board stating the reasons for its decision. Every determination, certification, redetermination, recertification, reduction, cancellation, rescission, restoration, crediting of service credit, placement on a waiting list, expiration of a reservation, denial of an election, and refusal to accept an election under this section is a determination of the Board for the purposes of this subsection, whether or not another provision of this section expressly provides for review of it. The Board shall act upon a request for reconsideration within 90 days. Where the Board does not act upon a request for reconsideration within the 90 days allowed by this subsection, the person who filed the request may file with the Board written notice that the period has expired. Where the Board does not act upon the request within 30 days after that notice is filed, the request is denied on the first day after the expiration of those 30 days, and that denial constitutes the final determination of the Board upon the reconsideration and a case decision as defined in § 2.2-4001 for the purposes of this subsection. The filing of a request for reconsideration under this subsection, and the filing of a request under the definition of "final qualifying retirement date" in subsection A, tolls the running of the member's final qualifying retirement date and of any period within which the member or applicant must act under this section or under subdivision B 4 f of § 51.1-142.2, until 30 days after the Board acts upon the request, except that (i) no second or subsequent request for reconsideration of the same determination or of the same failure, and no second or subsequent request under that definition, tolls any period under this section or under subdivision B 4 f of § 51.1-142.2, and (ii) the aggregate of all tolling under this subsection shall not exceed 120 days, excluding any period after the expiration of the 90 days allowed by this subsection and before the Board acts upon the request, provided that the aggregate of all periods so excluded shall not exceed 90 days. An application that is the subject of a request for reconsideration under this subsection retains its filing date for the purposes of subdivisions D 4 and D 5 until the Board acts upon the request, and the Board shall act upon the application no later than 15 days after it acts upon the request. Upon completion of a reconsideration under this subsection, the final determination issued by the Board shall constitute a case decision as defined in § 2.2-4001. A member, retiree, beneficiary, applicant, personal representative of a deceased member's estate, person who has claimed any benefit payable on the death of a member, person to whom this section requires a notice to be given in respect of a benefit payable on the death of a member, or state agency from whose appropriation an amount was paid on a member's behalf, aggrieved by, and claiming the unlawfulness of, such case decision, shall have a right to seek judicial review thereof in accordance with Article 5 (§ 2.2-4025 et seq.) of the Administrative Process Act. Nothing in this subsection limits the right of any person under § 2.2-4026 to seek judicial review of a case decision. Articles 3 (§ 2.2-4018 et seq.) and 4 (§ 2.2-4024) of the Administrative Process Act shall not apply to a reconsideration under this subsection.</u>

<u>3. The sentence of subsection B of § 2.2-4021 providing that a decision shall be deemed to be in favor of the named party, and the corresponding sentence of subsection C of that section, shall not apply to a determination of the Board under this section or to a reconsideration under subdivision 2.</u>

<u>**N.** Upon the settlement of a certified subsidy under subdivision G 1 or G 4, the Commonwealth shall pay the amount settled to the retirement system from an appropriation made for that purpose, no later than 60 days after the settlement or, where the second sentence of this subsection applies, no later than 60 days after the Board makes the determinations required by that sentence. The Commonwealth shall not pay an amount settled under subdivision G 4 on the death of a member before the Board has determined whether a retirement allowance is payable under § 51.1-162 on the member's death and, where such an allowance is payable, whether the person entitled to it elects under § 51.1-162 to be paid the member's accumulated contributions or a lump sum in lieu of any other benefit under that section. Nothing in this section charges a benefit payable under § 51.1-162 against the limitations stated in subdivision D 2. The Board shall account for the amounts certified, reserved, settled, released, and paid under this section separately from all other assets and liabilities of the retirement system and shall state those amounts in the report required by subsection J.</u>

---

### § 51.1-162. Death before retirement.

**A.** If a member dies before retirement, and if no benefits are payable under subsection B, the amount of his accumulated contributions shall be paid to the designated beneficiary or to a surviving relative according to the order of precedence set forth in this section. This amount shall be reduced by the amount of any retirement allowance previously received by the member under this chapter or the abolished system. Each member shall designate who is to receive a refund of accumulated contributions credited to his account in the event of the death of the member prior to retirement. The designation must be made in a manner prescribed by the Board.

If no designation has been made, or the death of the designated person occurs prior to the death of the member and another designation has not been made, the proceeds shall be paid to the persons surviving at the death of the member in the following order of precedence:

First, to the spouse of the member;

Second, if no surviving spouse, to the children of the member and descendants of deceased children, per stirpes;

Third, if none of the above, to the parents of the member;

Fourth, if none of the above, to the duly appointed executor or administrator of the estate of the member;

Fifth, if none of the above, to other next of kin of the member entitled under the laws of the domicile of the member at the time of his death.

**B.** To the extent required by § 401(a)(37) of the Internal Revenue Code, as amended or renumbered, and the regulations thereunder applicable to governmental plans, if a member dies in service, including a member performing active duty military service in the armed forces of the United States, and if no benefits are payable under subsection C of this section, a retirement allowance shall be paid to the person or persons designated as provided in subsection A of this section if the person is the member's (i) surviving spouse, (ii) minor child, or (iii) parent(s). If no designation has been made, or if the death of the designated person occurs prior to the death of the member and another designation has not been made, a retirement allowance shall be paid in the following order of precedence to the member's (a) surviving spouse, (b) minor children, or (c) parent(s). The retirement allowance shall be paid to the first person qualifying in the orders of precedence set out in this subsection. If more than one minor child survives the deceased member, the allowance shall be divided among them in a manner determined by the Board. If more than one parent survives the deceased member, the allowance shall be divided among them in a manner determined by the Board. The retirement allowance shall be continued during the lifetime of the person or in the case of a minor child until the child dies or attains the age of majority, whichever occurs first. The retirement allowance shall equal the decreased retirement allowance that would have been payable under the joint and survivor option so that the same amount would be continued to such person after the member's death. If the member dies prior to his fifty-fifth birthday, then, for purposes of this subsection, the member shall be presumed to be age 55 on his date of death. However, if the member dies in service prior to his sixtieth birthday and is (1) a person who becomes a member on or after July 1, 2010, (2) a member who does not have at least 60 months of creditable service as of January 1, 2013, or (3) a member of the hybrid retirement program described in § 51.1-169, then, for purposes of this subsection, the member shall be presumed to be age 60 on his date of death. When determining the allowance that would have been payable to the member had the member retired on the date of his death, the provisions of subdivision A 4 of § 51.1-155 shall not apply. If the person elects in writing, the amount of the member's accumulated contributions or lump sum payment shall be paid to him exclusively, in lieu of any other benefits under this section. This amount shall be reduced by the amount of any retirement allowance previously received by the member under this chapter.

**C.** If a member dies in service from a cause compensable under the Virginia Workers' Compensation Act (§ 65.2-100 et seq.), a retirement allowance shall be paid to the member's surviving spouse. If no compensation is finally awarded under the Virginia Workers' Compensation Act due to legal proceedings or otherwise resulting in settlement from the persons causing such death, the Virginia Workers' Compensation Commission shall determine whether the member's death was from a cause compensable under the Virginia Workers' Compensation Act. If the member leaves no surviving spouse or the surviving spouse dies, any minor children of the deceased member shall be paid an allowance until the children die or attain the age of majority, whichever occurs first. If more than one minor child survives the deceased member, the allowance shall be divided in a manner determined by the Board. If the deceased member leaves neither surviving spouse nor minor child, the allowance, divided in a manner determined by the Board, shall be paid to the member's parents during their lives.

The retirement allowance payable hereunder to a qualifying survivor shall be the annual amount which when added to the compensation payable under the Virginia Workers' Compensation Act for the death of the member equals 50 percent of the member's average final compensation if the survivor does not qualify for death benefits under the provisions of the Social Security Act in effect on the date of the death of the member. If the survivor qualifies for death benefits under the provisions of the Social Security Act in effect on the date of the death of the member, the allowance payable from the retirement system when added to the compensation payable under the Virginia Workers' Compensation Act shall equal thirty-three and one-third percent of the member's average final compensation.

Any beneficiary entitled to the entire amount of a retirement allowance under the provisions of this subsection as a result of the death of a member shall be entitled to waive his rights to the allowance by written notification to the Board within ninety days after the death of the member<u>, or within such longer period as subdivision G 4 of § 51.1-142.4 provides,</u> in order to make available a retirement allowance under the provisions of subsection B of this section.

---

*Editorial note, not part of the bill.* Revision 21 removes **§ 51.1-155 from the sections this act
amends**, by the Policy Director's direction of 12 October 2026 (pass 4's S11 and
[MOO-52](/MOO/issues/MOO-52), which agreed from different sources that the conforming amendment to
subdivision A 2 was superfluous because this cohort retires under § 51.1-153 B 2 and is paid under
subdivision A 1). The act now amends **two** sections and adds one: § 51.1-162 was added to the amended sections by the store revision recorded at §0 below, on the Policy Director's answer of 12 October 2026, to carry the conforming amendment to § 51.1-162 C. § 51.1-155 is still **cited** — in
subdivision B 4 a of § 51.1-142.2 and in four places in § 51.1-142.4 — but a cited section is not an
amended section and is not reproduced. The removal is checked reference by reference in **§5.11**,
and the §0 record for revision 21, now in the retained-record document, records what came out with it.

---

### Remaining enactment clauses

**2. That the provisions of the first enactment of this act shall become effective on July 1, 2029.**

**3. That § 51.1-142.4 of the Code of Virginia, as created by this act, subdivision B 4 of § 51.1-142.2 of the Code of Virginia, as amended by this act, and the amendments made by this act to subdivision B 2 of § 51.1-142.2 and to the undesignated paragraph of subsection B of § 51.1-142.2 beginning "Except as provided in subdivision 4" shall expire on July 1, 2033, except that (i) the Board of Trustees of the Virginia Retirement System shall continue to administer, service, recertify, settle, account for, and credit service for every election accepted before that date, and to make every refund, payment, notice, determination, rescission, restoration, and reinstatement required by § 51.1-142.4, and to determine the cost of service credit purchased under every such election under subdivision B 4 of § 51.1-142.2 as amended by this act and under the second sentence of subdivision B 2 of that section as amended by this act, in each case as though those provisions had not expired and notwithstanding clause (iv) of this enactment, and every right of a member under those provisions and every period allowed to a member by those provisions shall continue in effect and continue to run as though those provisions had not expired, and the Commonwealth shall make every payment required by subsection N of that section in respect of any settlement under that section; (ii) the limitations in subdivision D 2 of § 51.1-142.4 shall continue to apply after that date, every certified subsidy and every contingency amount reserved under subsection D of that section before that date shall continue to be charged against those limitations until the election is cancelled or the reservation is released, and a certified subsidy that has been settled shall remain charged against the limitation in the first sentence of subdivision D 2 as provided in that subdivision; (iii) the reporting and evaluation duties in subsection J of § 51.1-142.4 shall continue in effect until the last report and evaluation required by that subsection has been submitted, and for the purposes of those duties subsection A of that section and every provision of that section to which subsection J refers shall continue in effect as though those provisions had not expired; (iv) upon the expiration of the amendments made by this act to the undesignated paragraph of subsection B of § 51.1-142.2 beginning "Except as provided in subdivision 4," that paragraph shall read as it read immediately before the effective date of this act, except as clause (i) of this enactment provides; and (v) every duty of the Board to determine and to give notice under § 51.1-142.4 in respect of an application filed before that date, and subsection M of that section as it applies to such a determination or to a failure to make such a determination or to give such a notice, shall continue in effect until discharged, the application is withdrawn, or the applicant dies, and for the purposes of every such determination and notice subdivision B 4 a of § 51.1-142.2 as amended by this act shall continue in effect as though it had not expired; and (vi) upon that expiration the amendment made by this act to subsection C of § 51.1-162 shall expire and that subsection shall read as it read immediately before the effective date of this act, except as clause (i) of this enactment provides.**

**4. That the provisions of the first enactment of this act shall not become effective unless reenacted by the 2028 Session of the General Assembly.**

**5. That the Board of Trustees of the Virginia Retirement System shall, no later than December 1, 2027, report to the Governor and to the Chairmen of the House Committee on Appropriations and the Senate Committee on Finance and Appropriations on (i) the systems, staffing, and nongeneral fund resources the Board will require to administer § 51.1-142.4 of the Code of Virginia, as it would be created by the first enactment of this act, (ii) the Board's determination under § 415 of the Internal Revenue Code of the payment methods and the state agency payment authorized by subdivision B 4 of § 51.1-142.2 of the Code of Virginia, as it would be amended by the first enactment of this act, (iii) the Board's administrative construction of subdivision B 2 of § 51.1-153 and of subdivision A 2 of § 51.1-155 of the Code of Virginia, in each case as it applies to service credit purchased under § 51.1-142.2, and (iv) any technical amendment the Board recommends. The provisions of this enactment shall become effective on July 1, 2027, and shall apply whether or not the first enactment of this act is reenacted. If the first enactment of this act is reenacted by the 2028 Session of the General Assembly, the Board may adopt and publish the procedures required by subsection M of § 51.1-142.4 of the Code of Virginia, as it would be created by the first enactment of this act, before July 1, 2029, to take effect on that date.**

## 2. Section-by-section summary in plain English

**For the Editor, in three sentences.** The bill lets a Virginia state employee who is a veteran buy
up to four years of prior active-duty military service at a price based on what that service would
have cost had it been credited when the employee was first hired — the plan's published normal cost
rate applied to the employee's own first three years of state pay, plus 6 percent interest to a
valuation date — instead of the much higher "actuarial equivalent cost" the employee pays today for
missing a 24-month deadline that ran out years ago. Only employees who need the purchased months to
reach an unreduced retirement — and who are already within reach of one on the day they apply, on the
service they actually have — may buy at that price, they must actually retire within a 12-month window
that opens when the purchase is credited, and the Virginia Retirement System may not accept an
election at all unless it has first certified and set aside the Commonwealth's share of the cost
within a $3 million aggregate ceiling and a per-member ceiling. The program runs for four years, applies only to state agency
employees — no locality, school division or teacher is touched — and nothing in it creates any debt
a member can be billed for after a purchase.

| Provision | What it does |
|---|---|
| **§ 51.1-142.2 B 2** (one new sentence) | Points the military-service price at the new subdivision 4 for members accepted into the pilot. Leaves the four-year maximum, the "not dishonorable" condition and everything else in B 2 alone. |
| **§ 51.1-142.2 B 3** (one inserted phrase) | Carves the pilot out of the approximate-normal-cost sentence, so a member inside a 24-month window who elects does not face two live prices. The actuarial-equivalent-cost sentence is left **untouched** in revision 2 — that is what makes the "no worse than current law" ceiling measurable. Neither sentence changes for the eight categories in B 1 or for anyone outside the pilot. |
| **§ 51.1-142.2 B 4 a** | Defines six terms (the sixth, **"limitation year," is new in revision 12** and points at § 415 and its regulations, because the § 415 deferral in B 4 f runs on that unit): "initial average compensation" (the average of the member's own first 36 months of state pay, with a stated tie-breaker when VRS records are incomplete), "statutory interest" (6 percent, compounded annually), "total normal cost rate" (the rate VRS publishes and already uses for approximate normal cost, **with the July 1, 2026 figures stated in the text** — 12.50 percent Plan 1, 10.74 percent Plan 2), "standard cost" (what the member would pay if the pilot did not exist), and "qualifying military service election." |
| **§ 51.1-142.2 B 4 b** | The price. Normal cost rate × first-36-month average pay × years bought, plus 6 percent compound interest from the end of that 36-month period to a single valuation date — **or** the standard cost, whichever is lower. Interest stops at the valuation date, so the certified amount is a fixed sum no matter how the member pays. No member can be made worse off than today. |
| **§ 51.1-142.2 B 4 c** | VRS must show its work: both prices, the valuation date, the rate used and the assumptions behind the standard cost. The member gets one right to demand a re-quote before paying, and pays the lowest of all four figures. |
| **§ 51.1-142.2 B 4 d** | The 24-month window does not apply to a pilot purchase. This is the reform: the price stops depending on how long ago the member was hired. |
| **§ 51.1-142.2 B 4 e** | The member pays at least 5 percent of the price from money the employer did not give them for that purpose; an agency may pay the rest only after filing a written policy covering all of its **eligible** employees, and the policy may cap the agency's own annual exposure. |
| **§ 51.1-142.2 B 4 f** | VRS must apply the federal § 415 limits before accepting any payment, and nothing in the bill touches USERRA rights or the existing free military-leave credit. **Revision 12:** if the limit blocks part of a payment, the member is told within 30 days and may buy fewer months, **defer the blocked amount to the next limitation year** with the payment deadline extended and the reservation held, or cancel — so a federal limit nobody controls cannot cost the member the election and the price. The determination is reviewable, and it is expressly not treated as VRS error. |
| **§ 51.1-142.4 A** | Definitions, including "certified subsidy" — the Commonwealth's share, measured as the increase in the plan's liability caused by the election, counting both the price discount and the earlier retirement, minus what the member and employer pay, with a floor equal to the price discount, **reserved on the assumption that the member retires on the first date they may and then redetermined against the date they actually retire**. Also defines "unreduced retirement eligibility date," which is the test every eligibility and quantity rule now turns on; **"earliest qualifying retirement date" and "final qualifying retirement date," which are the two ends of the retirement window — the **12 months** in the second of those sets how long a member has to go, chosen by the Director's direction of 12 October 2026 and no longer bracketed**; "involuntary separation," by reference to the Workforce Transition Act; and "state agency." Eligibility expressly covers **both benefit tiers** (VRS Plan 1 and Plan 2), stated by reference to the Code's own discriminator in § 51.1-155 A 1 rather than by the administrative plan names; hybrid program participants remain out. **Revisions 16 and 17 complete the admission test.** "Eligible member" now has **two** date legs rather than one: the employee must be within reach of an unreduced retirement **on the day the application is filed** (clause (vi)), *and* the months actually bought must get them there when the service is credited (clause (vii)). Without the first leg, an employee up to about 13 months short could qualify simply by taking the full 12 months to pay — a different and larger group than every costed figure in the package describes. Revision 17 then bounds the first leg by **the military service the employee actually has** and by the four-year and state-service limits, so no one can be admitted on a purchase they could never make and left to fail at crediting; and it says in terms that the *minimum-necessary* trim in subsection C does **not** bound that test, which is what keeps the program from admitting nobody at all. |
| **§ 51.1-142.4 B** | Establishes the program; confirms purchased credit is ordinary creditable service; makes participation voluntary and forbids an employer from pushing an employee into it. **Store revision 69 also moved the eligibility bar here from subsection L:** no person whom the definition of *"eligible member"* in subsection A excludes — including a teacher, an employee of a political subdivision and a member of a Chapter 2, 2.1 or 3 retirement system — may make an election. |
| **§ 51.1-142.4 C** | Quantity limit: no more than the months actually needed to reach unreduced retirement, measured as of one stated completion date, capped by the existing four years and by the member's own years of state service. Paying early requires a re-quote. **Store revision 59, on the Director's direction of 12 October 2026 (§7 item 109), extends the subsection's own reporting duty to a failure to *state* a matter the subsection requires the Board to state** — previously it reached only a failure to determine or to notify, so the one duty edit 1 created had no *"or else."* |
| **§ 51.1-142.4 D** | The cap as an admission rule. VRS must certify the price and the Commonwealth's share, get written confirmation from DPB (or a designee, within 30 days) that money is legally available, and reserve the money **before** accepting an election. Aggregate ceiling $3,000,000, per-member ceiling **$170,000, set by the Policy Director's direction of 11 October 2026 and no longer bracketed (recorded at §0)** — and note that the sentence **refuses** an election above the ceiling rather than trimming it (recorded at §0); first-come order with a 60-day cure period that keeps the original filing date, a lot-draw for same-day ties, a non-electronic filing channel, a published running balance, wait-listing instead of rejection with written notice on expiry, a fixed **`[20]` percent** contingency that is **charged against the $3,000,000**, recertification on **any** increase, and release of money when a reservation lapses. Reservations do not survive past the fiscal year following the year they were made without a fresh DPB confirmation. A zero-cost certification cannot create headroom for someone else. **Revision 3:** a reservation now lasts until the election settles or is released, and the annual DPB confirmation is a brake on *new* admissions rather than something that can strand a member who has already paid. The member signs an acknowledgment of obligations at acceptance. **Revision 12 fixes the notice deadline, which could not be met as drafted:** the member files the formal § 51.1-153 notice of retirement on the later of 30 days before the date they choose and **30 days after VRS tells them the window** — and because § 51.1-153 E allows a notice up to 90 days after the effective date, a member whose window opens almost immediately can still file and is paid retroactively. **Revision 12 also puts a consequence behind DPB's 30 days:** if the Director does not act, VRS must notify the member and the money-committee chairmen within 15 days, report every lapse, hold the application's filing date, and act within 15 days of the confirmation when it comes. **Revision 5:** the contingency is counted against the $3,000,000, so the cap is a cap, and it rises to `[20]` percent because 10 percent failed the 90th percentile of recertification increases. (Revision 5 also set the per-member ceiling at `[$100,000]`; **that figure is superseded by the $170,000 above** and is recorded only as the revision history.) **Store revision 59 puts two more clocks in, on the Director's direction of 12 October 2026 (§7 item 108):** VRS must **file** the DPB request within 15 days after the application is completed, and must **give** the certification within 15 days after the confirmation is obtained. Before them the Board could meet every period the section stated and still leave an eligible member waiting for ever, because subdivision D 1 bars acceptance until the certification issues and nothing required the certification. The worst case now ends in the existing 90-day applicant notice at about **day 105** instead of never. |
| **§ 51.1-142.4 E** | Payment within 12 months and in the certified amount; VRS credits the service only when payment is complete, and within 30 days of completion. Nobody is treated as retirement-eligible on a half-paid purchase. **Revision 12:** the member may ask VRS to credit the service **as of the last day of a stated month**, and VRS must confirm the date it credits within five days. That matters because § 51.1-153 E requires a retirement to take effect *after* the member's last day of service, and without a fixed crediting date the member would have to resign against a date they could not yet know. |
| **§ 51.1-142.4 F** | The retirement condition: the member must retire on the first of some month in the window that opens the month after the credit is posted and closes **12 months** later, with up to three further months where VRS's own processing — or **any other cause not the member's fault** — makes retirement by the deadline impracticable. **Revision 12 turns that extension from a discretion into a duty**: VRS *shall* approve it, the member may ask for it in writing at any time before the deadline, VRS must answer within 30 days with reasons, and the refusal is reviewable. VRS must tell the member both dates in writing within 15 days of posting the credit — i.e. before the member resigns — and must pay retroactively if its own processing delays commencement. States in terms that failure to retire creates **no** debt and that nothing may be collected or withheld. **Revision 21 closes the one place where that promise was not kept** (pass 4's F4 and [MOO-65](/MOO/issues/MOO-65)'s F-2, found independently and both called fatal): VRS may now make the G 2 determination as soon as the member retires in a non-qualifying retirement rather than only after the deadline has passed, the member's first allowance payment must carry every amount that would have been payable from the effective date of retirement, and subsection F says in terms that the deferral is not a withholding and reduces nothing. A member who retires early in her window is no longer left up to roughly thirteen months without a retirement cheque. |
| **§ 51.1-142.4 G** | Settlement at retirement, and what happens if it does not happen. If the member retires, the subsidy settles and the credit vests, and within 90 days VRS **redetermines the subsidy against the date the member actually retired** — capped at the amount reserved, with the difference released to fund other elections, and with no consequence of any kind for the member, the member's beneficiaries or the employer. If the member chooses not to retire, VRS re-values the payments at ordinary prices, keeps the member's credit at that reduced amount, refunds the excess within 60 days and releases the reservation — with 30 days' written notice, reasons and a review right, and **before any allowance is ever paid**, so no overpayment can arise. Partial payment buys pro-rata credit at ordinary prices. **Revision 10 — the middle treatment.** Death, disability retirement and VRS's own error are carved out: the credit vests at the pilot price, and a survivor is paid as though the member had retired. **Involuntary separation is no longer carved out.** A member who is involuntarily separated keeps the pilot price only by retiring in a qualifying retirement within 90 days of the separation; otherwise the ordinary re-valuation applies — the member's own payments buy credit at ordinary prices, the excess is refunded, and nothing is billed to anyone. VRS must tell the member the date within 15 days of learning of the separation. The 12-month rehire rule is deleted, because there is no longer a vesting carve-out for it to police. **Revision 12 closes the hole in that carve-out.** The credit vested on death, disability or VRS error but nothing *settled*, because settlement keyed only to a retirement inside the window — so the Commonwealth's appropriation never paid and the pension trust was left carrying the subsidy. G 4 now settles on the earliest of death, the start of a disability allowance, and the member's actual retirement, runs the same redetermination as a normal settlement, and repeats the no-debt guarantee for survivors and beneficiaries. |
| **§ 51.1-142.4 H** | One election per member — but the bar bites only on a member who actually got credit or settled, so a member whose application expired on the waiting list or whose election was cancelled by VRS error is not locked out. Every existing purchase right is preserved. |
| **§ 51.1-142.4 I** | Closes the contractor route: a retiree who comes back within 12 months doing substantially the same work — measured against the Employee Work Profile for the job they left — is treated as re-employed, so the allowance stops under § 51.1-155 B 1. The agency reports within 30 days, and VRS must give notice, 30 days to respond and a written determination **before** stopping any payment, which stops prospectively only. No service accrues and no contributions are owed for the period. |
| **§ 51.1-142.4 J** | Reporting. VRS reports every December 1, during the pilot and then until every election has been settled, cancelled or determined — a tail tied to the facts rather than to a fixed three years, because at a long retirement window the last determinations would otherwise fall after the last report — on applications, admissions, denials, wait-lists, money reserved, settled and paid, who actually retired, and the variance between its certified subsidies and realised actuarial cost. DHRM and DOA supply replacement-cost payroll and contract data to **DPB**, and **DPB** — not the Board — writes the December 1, 2032 evaluation. **Store revision 59 gives the post-terminal further report an independent trigger** (§7 item 113): a matter arising after the last annual report requires a further report even where no rescission under subdivision G 4 occurs, the rescission having been the only trigger before. |
| **§ 51.1-142.4 K** | The kill switch. If the General Assembly declines to appropriate what the Board certifies it needs, VRS stops accepting **new** elections — and elections already accepted, money already reserved and credit already posted are untouched. |
| **§ 51.1-142.4 L** | Says in the text that no locality, political subdivision, school board or local retirement system bears any duty or cost. **The bar on teachers and local employees electing is no longer in this subsection** — store revision 69 moved it into subsection B — and what excludes them is the definition of *"eligible member"* in subsection A. |
| **§ 51.1-142.4 M** | Delegation boundary. VRS must adopt and publish procedures by **July 1, 2029** — the subject matter the procedures must cover is listed in the text, and the Board is told what it may not require in a complete application. **Revision 21 drops the APA Article 2 exemption entirely**, by the Director's direction of 12 October 2026: the procedures go through ordinary Article 2 rulemaking, which restores the § 2.2-4006 C document step, executive review (§ 2.2-4013), legislative review (§ 2.2-4014) and periodic review (§ 2.2-4017) over the rules that ration a capped benefit. The deletion is operative rather than cosmetic — § 2.2-4002 contains no retirement exemption, verified again on 12 October 2026 — and it has a timing consequence the Board will have to manage, which is **§7 item 27**. Written reconsideration with reasons within 90 days and tolling of every deadline while a request is pending. **Store revision 59 enacts §7 item 88 option (d) with its optional second limb, on the Director's direction of 12 October 2026 and against my own withdrawn recommendation:** the completed reconsideration is made a case decision as defined in § 2.2-4001 **by statute**, so Article 5 judicial review is **conferred** rather than merely preserved — which is what pass 15's S-3 said the old savings clause could not do — and **Articles 3 (§ 2.2-4018 et seq.) and 4 (§ 2.2-4024) of the Administrative Process Act are disapplied to a reconsideration**, the device §§ 51.1-1135.1 and 51.1-1177 use for this Board. **Stated rather than netted away: the limb removes the member's Article 3 informal-fact-finding and formal-hearing rights in the reconsideration**, which is the cost counsel objected to and the Director accepted; M 2's own floor — a 30-day window, written reasons, a 90-day duty to act, and tolling — is what remains. **New subdivision M 3** provides that § 2.2-4021 B's and C's deemed-decision sentences do not apply to a determination under this section or to a reconsideration, so agency silence cannot award a member a subsidy against the $170,000 and $3,000,000 limits (§7 item 110). |
| **§ 51.1-142.4 N** | **Resolved in revision 9 — Alternative A.** Who pays: on settlement — **under G 1 or, as revision 12 adds, under G 4** — the Commonwealth pays the certified subsidy to the retirement system from an appropriation made for that purpose, and VRS accounts separately for every amount certified, reserved, settled, released and paid. No longer bracketed. |
| ~~**§ 51.1-155 A 2**~~ **DROPPED IN REVISION 21** | The conforming amendment is **out of the bill**, by the Director's direction of 12 October 2026. Pass 4's S11 and [MOO-52](/MOO/issues/MOO-52) agreed from different sources that it was superfluous: this cohort retires under § 51.1-153 B 2, whose benefit *"shall be calculated in accordance with the provisions of subdivision A 1"* — normal retirement, no reduction — so A 2 never reaches them. The cost of keeping it was that enactment 3 made it permanent and it put the Commonwealth's general early-retirement reduction provision into a pilot bill's title and object. **The act now amends one section and adds one**, and "early retirement reduction" is out of the title. The belt-and-braces survives as a report item: enactment 5 (iii) still requires the Board to put its own construction of A 2 on the record by December 1, 2027. |
| **§ 51.1-162 C** (one inserted clause) | The only change this act makes to an existing death-benefit section. A survivor who is paid the work-related-death allowance under subsection C may give it up within 90 days of the death so that the larger subsection B allowance can be paid to the member's spouse, children or parents instead. Subdivision G 4 of the new section provides that where the Virginia Retirement System's own notice is late her ninety days cannot end before the thirtieth day after that notice — **it is a floor and never shortens the ninety** — and this clause says so in § 51.1-162 C itself, so that nobody reading § 51.1-162 C alone tells her the right has expired. **Nothing else in § 51.1-162 changes**, and the section is reproduced in full in §1 because the act now amends it. |
| **Enactment 2** | Effective July 1, 2029. |
| **Enactment 3** | The pilot pricing and the program expire July 1, 2033, but VRS keeps full authority to service, settle, pay and report on everything accepted before that date. **Revision 21 completes the expiry list**: the B 2 pointer sentence and the B 3 "except as provided in subdivision 4" phrase expire with subdivision B 4, so that after July 1, 2033 nothing is left in § 51.1-142.2 pointing at a subdivision that no longer exists, and B 2 and B 3 read exactly as they do under current law. Nothing now survives the sunset. |
| **Enactment 4** | The Code amendments do not take effect unless the 2028 Session reenacts them. |
| **Enactment 5** | Operative regardless of reenactment: VRS reports by December 1, 2027 on what it needs to run the program, on the federal tax treatment of the employer payment, **on how it actually reads "30 years of service" in § 51.1-155 A 2** — kept deliberately in revision 21, because with the conforming amendment dropped this report is the only place the act touches that question — and on any technical amendment it recommends. It also lets the Board adopt the subsection M procedures early, to take effect July 1, 2029, if the first enactment is reenacted; **with Article 2 now applying, that permission is doing considerably more work than it was** (§7 item 27). |

## 3. What the actuarial cost statement will need from this draft — performed; moved to the retained record

**Moved verbatim** to
[`bill-text-s8-revision-history-2`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-revision-history-2) in store
revision 44, every item number and every bracketed correction preserved, so a citation of the form **§3
item 7** still resolves there. It went out under the Policy Director's standing rule on
[MOO-101](/MOO/issues/MOO-101) section E — **§3 moves next; §1, §5, §6 and §7 stay with the bill** — to
make room for this revision's record, and it went to the **second volume** because
[`bill-text-s8-revision-history`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-revision-history) is itself
within 1,000 characters of the 524,288-character ceiling. **The durable results this file still relies
on:**

- **Cap utilisation is not published from §3.** §3's own reservation-basis range — low **38**, central
  **76**, high **126** percent, each including the mandatory 20 percent contingency — is **superseded for
  every external use** by MOO-32 v6's authorised **78.1872** percent central and **130.31** percent high
  on **18.3965** participants. Both sets of figures are in the moved text with their bases named. What
  survives from §3 as reasoning rather than as a number: the cap rations **total** admissions over the
  pilot and not concurrent ones, because a settled subsidy is never released; and the 12-month window does
  **not** ease the cap, because reservations are certified on the anticipated **earliest** qualifying
  retirement date.
- ***"Certified subsidy"* measures one liability change, not two overlapping ones**; the acceleration
  counterfactual is in the Code rather than in the model; the per-member ceiling is **$170,000**, set off
  the distribution (§3 item 4's earlier ceilings are superseded); and the retirement-window numeral is the
  dominant cost dial, so the statement must carry it.
- **The § 30-19.1:7 statement must disclose two one-sided omissions with a known direction**: the clause
  (ii) rounding, which store revision 33 measured at **−0.08** percentage points of cap utilisation and
  which [MOO-102](/MOO/issues/MOO-102) has since measured at **zero** on the authorised cohort, because
  that cohort carries no fractional needs; and the clause (ii) **base** repair, enacted in store revision
  33, which lowers quantity for members holding non-membership creditable service and is unmeasured
  because the engine runs with `prior = 0.0`.
- **Settlement on death, disability and Board error is a determination and not a silence**; subdivision
  B 4 f's § 415 deferral can push a completion date, and therefore a valuation date, out by up to 24
  months; and no statement may assume that every accepted election either settles on a qualifying
  retirement or disappears.
- **One class §3 does not price separately, and it is now §7 item 78**: the Plan 1 member one month short
  of fifty, for whom clause (ii) has no value, buys up to **six times** the credit she needs and is
  refused at the per-election ceiling 69 percent of the time. Capping her **raises** utilisation to
  78.2929 percent and admits 18.4172 participants — inside publishable precision, and the opposite sign
  from the intuitive one.

- **NEW IN STORE REVISION 52, on the Director's answer to §7 item 99: the § 30-19.1:7 statement must
  *quantify* two one-sided costs, not merely disclose them.** Both were argued to the Director on the
  footing that the Commonwealth bears them either way and both were taken on that footing, so **neither
  answer is reopened** — the requirement is only that the statement name the amounts. They are: **(a)**
  the retirement allowance item 46 requires the Board to pay a member while her disability application is
  pending, which a later determination may show was too large and which subsection F's no-collection limb
  makes **irrecoverable** — net of the one month-for-month offset store revision 52 authorises as item 95;
  and **(b)** the clause (ii) refund item 50 makes irrecoverable **even where the member buys her service
  credit back**. **The amounts are [@Fiscal & Impact Modeler](agent://428b53bf-f49e-4505-a607-3560a26b3d50)'s
  and are requested, not asserted here**; pass 12's own illustrative figure for the offset duplicate was
  $1,300 to $2,500 per affected member on an expected headcount well under 0.1, which is the reviewer's
  arithmetic on an assumption and not a figure of Fiscal's. **Store revision 52 also leaves both outside
  subsection J's report lines — that is §7 item 104.**

## 4. What could not be drafted without the MOO-39 answer

MOO-39 asks whether a Plan 1 member's early-retirement reduction is measured back from age 65 or
from the date the member *would have* reached 30 years of creditable service. It is worth 32 percent
of the S9 score. **S8 as drafted above does not depend on the answer, and nothing in §1 is held for
it.** Three items are:

1. **The S9 reduction-waiver provisions are not drafted.** S9 buys off the early-retirement
   reduction instead of selling service credit. Its operative sentence has to say what is being
   waived, measured how — and that is precisely the open question. **Conditional on MOO-39:** if the
   answer is "projected," an S9 provision can be drafted as a purchased waiver of the reduction
   computed under § 51.1-155 with the unreduced date determined as though service had continued; if
   the answer is "frozen," the waiver is worth roughly 35 percent more and the price, the cap and the
   per-member ceiling all have to be reset before any language is useful.
2. **MOO-39's owner should be pointed at § 51.1-155, not § 51.1-153.** This is MOO-44's finding
   outside its assignment and it is correct, and I can now confirm it from the live text I retrieved
   for the conforming amendment. § 51.1-155 A 3 states the measure as *"the first date on which the
   sum of his then attained age plus his then creditable service **would have been equal to 90 or
   more had he remained in service until such date**"* and A 2 as *"the first date on which he
   **would have completed** a total of 30 years of creditable service."* **That is the projected
   reading, expressly so in A 3.** The Code is not silent on this question. Routing to Policy
   Director rather than acting on it, because MOO-39 is not mine.
3. **The per-member subsidy ceiling cannot be carried across to S9.** The bracketed figure, whatever
   MOO-48 returns, will be derived from the S8 run, on this definition, at a chosen window length. A
   single ceiling across two designs needs both priced on the same basis.

Note also that the eligibility test in § 51.1-142.4 A and the quantity limit in subsection C now
turn on the defined term "unreduced retirement eligibility date," which **as revised in revision 7**
cites § 51.1-155 **A 1, A 2 and A 3** by name — the positive limb routes to the normal-retirement
computation in A 1 and the negative limb excludes the two reduction provisions. That is a strict
improvement for MOO-39 purposes, and revision 7 makes it a larger one: the draft no longer pretends
§ 51.1-153 settles the reduction question, and any future S9 language has one defined term to attach
to whose both limbs are explicit. **For MOO-39 specifically** — whether a Plan 1 member's reduction
is measured back from age 65 or from the projected 30-year date — revision 7 changes nothing. The
definition asks whether an allowance **is** reduced under A 2 or A 3, not by how much or measured from
when, so the answer to MOO-39 moves the S9 price and not this bill's admission test.
