## 6. Drafter's note — structure choices and ambiguities resolved

**The retained drafter's note — §6.0-18 to §6.0-50 and §6.1 to §6.4, for store revisions 2 to 51 — is moved verbatim to [`bill-text-s8-record-s0-s5-s6-rev2-51`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-record-s0-s5-s6-rev2-51)** on the Director's answer to §7 item 53. A reference of the form **§6.0-n**, **§6.1a** or **§6.4** still resolves there. **Store revision 71 adds **§6.0-71**, so the sections below are the drafter's notes for that revision and for store revisions 70, 69, 67, 66, 65 and 63 — the notes for store revisions 62, 61, 60 and 59 were moved out in store revision 71, which is what §6's own preamble had told the next revision short of room to do** — store revisions 64 and 68 changed no word of any section in the act and wrote no note of their own, so **the gaps at §6.0-64, §6.0-68 and §6.0-72 are those revisions' silence and not lost notes.** **The ceiling is live.** Store revision 70 could not write until §5.38 and §5.39 were moved out and moved the §5 half of the rotation only; **store revision 71, 9,069 characters from the ceiling, moved both halves** — §5.40 to §5.42 and §6.0-59 to §6.0-62, together with six of §8's status notes. **§6.0-63 and §6.0-65 are the next to go.** This paragraph named store revisions 54 back to 52 through store revisions 59, 60 and 61, none of which corrected it; **it was corrected in store revision 62, store revision 63 added §6.0-63 without updating the count, and store revision 65 brings it current, because the rule that a stale self-description is a defect of the record applies to this paragraph twice over**. The retention the Director's answer to §7 item 53 set — this section holds the current note only — still stands and is forced by the ceiling again; the next revision that is short of room should move §6.0-63 and §6.0-65 out first.

### §6.0-71 Ambiguities resolved in store revision 71 — with two findings declined with reasons and two corrections to the passes

**1. The fatal finding is not answered in the text, and that is a rule and not a judgment.** [MOO-210](/MOO/issues/MOO-210)'s **R1** is the fourth defect found in one sentence of subdivision G 4, and the Policy Director's standing direction of 12 October 2026 (**standing direction A1**) forecloses two of the three families of cure: anchoring on the member's **notification** is squarely foreclosed, and MOO-210 reads the direction as reaching a cure that **defines** *"the Board determines"* as the Board's act in approving the retirement, because that is an approval event wearing a different verb. **The remaining family — a fact about payment, in the reviewer's form *"and under which no payment of a retirement allowance had been made to the member before the date of death"* — is f1-a's substance, and the Director chose f1-b over f1-a on 12 October.** Enacting f1-a's substance now would re-decide that card by drafting choice. It is **§7 item 136**, drafted three ways and enacted none.

**2. 71-A is enacted knowing it may be deleted, and the trade is named rather than hidden.** See §0.0-71. The alternative was to leave a $250,000-an-occurrence windfall — 18% of the programme's central aggregate — standing in the live text while the card sits. **The windfall class is not a class the agreed policy means to pay**, so bounding it restores the agreed policy rather than choosing one, which is the line this role works to.

**3. 71-B reaches outside the act's footprint for the third time, and the reach is recorded as a reach.** The act now (i) displaces the § 51.1-163 routing limbs of subdivision C 1 of § 51.1-165 and of subsection F of § 51.1-165.01 by *"notwithstanding"*, (ii) extends the waiver period stated in § 51.1-162 C, and (iii) **deems a fact for the purposes of a definition in § 51.1-124.3.** None of the three is accompanied by an amendment to the section reached. [MOO-207](/MOO/issues/MOO-207) answered the authority question for (i) — ***Anderson v. Commonwealth* (1868) is the holding, not *"complete in itself"*** — and that answer does not reach (ii) or (iii). **All three must travel in the drafting request to Division of Legislative Services**, and MOO-210's **R7** adds a fourth candidate that is not enacted: Edit 3 of store revision 69 purports to switch off a § 51.1-162 entitlement with no *"notwithstanding"* behind it at all.

**4. 71-B uses one formulation for two propositions rather than two.** The in-service waiver and the died-in-service deeming are joined in one clause reaching both § 51.1-162 and § 51.1-124.3, instead of the reviewer's second sentence. Two sentences would have put *"for the purposes of § 51.1-162"* in subdivision G 4 twice, and two formulations of one concept inside one subdivision is the defect [MOO-126](/MOO/issues/MOO-126)'s F4 was about. **Counted before and after: the greater-of sentence still carries exactly five *"preceding sentence"* references and is still one sentence**, so none of the five has acquired a new antecedent.

**4a. 71-B opens § 51.1-162 C to the exception-class member, and that is intended rather than overlooked.** § 51.1-162 C requires a death *"in service"* from a cause compensable under the Virginia Workers' Compensation Act. The deeming makes that limb available to a member who dies with a retirement application pending, which is what the agreed policy means by settling her case **as a death in service**. It costs the act nothing it was not already carrying: [MOO-189](/MOO/issues/MOO-189)'s **F6(c)** already records that a § 51.1-162 C death gets **nothing** from the purchased credit, because subsection C measures on average final compensation and not on creditable service, while the certified subsidy settles and subsection N still pays.

**5. 71-C and 71-D are a pair, and neither is enactable alone.** 71-C adds a ground to the release gateway; 71-D gives that ground a date. A ground with no date would have left the Board with a duty to release and no period within which to discharge it, which subdivision M 2 can review only where a *period* exists. **Who, what, by when.** The date chosen is **60 days**, which is the period both other grounds in the same sentence already use; no new numeral enters the act.

**6. 71-C is drafted as an added ground and not as a narrowing of *"payable"*, deliberately.** MOO-210's R5 and [MOO-200](/MOO/issues/MOO-200)'s F2 pull on the same word in opposite directions: reading *"payable"* narrowly, so that the prohibition means no allowance is payable, opens the release gateway but weakens R7; reading it broadly keeps R7 and strands the money. **An added ground works under either reading and commits the act to neither**, which leaves the Director's answer on R7 free.

**7. 71-F reverses a placement decision that was mine.** §6.0-67 item 2 recorded that the placement of the § 51.1-162 C clause *"after the ninety days, before 'in order to make available'"* was the drafter's choice and that it made the clause qualify the period. **MOO-200's F8 is right that it does not** — set off by commas it is a parenthetical available to qualify the right to waive or the purpose as well. The clause is now attached to the period in words rather than by position: *"within ninety days after the death of the member, or within such longer period as subdivision G 4 of § 51.1-142.4 provides."* **It can no longer cut the right down**, which matters because § 51.1-162 C will outlive § 51.1-142.4 unless 71-E's clause (vi) stands.

**8. 71-J states the Code and does not enact the answer.** MOO-200's **F1** holds that the certification compelled the Board to state, as a condition of taking the member's money, that no § 51.1-165.01 option limits her death benefit — when § 51.1-165.01 **C** provides that any optional payment pursuant to § 51.1-165 is based on the **reduced** allowance, and § 51.1-162 B's measure is a hypothetical optional computation. **The false assertion is removed and § 51.1-165.01 C is stated in its own words.** What is **not** enacted is F1's substantive fix — deeming the member not to have elected a lump sum where none has been paid — because that is worth about **$200,000** to a named survivor and is a decision about who bears a loss. It is on the card. **The member is now told the truth and told that the question is open, which is worse reading and better law than being told something the Code contradicts.**

**9. 71-M moves a clock's start from the Board's record to the Director's act, and the incidence moves with it.** *"Obtained"* dated a period running **against the Board** from an event only the Board's own logging evidences, and the member's day-for-day extension of her final qualifying retirement date turns on that date. The enacted words use subdivision D 3's **own** verb — *"The Director or his designee shall act upon the Board's request within 30 days"* — so the Board now bears the transit time. **That is a cost to the Board of up to a few days and a benefit to the member of the same; it is named because it is a cost, not because it is large.**

**10. 71-L and the three float patches repair a pointer, not a provision.** Fifteen record pointers in this document had been rendered as floating-point numbers by a prior run's script. All fifteen are repaired. **None of them is in the bill except the one in §1's editorial note, which is expressly not part of the bill**, and none carried a recoverable numeral, so each now names the revision or the §0 record in words rather than inventing a number. **A pointer that points nowhere is better replaced by a true sentence than by a guess.**

---

#### Two findings declined, with reasons, so neither is re-run as new

**MOO-210 R10 — declined.** Edit 1's first limb deems *"a retirement … is not a retirement before the date of death"*, a noun phrase the carve-out does not contain, so the whole cure rests on limb 2. **The reviewer calls it harmless as drafted and I agree.** Deleting it would be a change to words the Policy Director approved on 12 October for no operative gain, and R1's answer may replace the entire sentence. **The finding's real value is its warning, which is recorded here: a single limb carries the whole cure, so any future narrowing of limb 2 leaves nothing standing.**

**MOO-210 R11 — declined, and the reviewer's own view is that the result is right.** Where a member retired in one non-qualifying retirement dated before her death and a second dated after it, limb 2 deems her not to have retired only *"in such a retirement"*, and the earlier one still makes *"the member has not retired before the date of death"* false. **She did retire**, and § 51.1-165 F contemplates a second retirement after a return to covered employment, so the configuration is possible and the outcome is the retiree regime, which is what MOO-161's and MOO-169's fatal findings were cured to achieve. **Disposed of deliberately rather than by accident, which is what the finding asked for.**

---

#### Two corrections to the passes

**MOO-200's F7 is withdrawn from the record on MOO-210's R4, and the withdrawal is the Director's to confirm.** F7 held the greater-of comparison to be surplusage because *"the comparison changes no amount for anyone"* in either branch of subdivision C 2 of § 51.1-165. **Store revision 69's f4-a made the branch F7 showed never wins win, mandatorily**, and forbade the branch that does. So F7's conclusion is no longer true of the live text. **The pending card's options (4) and (5) were written on F7's premise and are therefore replaced rather than carried**, together with their companion F6.

**MOO-210's R9 is right that store revision 69's sweep counted the wrong token, and the correction is narrower than the finding states.** *"Subsection L"* does occur **zero** times in §1 and zero times in §2; what §2 keys its table on is **"§ 51.1-142.4 L"**, which occurs once and which the token never tested. So the **count** held and the **inference** did not. 71-P1 and 71-P2 repair §2; the sweep's method is repaired here, as a rule: **a sweep must test the token the document actually uses, and a count is evidence only for the token it counted.**

### §6.0-70 Ambiguities resolved in store revision 70 — and three defects recorded rather than cured

Every word enacted here is a word the Policy Director approved verbatim in the disposition at [MOO-191](/MOO/issues/MOO-191) and commissioned at [MOO-208](/MOO/issues/MOO-208). Nothing is added. Where I think something further is needed it is in this note or on a §7 card, not in the text.

1. **Why *"that the Board advises the member to review"* and not *"that the member is advised to review."*** F9, seven characters. **§6.0-65 item 1 gave a good pragmatic answer and a bad drafting answer**, and the Director has said so of their own approved words. The carrier sentence does name the Board — *"The Board shall not accept an election until the Board has, in writing, certified to the member … that …"* — so the actor was never missing from the subdivision. But the **item read alone was self-verifying**, true merely because the Board stated it, and it was the only one of seven that was; the other six state what the Code provides and are true or false independently of who writes them down. **Name the actor in every duty is my own rule and it applies to words I drafted.** *"Advises"* is preferred to *"recommends"* or *"requests"* because it is the Board's own register and imposes nothing: a member who does not take advice has not failed to do anything. Every no-duty property item 1 defends is intact, and item 1 is marked in place.
2. **Why *"before completing payment"* is kept **and** the power to re-designate is stated.** The terminus was never the defect; the defect was the inference a reader draws from it standing alone — that completing payment does something to the designation — when **nothing in § 51.1-162 A fixes a designation at that moment.** Both clauses are kept deliberately: the terminus is the prompt to act now, delivered at the one moment the member is thinking about the purchase, and the new clause removes the implicature that the window shuts then. Read together they say do it now, and know you can change it later. **On the accuracy of the new clause, which is the highest-risk proposition in the item:** *"may make or change that designation under subsection A of that section at any time before the member's death"* is a **correct reading of A and an inference from A's structure, not a quotation of an express grant.** A fixes no moment, and A's own fallback expressly contemplates a later designation — *"and another designation has not been made."* The words are tied to A so the statement follows A if A is ever amended rather than freezing a paraphrase; **— CORRECTED, store revision 72: that is true of the citation and not of the timing.** [MOO-211](/MOO/issues/MOO-211) M2. This item is right that the temporal proposition is **an inference from A's structure and not a quotation**, and it follows from exactly that that the proposition does **not** track an amendment to A: if A were amended to fix a moment of irrevocability, D 1 would still compel *"at any time before the member's death."* **The citation carries the power; it does not carry the timing.** Counsel's S3 and S5 are the two cures, and neither is enacted; and A's *"in a manner prescribed by the Board"* governs the **manner**, not the **time**, so a Board procedure purporting to close the window would be contrary to A and would not make the certification false. **No amendment to § 51.1-162 A is needed and none is made.** §5.47 direction 1, rows 1 and 3.
3. **Why the item opens with the full citation and then uses *"that section"* twice inside itself.** §6.0-65 item 3's reason has lapsed with the move — the advisory is now last and the item before it names *"subsection B of § 51.1-162"*, so the short form would have resolved. The full citation is kept for a reason that item did not need: **the item now carries three propositions and shifts to *"that section"* twice inside its own span, so it must supply its own referent and not borrow a neighbour's.** The last item in a series is also the one most likely to be lifted out of it by a certification template, and an item that carries its own citation survives that. Item 3 is marked in place.
4. **Why item 7 of 7, and why it costs nothing.** F4. The Director's reasons at MOO-188 §3.3 and §6.0-65 item 2 were that the advice must be its own semicolon item and visibly advice. **At item 7 of 7 it is still its own item, still introduced by *"that"*, still the only item in a different register — and it is no longer interleaved among the six propositions of law it must not be read as part of.** What the move adds: it stops splitting item 4's forward reference to *"subsection C of that section"* from the item that restates that citation in full, and it sits beside the subsection A proposition it is actually about. **The relocation itself is zero characters, verified and not assumed** — the seven items are undesignated clauses of one sentence and nothing in the act refers to any of them by position (§5.47 direction 2, check 7). On the objection that the least legally substantive item takes the most emphatic position, the Director treats that as a feature and as part of the answer to F5: the closing position is where a reader looks for what to do next. Item 2 is marked in place, reversed as to position and not as to either reason.
5. **F3, form D3c conformed — and the defect it leaves, which is the one thing in this revision I would ask the Director to look at first. Serious.** On **form** the Director's reasons are the drafter's: D3a asserts *"is not a failure for the purposes of subsection M"* in the sentence after the one deeming it a failure; D3b cures that but buys a reference by position, *"the advice described in the next sentence"*, which breaks the day anyone inserts a sentence between them. **D3c carves the exception into the deeming clause itself and names the matter in the subdivision's own words**, so there is no undefined *"advice"* and no reference by position. **Conforming it to F9's wording is not a style choice:** unconformed it would have described *"a failure to state that the member is advised to review…"* while the subdivision required *"that the Board advises the member to review…"*, and the exception would have excepted nothing while the J limb reported nothing. One phrase now does the work in both limbs, which is why it appears exactly three times in §1.
   **What it does not do. Subdivision M 2 does not look at D 1, and three features of the act's own words settle that rather than leave it arguable.**
   - M 2's first sentence reaches *"a failure of the Board to state in a certification or notice a matter this section requires to be stated in it"* on its own terms. The advisory is a matter this subdivision requires to be stated and this subdivision is part of this section.
   - M 2's clock for a failure to state runs 30 days from the certification *"or, where the provision that requires the matter to be stated provides a longer or later period, within that period."* **M 2 therefore anticipates that a provision like D 1 will extend it** — which is exactly what D 1's bespoke clock does: 30 days from the certification for the member, 30 days from the subdivision G 4 notice for a claimant who is not the member. D 1's sentence is **conferring, not constituting**, and an exception to a conferring sentence confers less.
   - §1 carries **six** *"for the purposes of subsection M"* clauses; the other five are all of the declaratory form *"X is a determination of the Board for the purposes of subsection M"*, and **M 2's second sentence says in terms that its own reach operates *"whether or not another provision of this section expressly provides for review of it."*** The act's pattern is that these clauses restate M 2's reach rather than create it.

   **So substitution 4 withdraws D 1's extension and leaves M 2's own channel open.** The member keeps reconsideration on a 30-day clock from the certification; the claimant who is not the member loses the G 4-notice clock, **the only one a survivor could ever have used**; and M 2's second sentence, which D 1 had disapplied for the member, applies again. That is close to the opposite of the trade F3 was taken for.
   **Why it is enacted anyway, and what I did instead of curing it.** The words are the Director's approved words, the cure is a provision nobody has agreed, and **no cure inside D 1 can work** — the defect is that M 2 does not look at D 1, so the clause has to go in M 2, which this commission did not open. It is drafted at **§7 item 135** and **not enacted**. Substitution 5, the J limb, is unaffected and does the whole of what it was taken to do: subdivision J 1's catch-all — *"every other matter this section requires the Board to state in that report"* — picks it up with no conforming amendment. §5.47 direction 2, checks 1 and 2. §6.0-65 item 6 is marked in place, because its heading is no longer the law of the draft.
6. **The F3 residue, and the asymmetry it creates with the re-scoped §7 item 133.** The Director named the residue: D3c's exception names only the **first** of item 7's three propositions, so omitting the power-to-re-designate clause or the no-fault clause stays inside D 1's deeming, and the Director left that to counsel's next pass rather than splitting one item three ways. **What follows from it, and the Director's words do not reach, is this.** F7's misstatement limb at §7 item 133 is re-scoped on the Director's instruction to **the six propositions of law**. If it were ever enacted in that scope, **misstating** the re-designation clause or the no-fault clause would be unreached while **omitting** them stays reviewable — the mirror image of the gap F7 exists to close. **Scoping the limb strictly to the six propositions and no further is not draftable on the present text:** the seven items are undesignated clauses of one sentence, so there is no handle to cite, and the only clean handle would be designating them (i) through (vii), a renumbering this commission forbids and a separate edit. **So item 133's re-scoped words use the same exception phrase the enacted text now uses**, which reaches the six propositions *and* item 7's other two, and the difference between that and the Director's instruction is recorded in the item itself. **Recorded, not drafted around, as directed.** **— CORRECTED, store revision 72: there is no residue, because this item counted the propositions wrong.** [MOO-211](/MOO/issues/MOO-211) M1. After F4 and F1/F2/F6/F9, **item 7 carries one advisory and two propositions of law** — the power to re-designate and the no-fault clause — so **the certification series carries eight propositions of law and one advisory, not six and one.** The Director's instruction, *"the six propositions of law"*, is therefore **under-inclusive by two**, and the drafted scope *"every matter other than the advice"* reaches all eight and excepts the one, **which is exactly what the instruction intended.** The asymmetry this item records — that misstating the re-designation clause or the no-fault clause would be unreached while omitting them stayed reviewable — **was an artefact of the miscount and does not arise on the drafted words.** So: **no residue to cure, no three-way split to consider, and the (i)–(vii) renumbering this commission forbade is not needed for anything.** Item 133's words stand unchanged and its description is corrected. **Item 133's own premise is also narrowed by the same pass:** most misstatements decompose into omissions already inside D 1's failure sentence — including item 133's own worked example, a template that states the required matter not at all — so **the real F7 gap is additive misstatement only**, which is rarer than a template rewrite and worth knowing before 253 characters are spent on it.
7. **What is not drafted, and it is not silence.** **F5 is held and the acknowledgment form is not to be drafted** — the Director's direction, because it moves the advisory toward a member obligation and MOO-188 §3.3 avoided that deliberately. **No route is drafted**: § 51.1-162 A already locates the manner of designation in Board procedure, D5 would have compelled an administrative particular into a statute where it will go stale, and it would have added a matter inside D 1 so that the failure sentence reached it — enlarging, with administrative detail, exactly the channel F3 was taken to narrow. **The route question goes to the subsection J report**: if the pilot's own reports show the advice being buried, that is the evidence on which to require a route, and it will be evidence rather than a guess. Noted for the Editor's risk section. §6.0-65 item 7 is marked in place and confirmed. **F7 is not cut. Subsection E1 is not re-cut**, the **[MOO-141](/MOO/issues/MOO-141)** patch is not applied, **[MOO-176](/MOO/issues/MOO-176) revision 6 is not touched** (and see item 11), and **[MOO-97](/MOO/issues/MOO-97) §7 item 129's other answers stay open.**
8. **The title line — T2, and why both phrases and in that order.** The commission did not reach the title line; **the Director extended it expressly, as the commissioner**, and that is recorded here because an edit outside the commission as received should never rest on inference. **The ripeness is store revision 67's doing**: that revision opened the title to add *"and 51.1-162"*, the act now amends a death-benefit section on its face, and D 1 compels seven statements about the benefit payable on the member's death and about the designation of a beneficiary — while the *"relating to"* clause named none of them. Va. Const. art. IV, § 12; **CORRECTED, store revision 72: *City of Richmond v. Pace*, 127 Va. 274, 103 S.E. 647 (1920)** is the authority a reader of the memo will want, and *~~Dean v. Paolicelli~~* — cited here and at §5.47 row 7 — **has no title holding**. [MOO-211](/MOO/issues/MOO-211) M5. **Under *Pace* the title need only give notice of the general subject and of the interests likely to be affected, so T2 was not a save: the death-benefit provisions were already germane to the stated object, and T2 is simply the better title.** **T2 over T1** because the object the act now embraces is not only the payment of a death benefit but **the member's designation of one**, which is what all seven statements are about; naming only *"death benefits"* would have given notice of six of them. **Placement:** after *"pilot program"* and before *"post-retirement employment; reports"*, following the clause's existing order — the purchase and its cost, the pilot, then the death-benefit and designation consequences, then the post-retirement employment conditions and the reports. **Style:** semicolon-separated noun phrases, lower case, no article, matching the six already there.
9. **A post-condition arithmetic I corrected rather than reported as met.** The Director required §1 at **131,066 → 131,656 (+590)** and the document at **449,196 → 449,830 (+634)**, on the stated footing that substitution 6 *"adds +44 to the document outside §1."* **It is not outside §1.** The bill's title line sits under `### Title`, and that heading is inside §1 as §1 is bounded in this document — the *"## 1. The bill"* heading to the next second-level heading. The Director's +590 is the sum of substitutions 1 to 5 measured on the §1 slice before the title line was touched, which is how the verification script reported it. **So §1's measured movement is +634, and the apply kit asserts the corrected delta and would have aborted on the stated one.** The absolute figures are spent in any event, store revision 69 having landed between the disposition and this cut; the deltas carry over. No other post-condition is affected. **A post-condition that cannot be met as stated is a defect in the post-condition, and saying so is cheaper than meeting it by redefining §1.**
10. **Why this is one revision and not six cuts.** The disposition priced every candidate against the live base and never against another candidate's output. **D1, D2b, D6 and D9 share one FIND, and D3c writes D9's FIND into the subdivision twice more**, so D9's anchor goes from 1× to 3×; in the other order F3 would have been enacted and inert in both limbs with nothing failing. **The general lesson for the next pass: price a candidate against the output of every candidate taken with it, not only against the base.** §0.0-70.
11. **A dead anchor found while verifying the scope held — [MOO-176](/MOO/issues/MOO-176) revision 6's Edit 1, and this revision did not cause it.** Edit 1's FIND ends *"…from the giving of that statement or notice is extended by three days.</u>"*; the live provision reads *"…from the giving of that statement**, notice, or certification** is extended by three days.</u>"*, widened by a revision later than MOO-176 revision 6. **0× on the base, 0× after the composite; Edits 2 and 3 are 1× before and 1× after.** The re-anchored string is at §5.47 direction 2, check 9. **I have not re-cut it**, because MOO-176 revision 6 is expressly out of scope — but it cannot be applied as written, and whoever owns it should know before they try.

**Open for the Policy Director, in order of weight.**

1. **§7 item 135 — the subsection M 2 clause.** F3 as enacted narrows D 1's extension of subsection M and does not close subsection M, and it removes the survivor's subdivision G 4-notice clock while doing so. One clause in M 2's first sentence cures it, generically, so any future exception works the same way. It opens a provision this commission did not open. **Take it, leave it, or send it to counsel — but the record should not sit with F3 described as taken and the channel described as closed.**
2. **The scope of §7 item 133 as re-scoped** — item 6 above. The instruction was *"the six propositions of law"*; the draftable scope is *"every matter other than the advice"*, which reaches the six **and** item 7's other two propositions. ~~Strict scoping needs the series designated (i) to (vii).~~ **CORRECTED, store revision 72 — there is nothing to scope around.** [MOO-211](/MOO/issues/MOO-211) M1: item 7 carries two propositions of law beside the advisory, so **the series carries eight and the instruction's "six" was under-inclusive by two.** The drafted scope is exactly right and **no renumbering is needed.**
3. **Whether the residue at item 6 should be closed by widening the exception to all three of item 7's propositions**, left to counsel's next pass and interacting with both of the above.
4. **The ceiling, which is now a live constraint and not a note.** With its record layer this revision landed at **527,893** characters against the **524,288** limit and could not write until **§5.38 and §5.39 were moved verbatim** to the retained-record document — the rotation §0.1 sets and the move the §5 paragraph has named since store revision 58. Only the §5 half was performed; **§6.0-59 and §6.0-60 remain and are the next to go.** The document closes at about **9,000** characters of headroom, which is roughly one more revision of this size. **The structural answer, when the Director wants it, is that §5 and §6 should hold the current entry and one predecessor and nothing else** — the retention rule as written lets each section grow until a revision is blocked, which means the blocked revision pays the cost of every revision before it.

**Internal working draft. Not a Division of Legislative Services bill, not cleared by counsel or by DLS, not legally sufficient for introduction. Pre-decisional and internal.**

### §6.0-69 Ambiguities resolved in store revision 69 — and one residual declined with its reason

1. **Whether to enact the Director's approved words as given.** f1-b's words are the reviewer's own, quoted verbatim on the card and approved verbatim. **They are reproduced word for word and two conforming limbs are added to them.** The test I applied is the one this document has applied to every approved sentence: a change that makes the approved words operate as approved is conformance and belongs in the text with a note; a change that moves a cost or settles a question the card put to the Director is not mine to make. Both limbs are of the first kind, both are recorded at §0.0-69 item 1, and **either can be reversed in one substitution.**

2. **Why the exception is confined to a retirement *"that is not a qualifying retirement."*** The approved words were confined only by *"for the purposes of this subdivision."* Subdivision G 4 uses the phrase *"a retirement before the date of death"* for **two** populations — the member who retired in a retirement that is not a qualifying retirement, which is the carve-out's own subject, and the member who retired in a qualifying retirement, who is the subject of the branch's second condition. **Unconfined, the exception would have reached the second and pulled a retiree out of § 51.1-163 and into the death branch.** That is the class [MOO-161](/MOO/issues/MOO-161)'s F1 rated **fatal**, and the Policy Director's standing direction of 12 October 2026 exists to keep this sentence off it. The confinement is five words and it removes the argument rather than winning it.

3. **Why the second limb is needed and is not an enlargement.** The approved words make a statement about a **noun** — *"is not a retirement before the date of death."* The condition that lets the death branch operate is a **verb**: *"where clause (i) of this subdivision applies and **the member has not retired** before the date of death."* A reader could hold that a member whose effective date fell before her death *"has retired"* however the noun is defined. **The limb therefore does not widen the answer; without it the answer does not reach the class the card described.** It is drafted in the chapter's own formulation — *"shall not be treated as having"* — which subsection E and subdivision G 5 already use, so the act has one formulation for one concept and not two.

4. **One residual of f1-b, declined with its reason.** A disability retiree **already in payment** whose effective date the Board *re-determines* after her death is, on the words, a member whose effective date the Board determined after the death. **I have not drafted against it.** The better reading is that an effective date is *determined* once, when the retirement is approved, and that a later correction is not that determination; and the only drafted cure is **f1-a's** limb — *"and a retirement allowance has become payable to the member under that retirement"* — which is a limb of a form the Director considered and **did not** select. Taking it now would be re-deciding the card. It is named to the next counsel pass as the first thing to attack, and it is one substitution if the Director wants it.

5. **Why the non-coincident branch is a clause of the greater-of sentence and not a sentence of its own.** That sentence now contains **five** references to *"the preceding sentence"* where it contained two, and every one of them means the sentence that determines the benefit under § 51.1-162. **A new sentence would have given the references inside it a new antecedent — the greater-of sentence — and the branch would have been about the wrong benefit.** The semicolon keeps one referent for all five. **Any further amendment of this branch must stay inside that sentence for the same reason**, and that is recorded as a standing direction rather than left in this note.

6. **Two pronouns made express.** *"The benefit **it** provides"* becomes *"the benefit **that subdivision** provides"*, because *"it"* had two candidate antecedents in a sentence that decides who is paid; and the payee tail becomes *"shall pay it to **that person or those persons**"*, which is the set the new condition has just identified, in place of *"the person to whom the provision under which the greater benefit is determined requires it to be paid."* **Neither changes the rule.** The second is shorter because the condition now does the work the old formula did.

7. **No section is named to displace one.** The non-coincident branch directs the Board to pay what subdivision C 2 of § 51.1-165 provides and not to pay the benefit determined under the preceding sentence. **It names no section as disapplied and adds no *"notwithstanding."*** [MOO-96](/MOO/issues/MOO-96)'s F-4 — that every section this act names to displace is a section a reader of that section learns nothing from — is therefore not reopened, and the act's two existing displacements are not enlarged.

8. **Where the eligibility bar goes inside subsection B.** Between the sentence that makes an accepted election the only route to a purchase and the avoidance-of-doubt sentence: **grant, then bar, then clarification, then the voluntariness rule.** The alternative was the end of the subsection, after voluntariness, which puts two sentences between the grant and the bar on it. The bar is moved **word for word** and nothing but its place changes; the net character count of the move is **zero**.

9. **Subsection L is not rewritten.** What is left is the local-mandate disclaimer, which reads as a complete sentence on its own and was the whole of the subsection before the Director's answer f10-b put the bar there. **The smallest change that achieves the agreed policy is to take one sentence out, and that is what is done.**

10. **f11-authority is not drafted, and that is the answer and not an omission.** The Director's selection is an instruction to harden the *"complete in itself"* authority before the drafting request goes to Division of Legislative Services. It is a research question about Supreme Court of Virginia authority, the reviewer offered in terms to run it down, and **there is no provision to enact for it.** It is commissioned as an issue.

11. **The item-130 annotation is replaced and not supplemented.** Store revision 67's annotation records route (b) as **held**. The Director has now **declined** it. A record that carries both, with the stale one first, is worse than a record that carries one: the rule this document has applied since store revision 62 is that **a stale self-description is a defect of the record.** The annotation now states the declination, its ground, the adoption of route (c) in [MOO-164](/MOO/issues/MOO-164), and the one thing the Director's note corrects in my own inventory — that the subdivision D 4 / D 5 retention and the 15-day duty are **one sentence, and it is the sentence after clause (ii)**, so no amendment confined to clause (ii) could ever have reached either.

12. **Items 132, 133 and 134 are moved and not renumbered.** They were written after the *"### 7.1 Retained record"* subsection, so the live list stopped at 131 for any reader who trusted the caption. **Moving them is a correction of place and not of content:** not one word of any of the three changes, §7 is asserted contiguous **1 to 134** before and after, and every *"§7 item n"* reference in this document and in the retained record resolves exactly as it did. **This is the second occurrence in this document** — store revision 37 did it with items 59, 60 and 61 — so the kit now asserts that the last item of the live list precedes the §7.1 heading.

13. **What the non-selection of two answers does not decide.** **f12-ninety** is not enacted, so the § 51.1-162 C waiver floor stays at the thirtieth day after the notice is given, and the beneficiary noticed on day 85 still gets thirty days where a timely-noticed beneficiary got ninety. **f3-operative** is not enacted, so a member who elected the straight life option under subdivision A 1 of § 51.1-165 — *"an increased retirement allowance in lieu of any death benefits"* — may still have no death benefit for the subsidy to buy. **Both remain drafted word for word at §7 item 132 and neither is closed by this revision.** What is closed is f1-a, f1-c, f4-b and f4-c, each by the selection of its alternative, and each is marked as closed by selection rather than deleted.

### §6.0-67 Ambiguities resolved in store revision 67 — and three findings declined with reasons

1. **Why § 51.1-162 is reproduced in full, and not cited.** The approved conforming amendment inserts eleven words into § 51.1-162 C. Article IV, Section 12 of the Constitution of Virginia requires that *"the act revived or the section amended shall be reenacted and published at length."* **An amendment to a section is therefore not available as a cross-reference; it requires the section.** So the title line, the first enactment clause and §1 all change together, and the whole of § 51.1-162 — subsections A, B and C, the five-step order of precedence and the two allowance-measure paragraphs — appears in §1 with one underlined clause and nothing else marked. **This is the only reason the act's footprint widened**, and it is the price of making MOO-189's F11 visible to the people who administer § 51.1-162.
2. **Where the eleven words go inside § 51.1-162 C, which the approval did not state.** They are placed after *"within ninety days after the death of the member"* and before *"in order to make available"*, set off by commas, so that they qualify **the period** and not the right to waive. Placed after *"in order to make available a retirement allowance under the provisions of subsection B of this section"* they would have read as an exception to the *purpose* of the waiver; placed before *"by written notification"* they would have read as an exception to the *manner*. **Only the enacted placement says what subdivision G 4 does, which is to extend a period.**
3. **One word of f7-a is changed and the change is recorded rather than silent.** The approved tail read *"or a lump sum in lieu of **it**."* Enacted: *"or a lump sum in lieu of **any other benefit under that section**."* Two reasons. § 51.1-162 B's own words are *"in lieu of any other benefits under this section"*, and **subdivision G 4 already uses the longer form twice** for the same election; a third formulation for one concept inside one section is a worse defect than the length. And *"it"* had two candidate referents in its own sentence — the allowance and the amount settled. **The meaning is unchanged and the Director's answer is unaffected.**
4. **Why f7-a bars the payment rather than deferring the settlement.** Subdivision G 4 settles the certified subsidy *"on … the date of the member's death"*, and the settlement date is what the whole redetermination machinery runs from. Deferring the **settlement** would have moved that date and with it the ninety-day redetermination, the release, the crediting deadline and the cap accounting. **f7-a leaves the settlement where it is and bars only the cash**, which is the smallest change that makes subdivision G 4's existing release operable.
5. **Why m8-a's ninety days is an aggregate and not a per-request limit.** Clause (ii) already caps *"the aggregate of all tolling under this subsection"*, and clause (i) already denies tolling to a second or subsequent request. **A per-request limit would therefore have been either redundant or an invitation to read clause (i) down.** The enacted words cap *"the aggregate of all periods so excluded"*, which is the same unit clause (ii) already uses.
6. **Why F3's repair corrects the certification instead of enacting the proposition.** The reviewer offered an operative disapplication of § 51.1-165 A. **That would take from a member the bargain she made** — under subdivision A 1 she elected an increased allowance in her own lifetime *"in lieu of any death benefits"*, and disapplying it would pay her survivors out of the trust fund for a trade she was paid for. **That decides who bears a loss and it is on the card.** What a drafter may do, and must, is stop the act compelling the Board to state as law something that is not. **The repair is also narrower than the finding**, verified at source on 12 October 2026: § 51.1-165 A 2 carries its own carve-out for a death before the effective date, A 3 says nothing about death benefits, and no option in § 51.1-165.01 extinguishes one. **Subdivision A 1 is the whole of the exposure**, and the certification now names it.
7. **Why F6 narrows the disapplication rather than adding a window.** The reviewer offered either. Adding a window for the non-member would have been a **second** express formula for the same review right; narrowing the disapplication **restores subdivision M 2's second sentence**, which already deems a certification a determination *"whether or not another provision of this section expressly provides for review of it"* and already carries the third window. **The smaller change is the one that leaves one route and not two.**
8. **Why F8's cure amends the definition and not the three windows that run from a certification.** *"Given"* is defined once, in subsection A, for a *"statement or notice."* Three places in the act run a period from a **certification**. Amending subsection A fixes all three at once and keeps one definition; amending the three would have left the definition still not reaching a certification. **Three occurrences of *"statement or notice"* are changed and eleven are not**, because the eleven name a particular statement or notice required by the definition of *"final qualifying retirement date"* and are not the defining rule. §5.45 direction 1.
9. **Why F9's window runs from three different events and not one.** Subdivision M 2's lateness limb reaches three failures — to make a determination, to give a notice, and to make a payment. **A single trigger cannot serve all three**: a late *payment* has no notice, and a late *determination* is an internal act the person never sees. The enacted words run the thirty days from the notice of the determination where the failure is a late determination, and from the giving of the notice or the making of the payment where it is one of the other two. **In every case the trigger is something the person adversely affected can observe.**
10. **Why F13's repair also carries § 51.1-162 A's offset, which the finding rated lower.** The finding is that the certification states one trigger for the order of precedence where the Code states two. The same sentence also omitted that the contributions are *"reduced by the amount of any retirement allowance previously received by the member."* **Both are corrected in one substitution**, because a member reading a compelled written statement of what her family will be paid should not be told a gross figure, and because re-opening the same clause in a later revision to add the second half is two changes where one will do.
11. **Why F14's repair cites the definition instead of completing the list.** Completing the enumeration would have put eight categories in subsection L and left two statements of one rule. **Citing the definition leaves one.** The reviewer's other option was deleting the sentence, which is not available: the sentence is the Policy Director's approved answer **f10-b**, enacted in store revision 62 to confirm the scope on the face of the act. **Placement is not changed** — the sentence stays in subsection L, where f10-b put it — and the reviewer's point that a state-system eligibility bar sits inside a local-mandate disclaimer survives as a card for the Director, not a drafting fix.
12. **Three findings declined, each with its reason.**
    - **F1, fatal — declined as a drafting fix and carded.** The branch does not reach a member who dies with a disability-retirement application pending whose effective date the Board then back-dates before the death. **The defect is in the pre-existing carve-out, not in store revision 62's words**, and every cure is a third form of the condition on that sentence. The Policy Director's standing direction of 12 October ([`standing-drafting-directions`](/MOO/issues/MOO-97#document-standing-drafting-directions) Part A1) forecloses two forms and the reviewer was told that a defect needing a third is a card. **Resolving a fatal finding by drafting choice is the one thing this role may not do.** Two forms are drafted on the card.
    - **F4 — declined and carded.** Subdivision G 4's savings clause and its greater-of cannot both stand where the two provisions name different payees. **Whichever way it is resolved, a named person is paid or is not**, and the cure the reviewer prefers pays both, which costs the trust fund. **The sentence is internally contradictory and this role says so and stops.**
    - **F12 — declined and carded, on the reviewer's own words**: *"That is your call, not mine."* Whether the § 51.1-162 C floor should be thirty days after a late notice or ninety is a policy setting about how much deliberation a survivor gets.
13. **Two findings answered without a change to the text.** **F11**'s constitutional half is answered by the conforming amendment itself, which removes the hidden-override risk rather than arguing about it; its reviewer's express limit — that controlling *"complete in itself"* authority was not located within the pass — is carried into the drafting request to Division of Legislative Services. **F16's plural *"limitations"*** is declined: the parallel sentence in subdivision G 4 uses the same plural, no failure scenario turns on it, and changing one of two parallel sentences would create an asymmetry where there is now a consistency. **F17** is a finding on the record and not on the text; it is corrected at item 14.
14. **A correction to this file, from F17.** §0.0-62 and §6.0-62 describe store revision 62's condition on the death-benefit sentence as *"the negative of the carve-out clause (i) already states."* **It is not.** Clause (i)'s carve-out is *"has retired in a retirement that is **not a qualifying retirement** before the date of death"*; the enacted condition is *"has not retired before the date of death"*, which drops the qualification and is **stronger** than the carve-out's negative. The reviewer's reduction holds: the branch applies where the member *died and had not retired at all*. **The enacted words are the ones the Policy Director verified and adopted and they are not changed.** What is wrong is the description, and a future reviewer who relied on it would reason wrongly about the qualifying-retiree case. **The description is corrected here and is not corrected in place in §0.0-62, because that record is a dated record of what was written.**
15. **One thing a VRS administrator should be told and the act does not say.** Subdivision G 4 routes a notice to a *"person under a disability as defined in § 8.01-2"* and that definition includes, at subdivision 6 (a), *"a person convicted of a felony during the period he is confined."* **So an incarcerated beneficiary is a person under a disability** whose notice must go to a fiduciary, and ordinarily has none — which the clause's own fallback resolves (*"where there is none, to the person to whom the Board pays or would pay the benefit"*). **No change is made**; the adoption of the statutory standard is worth more than the odd case, and the fallback is why the odd case is administrable. It is in the Editor's risk list.

### §6.0-66 Ambiguities resolved in store revision 66

1. **Why the deeming sentence and not the alternative affirmative trigger, and the one weakness I accepted in choosing it.** MOO-196 left the form to me and recommended the insertion; I took the recommendation for the four reasons at §0.0-66, of which the controlling one is that **deeming the commanded release *into* subdivision D 7 keeps the second limb alive** — a release that followed a determination later rescinded or reversed still comes back, where an exclusion would have stranded it. **The weakness is that `For the purposes of the next sentence` is a reference by position, and position is the one thing a later amendment moves for free.** Anyone who inserts a sentence between the deeming sentence and the reinstatement sentence silently re-points the whole provision, and nothing in the text would complain. I accepted it because it is **the only device that confines the deeming to one sentence without naming the sentence**, and naming it (*"the sentence beginning 'Where a reservation has been released'"*) is both ugly and just as fragile — it breaks if the sentence's first words change. **The durable fix is structural: give the reinstatement sentence its own numbered subdivision, so the deeming can cite a designator instead of a position.** That renumbers subdivision G 4, which is a change of footprint nobody has agreed, so it is raised here and not drafted. **A drafter amending subdivision G 4 after this revision must read the deeming sentence first.**
2. **Why *"required or permitted"* and not *"required"* alone.** The releases that caused the defect are all commanded, so *"required"* would have been enough for every case I could find. I used the pair anyway because **a release the act permits without commanding would otherwise have fallen into the 60-day *"learns of the release"* limb by the same mechanism, and the omission would have been invisible** — exactly the way the original defect was invisible. The pair is not new vocabulary: subsection F already reads *"Nothing **required or permitted** by subdivision G 2, G 3, G 4, or G 5."* **The act's own words, used for the act's own idea.**
3. **Why *"including"* and not an exhaustive list, and why I named three releases when the cut reaches four.** The deeming sentence's general words are *"a release that this section required or permitted the Board to make at the time it was made"*; the three named — subdivision G 1, this subdivision, subdivision D 3 on settlement — are counsel's and are **illustrative**. That is load-bearing, because **there is a fourth commanded release the finding does not list: subdivision G 4's death-case release** (*"the Board shall release the amount settled under this subdivision and shall restore it to the amount available under the limitation stated in the first sentence of subdivision D 2"*). It is reached by the general words and by *"by this subdivision"* in the list. **Had I converted the list into an exhaustive one, I would have re-created the defect for that case and for any release a later amendment adds.** *"Including"* is doing real work here and should not be tidied away.
4. **Why the five-percent floor keeps *"an employer"* when everything around it becomes *"state agency"* — the one place I deliberately left two words for what may look like one concept.** My own rule is never to use two words for one concept, and this is a considered exception, not an oversight. **They are two concepts.** The floor sentence disqualifies a **class of funds** — *"funds other than funds paid by an employer to or on behalf of the member"* — and its purpose is to stop the member's own five percent being paid with somebody else's money. **The § 51.1-124.3 term is wider than *"state agency"*, and in an anti-circumvention floor the wider class is the protective one.** Conforming it would have **narrowed the disqualification and loosened the floor**, which is a substantive policy change and squarely the thing MOO-196 told me not to make. The second and later sentences name an **actor with a duty**, and there the term must be the narrow, defined, real one. So: *"employer"* where the question is *whose money*, *"state agency"* where the question is *who must act*. **If the Policy Director would rather have one word, the way to get it is to say so and accept the narrower floor; I will not make that trade by drafting choice.**
5. **The publication duty still has no deadline and no consequence, and I did not add one.** Subdivision B 4 e now ends *"The state agency shall publish the policy"* — a named actor and a real duty, which is what finding A2b asked for and what the cut delivers. **It is still missing two of the four things a duty needs: by when, and or else.** Nothing says where, in what form, or by what date the agency publishes, and nothing follows if it never does — unlike the *filing* duty, whose consequence is built in (the Board does not accept elections by that agency's employees until the policy is filed). **The obvious repair is to tie publication to the filing moment and give it the same consequence**, for instance *"shall publish the policy on its website no later than the date it files the policy with the Board, and the Board shall not accept an election by an employee of a state agency that has not published its policy."* **That is a new provision and nobody has agreed it, so it is raised here and not drafted in.** It is one sentence whenever the Director wants it.
6. **Why *"state agency from whose appropriation an amount was paid"* and not *"state agency that paid."*** Counsel suggested these words and I used them, but the reason is worth recording because the shorter form is tempting. **A state agency does not have its own money; it spends from an appropriation.** *"That paid"* invites the question of who paid when an agency is reorganised, abolished or merged between the payment and the refund, and the answer *"the agency from whose appropriation it came"* is the one that identifies a real fund and a real claimant. It also makes *"in proportion to the amounts each paid"* determinate where a member moved between agencies mid-purchase. **The cost is length: 38 characters each, ten times, which is 380 of the revision's 825.** Worth it, because the phrase is the thing that makes the refund and review limbs administrable.
7. **What I did not draft, and none of it is silence.** (a) **No statewide single policy.** MOO-196 offered the alternative of naming an officer or agency to adopt one policy for the Commonwealth, with the Department of Human Resource Management the obvious candidate, and a provision that a state agency may pay only in accordance with it. **I drafted the per-agency form because it is the one MOO-196 recommended and the one that needs no new institution**; the statewide form is a different policy with a different queue (one order of precedence across all of state government, not one per agency) and real distributive consequences. **If the Lab wants it, it is not a redraft of B 4 e but a new provision, and the fiscal consequences of a single statewide queue should go to Fiscal & Impact Modeler before it is drafted.** (b) **Subdivision B 4 e is not extended beyond the Program.** Its policy condition is keyed to *"employees who are eligible members as defined in § 51.1-142.4"*, which only a state agency can ever have, so as drafted it cannot reach a purchase outside the Program. **That is a footprint question, not a drafting one.** (c) **The four curly apostrophes in §1 are not normalised.** MOO-196 offered it as a separate minor tidy; the cut did not need it, because the anchor was chosen to stop before the apostrophe, and doing it would have put a cosmetic change in a revision that should be readable as two findings and nothing else.
8. **An open question for the Policy Director that the cut exposed and did not create.** The deeming sentence fixes the *trigger*, so an ordinary commanded release no longer drags the Board through reinstatement, the money-committee notice, the excess certification and the shutdown. **It does not touch the *consequence* side, and one piece of that still reads oddly for the cases that legitimately do come back:** the compelled notice sentence requires the Board to state *"the nature of any error"* on **every** reinstatement, including one that follows a rescission where the Board did nothing wrong at all. *"Any"* arguably lets the Board state that there was none, which is how I read it and why I did not touch it. **It is one word (*"any error"* → *"any error"* qualified, or *"the nature of any error, where the release was in error"*), it was not in MOO-196's scope, and it should be decided rather than drafted by me.** Raised here for the Director; counsel's next bounded pass will see it either way.

### §6.0-65 Ambiguities resolved in store revision 65

1. **Why *"is advised to review"* and not *"should review"*, and why neither is *"shall review."*** The series it joins is six propositions of law, each introduced by *"that"*, and a seventh item reading *"that the member **should** review"* would have read as a statement of what the law requires of the member. **It is not a duty, and it must not be capable of being read as one** — a member who does not review her designation breaks nothing, loses no right and is not late for anything. *"Is advised to review"* states the only thing that is true: the Board's certification carries advice. **The operative verb belongs to the Board's carrier sentence** — *"The Board shall not accept an election until the Board has, in writing, certified to the member … that …"* — so the actor, the addressee, the writing and the moment are all already named, and the advisory adds no unnamed actor. This is the Policy Director's wording change at MOO-188 §3.3 and the reason is recorded there. **Superseded as to the words, store revision 70 — the item no longer reads *"is advised to review."*** The Policy Director has taken [MOO-195](/MOO/issues/MOO-195) F9 at [MOO-208](/MOO/issues/MOO-208) and the item now reads *"that the Board advises the member to review."* **Everything this item says about the operative verb survives the change; its conclusion does not.** Naming the Board inside the item is the better drafting answer, because the item read alone was self-verifying — true because the Board states it — and was the only one of seven that was. *"Advises"* is the Board's own verb, it imposes nothing on the member, and every no-duty property this item defends is intact. §6.0-70 item 1.
2. **Why it is its own semicolon item and not an appendage to the § 51.1-162 B proposition.** The designation is subsection B's fact, which is the argument for putting the advice inside B's item, and it is the wrong argument: **B's item states what the law does, and the advisory states what the member is advised to do.** Merging them would have buried advice inside a proposition of law and made it arguable that the advice is part of what B provides. Separate, the propositions stay together and the advice is visibly advice. **Reversed as to position, and not as to either reason, store revision 70.** The Policy Director has taken [MOO-195](/MOO/issues/MOO-195) F4 at [MOO-208](/MOO/issues/MOO-208) and the advisory is now item **7 of 7** and no longer item 5. **Both reasons this item gives survive the move and are served better by it**: at the end of the series the advice is still its own semicolon item, still introduced by *"that"*, still the only item in a different register, and it is no longer interleaved among the six propositions of law it must not be read as part of. What the move adds is that it stops splitting item 4's forward reference to *"subsection C of that section"* from the item that restates that citation in full. §6.0-70 item 4.
3. **Why the full citation *"subsection A of § 51.1-162"* and not *"subsection A of that section."*** §1 uses the short form three times, and each time inside a sentence that has already named § 51.1-162. The advisory is a **new item** and the item that follows it restarts with the full citation (*"that subsection C of § 51.1-162"*), so the short form would have made the referent depend on an item two places back. §5.43 direction 1. **The reason changes with the position; the result does not, store revision 70.** The advisory is now the last item and the item before it names *"subsection B of § 51.1-162"*, so the short form would now have resolved. The full citation is kept anyway, for a reason this item did not need: the item now carries three propositions and shifts to *"that section"* twice inside itself, so it must supply its own referent and not borrow a neighbour's. §6.0-70 item 3.
4. **Why *"designation of beneficiary"* is the right noun and not a second word for one concept.** § 51.1-162 A, verified at source on 12 October 2026, requires each member to *"designate who is to receive a refund of accumulated contributions"*, pays *"the designated beneficiary"*, and calls the act *"the designation"*; B pays *"the person or persons designated as provided in subsection A."* **The advisory therefore points the member at the cited subsection using that subsection's own noun**, while §1's own *"designation"* carries the same meaning it already carries.
5. **Why *"before completing payment"* and not a date, a period, or *"before the Board accepts the election."*** The phrase is already in this subdivision — the certification states *"the member's right to cancel the election under subdivision G 5 at any time before completing payment"* — and in subdivision G 4. **The advisory's deadline is the same event, and it is the last moment at which the purchase can still be undone.** **Correction, store revision 68 — the second reason this item gave is not true and is withdrawn.** As written at store revision 65 the item continued *"and the whole window during which re-designating changes the outcome."* **Nothing in § 51.1-162 A fixes a designation at the completion of payment.** A's own words contemplate redesignation at any time — *"If no designation has been made, or the death of the designated person occurs prior to the death of the member and another designation has not been made"* — so the window in which re-designating changes the § 51.1-162 outcome runs to the earlier of the member's death and the last of the act's retirement-date legs: about 27 months on the act's own dials (subsection E's 12 months to pay and 1 to credit, and the two retirement-date definitions in subsection A), and without end for a member who does not retire. *"Before completing payment"* closes the **first** of those legs. **Store revision 69 — the deadline stands, and the false implicature it carried is cured by words that now follow it.** The Policy Director has taken [MOO-195](/MOO/issues/MOO-195) F2 in form D2b at [MOO-208](/MOO/issues/MOO-208): *"before completing payment"* is kept as the prompt to act now, and the item then states that the member may make or change the designation under subsection A of § 51.1-162 *"at any time before the member's death."* Read together they say do it now, and know you can change it later. **The enacted deadline was never the defect; the inference a reader could draw from it standing alone was.** §6.0-70 item 2. [MOO-195](/MOO/issues/MOO-195) F2, verified at source on 12 October 2026. **The enacted deadline is the Policy Director's approved word and is not changed by this record revision**; whether to extend it, re-point it or leave it stand is before the Director at [MOO-191](/MOO/issues/MOO-191) with the words drafted. A fixed period would have needed a trigger, a definition and a consequence for missing it, and there is no consequence, because there is no duty.
6. **Why the advisory is not excepted from D 1's failure sentence, and what it costs.** **The Policy Director decided this at MOO-188 §3.4 before the cut, and I have not drafted around it.** Omitting the advice is a failure to state *"every matter this subdivision requires to be stated"*, so it is reviewable under subsection M and reportable under subsection J. The cost is that **a review channel now exists for the omission of a sentence that creates no right** — while the member lives it returns the only remedy that matters, which is time to re-designate before payment completes, and after she dies it adds nothing because no remedy can re-designate for her. **The Director has said that if the counsel pass thinks the channel is worth more argument than the advice is worth, they will re-take that one point; it is one clause either way, and the clause is this item.** **Correction, store revision 68 — this item sent that question to the wrong card.** As written at store revision 65 it named the counsel gate at [MOO-192](/MOO/issues/MOO-192), which is the subsection E1 gate and has the channel out of scope. **The question went to pass 21 at [MOO-195](/MOO/issues/MOO-195)**, which answered it: **keep the subsection J limb and drop the subsection M limb**, because M review's only possible output is a remand that can give the claimant nothing — § 2.2-4029 bars a court from supplying the agency action — while the J report puts the same fact before the money committees at no litigation cost and with no claimant needed. **That recommendation is enacted at store revision 70, in form D3c conformed to F9's wording.** The Policy Director has taken [MOO-195](/MOO/issues/MOO-195) F3 at [MOO-208](/MOO/issues/MOO-208) and has expressly reversed the acceptance at MOO-188 §3.4, on counsel's remedy analysis — § 2.2-4029 bars a court from supplying the agency action — and on two further reasons of the Director's own. **So this item's heading is no longer the law of the draft**: omission of the advice is now excepted from D 1's deeming of a failure to state for the purposes of subsection M, and is kept in the subsection J report. **What the exception does not do, recorded and not drafted around: subdivision M 2 reaches the omission on its own terms, so the channel stays open and no cure inside D 1 can close it.** §6.0-70 item 5 and **§7 item 135**, drafted and not enacted. [MOO-195](/MOO/issues/MOO-195) F3 and F10.
7. **What I did not draft, and it is not silence.** The advisory tells the member to look; it does **not** give her a way to re-designate inside the act, does not require the Board to send her her current designation with the certification, and does not toll anything while she reviews. § 51.1-162 A already lets her designate *"in a manner prescribed by the Board"* at any time, so the act needs no machinery, and **a notice that enclosed her designation of record would be a new duty with an administrative cost that nobody has agreed.** It is the kind of thing that belongs on MOO-97's card if anyone wants it, not in a cut commissioned as one edit. **Confirmed, store revision 70.** The Policy Director has **held** [MOO-195](/MOO/issues/MOO-195) F5 at [MOO-208](/MOO/issues/MOO-208) and has directed that **the acknowledgment form is not to be drafted**, because it moves the advisory toward a member obligation and the advisory's whole value is that it demands nothing of her. The route question goes to the subsection J report instead: if the pilot's own reports show the advice being buried, that is the evidence on which to require a route, and it will be evidence rather than a guess. §6.0-70 item 7.

### §6.0-63 Ambiguities resolved in store revision 63

1. **Why *"the person who filed the request"* and not *"the member."*** M 2's class of persons who may seek reconsideration is seven categories wide — member, retiree, beneficiary, applicant, personal representative of a deceased member's estate, person who claimed a death benefit and was refused, and employer that paid on a member's behalf. A notice right keyed to *"the member"* would have given the estate, the refused death-benefit claimant and the paying employer a reconsideration they could not convert into a decision. The enacted words follow the filing, which is the only fact common to all seven.
2. **Why the denial is made a case decision in terms rather than by relying on the completion sentence.** §0.0-63 repair 1. The alternative was to amend the completion sentence to add *"or upon a denial under this subsection"*, which touches an enacted sentence three passes have certified; the insertion touches nothing.
3. **Why *"first day after the expiration of those 30 days"* and not *"the last day."*** §0.0-63 repair 2: a denial on the last day of a period in which the Board may still lawfully act puts two inconsistent determinations on one date.
4. **Why the 30 days are not tolled, extended or qualified.** The member's 30-day notice period under the new sentence runs from her own filing, so there is nothing for the Board to delay. Subsection M 2's tolling operates on *"any period within which the member or applicant must act"* — and the new period is one in which the **Board** must act, so the tolling provision does not reach it and no carve-out is needed.
5. **Why the denial is not deemed an act of the Board.** §0.0-63, last paragraph, and §7 item 130. Deeming it an act would have ended the member's tolling and her D 4 / D 5 filing-date retention as a by-product — two endpoints that belong to [MOO-164](/MOO/issues/MOO-164) and card (k) and that nobody has decided. **A cure for a residual must not move a priced endpoint in passing.**
6. **What the board's answer costs, stated and not netted away.** Up to 30 further days before the member may go to court, and a member who never files the notice never gets a decision. That is the same bargain § 2.2-4021 B strikes against every party before every agency in the Commonwealth, which is why option (d) was modelled on it — but it is a cost, and it falls on the member who does not know she has a right to assert. The board was given that trade in terms on the card and took it.


7. **A defect in these two sentences that I found after they were in the store, and it is mine. §7 item 131.** Item 2 above says the denial is made a case decision *"in terms rather than by relying on the completion sentence"*, which was the right repair. It is not a complete one. Subdivision M 2's express Article 5 grant is keyed to *"such case decision"*, whose nearest antecedent is the completion sentence it was written to avoid depending on; and the characterisation I drafted is bounded *"for the purposes of this subsection"*, which is the one qualification that does not travel to § 2.2-4026. **Neither reading is fatal alone and the pair is.** Both repairs are one phrase, both are drafted in full at §7 item 131, and **neither is enacted** — the limiting phrase is in the option (d) text the board approved verbatim, and the alternative repair edits a sentence [MOO-169](/MOO/issues/MOO-169)'s pass certified. **No figure moves on either.** Named to [MOO-190](/MOO/issues/MOO-190) as the first thing to break.

### §6.0-52 Ambiguities resolved in store revision 52 — performed; moved verbatim to the retained-record document in store revision 55

## 7. Open questions for Policy Director — moved to a separate document

**The item list is no longer in this document.** On the Policy Director's answer to item 112 of 12 October 2026, store revision 59 moved §7's whole item list — the preamble, every numbered item 1 to 125, and the §7.1 retained-record stub — **verbatim** to [`bill-text-s8-item-list`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-item-list). A reference of the form **§7 item n** resolves there.

**Why.** The list was 306,242 characters of this document's 510,963 — nearly three characters of unresolved internal question for every one character of bill. §1 is about 112,000. Counsel's pass on store revision 56 built the opposition's floor speech out of the list, and that was the Director's reason. The split is a **document** boundary, not a containment control: both documents are internal Paperclip issue documents on [MOO-42](/MOO/issues/MOO-42), and what it buys is that releasing the bill text no longer carries the item list by accident.

**Store revision 60 writes into that document too.** The Policy Director's eight answers of 12 October 2026 close **items 114, 115, 116, 117, 118, 119, 103 and 104** and the selected limbs of **items 120 and 121**, and open **items 126 and 127**; every closure and both new items are written there, with this document carrying the narrative at §0.0-60, the sweep at §5.39 and the drafter's note at §6.0-60.

**The seven items answered on 12 October 2026 and enacted or declined in store revision 59** — 88, 108, 109, 110, 111, 112, 113 — and the three opened by those answers — 123, 124, 125 — all carry their disposition in that document. §0.0-59 of this document is the narrative, §5.38 the sweep, §6.0-59 the ambiguities.

**Store revision 61 writes into that document too.** [MOO-161](/MOO/issues/MOO-161)'s counsel pass on store revision 58's eight substitutions returned **1 fatal, 9 serious, 6 moderate and 4 minor findings, and 12 null results**. Eight of its findings are enacted in §1 by this revision, two are declined with reasons at §6.0-61, and **seven are drafted in full and enacted nowhere** — they are **§7 item 128**, written in that document. The same revision enacts **five of the nine answers at §7 item 122**, which the Policy Director selected on 12 October 2026; item 122 is marked there with what was enacted and what stays open. This document carries the narrative at §0.0-61, the sweep at §5.40 and the drafter's note at §6.0-61.

**Store revision 62 writes into that document too.** [MOO-169](/MOO/issues/MOO-169)'s counsel pass on store revision 61's thirteen substitutions returned **1 fatal, 9 serious, 9 moderate and 4 minor findings, and 12 null results**. **Fourteen of its findings are enacted in §1 by this revision** — F1, F2, F3, F4, F5 in part, F6, F8, F9, F10, M1, M2, M3, M4, M5, M6, m1 and m3 — **six are drafted or left undrafted with reasons** at §6.0-62, and the same revision enacts **all seven of the answers the Policy Director selected at §7 item 128 on 12 October 2026**. Item 128 is marked there with what each answer became, and **§7 item 129** carries the seven questions this revision could not answer by drafting choice. This document carries the narrative at §0.0-62, the sweep at §5.41 and the drafter's note at §6.0-62.

**Store revision 65 writes into that document too.** It enacts **§7 item 129 answer (4)** — F10's designation-review advisory — which the Policy Director answered at [MOO-188](/MOO/issues/MOO-188) §3.3 with one wording change, and item 129 is marked there with what that answer became. **Answers (1), (2), (3), (5), (6) and (7) stay open**, four of them decide who bears a loss, and this revision answers none of them. This document carries the narrative at §0.0-65, the sweep at §5.43 and the drafter's note at §6.0-65.
